# Architecture decisions — Lambruk Pantry on Horizon

A decision record for how `design/DESIGN-TOKENS.md` maps onto this Horizon build. Written after confirming Horizon uses one global colour **palette** (not per-section colour **schemes** — verified: no `"type": "color_scheme"` setting exists anywhere in `sections/`, `blocks/`, or `config/settings_schema.json`) and after reading `config/settings_schema.json`, `assets/base.css`, and the `snippets/` that actually emit Horizon's CSS custom properties.

This record exists so the reasoning survives past this session — each decision below states what we're doing, why, and what evidence from the theme source drove it. **No theme file has been changed as part of writing this record.** It is the plan; implementation is separate work.

---

## 1. Colour: palette + a single heading override + an explicit muted token

**Decision**
- `page_background_color` → `--paper` (`#FBFAF7`)
- `page_text_color` → `--text-body` (`#4A5478`)
- One CSS rule overrides `h1`–`h6` to `--text-strong` (`#131A3E`)
- `--text-muted` becomes our own explicit custom property (`#686D84`), not Horizon's derived opacity-fade
- `--color-border` gets overridden globally to our gold hairline, with a specific fix for product cards (see below)

**Why global foreground = body colour, not heading colour**
Horizon has exactly one foreground role. `body { color: var(--color-foreground) }` in `color-palette.liquid`, and nothing in `base.css` gives headings an independent colour — they simply inherit. Since body copy vastly outnumbers headings on every page, setting the global foreground to `--text-body` and overriding the minority (headings) is less code than the reverse.

```css
/* new stylesheet, loaded after Horizon's own CSS */
h1, h2, h3, h4, h5, h6 {
  color: var(--text-strong);
}
```

One rule. It rides on top of whatever `page_text_color` is set to without touching `color-palette.liquid`.

**Why `--text-muted` needs to be a real second colour, not Horizon's fade**
Horizon's "muted" text isn't a colour — it's `rgb(var(--color-foreground-rgb) / 0.6)` (`--opacity-muted-text: 0.6` in `theme-styles-variables.liquid`). If foreground is `#4A5478`, a 60%-opacity fade of it is **not guaranteed to equal our approved `#686D84`**, and it's mechanically translucent rather than solid — it'll shift with whatever sits behind it (a photo, a navy band), which our contrast-checked, solid `#686D84` was specifically chosen to avoid. So:

```css
:root {
  --text-muted: #686D84;
}
```
...used directly wherever our own components need muted text, instead of Horizon's `--color-foreground-muted`.

**What `--color-border` inheriting foreground actually does — checked, not assumed**
`--color-border: {{ settings.page_text_color }}` in `color-palette.liquid` — so once foreground is `#4A5478`, `--color-border` becomes that colour too. Grepping every consumer:

- `accordion-styles.liquid`, `header-drawer.liquid`, `buy-buttons-styles.liquid`, `template-giftcard.css`, `search-modal.liquid`, `predictive-search-styles.liquid` all use it **solid** — `border: 1px solid var(--color-border)`. With foreground at `#4A5478`, every one of these hairlines renders solid blue-grey, not our warm gold `#E3D6C5`.
- `divider.liquid` already supports a per-instance override (`settings.divider_color`, falling back to `--color-border`) — useful precedent for point 2 below.
- `product-grid.liquid:225` — the actual **product card border** — reads `rgb(var(--color-border-rgb) / var(--product-card-border-opacity))`. Grepped for `--product-card-border-width` and `--product-card-border-opacity` everywhere else in the theme: **they are defined nowhere.** An undefined custom property with no fallback makes the whole `border` declaration invalid at computed-value time, so **product cards currently render with no border at all** — not "the wrong colour," genuinely absent. This was a surprise; worth knowing before assuming the card border "just needs a colour fix."

**Decision from that finding:** override `--color-border` globally to `#E3D6C5` (fixes every solid-consumer above in one shot), and *separately* define `--product-card-border-width: 1px` and `--product-card-border-opacity: 1` in the new stylesheet to activate the currently-dormant product-card border feature. Two small additions, not a rewrite of `product-grid.liquid`.

**Implemented 2026-08-20.** Went in as one coordinated change — palette values, CSS additions, and the fixes below all ship together, since setting the palette without them breaks hairlines and leaves headings uncoloured.

- `page_background_color` → `--paper` `#FBFAF7`; `page_text_color` → `--text-body` `#4A5478`. Both already referenced `settings.color_palette.background`/`.foreground` in `settings_data.json`, so only `color_palette` itself needed new values.
- `color1`/`color2` weren't scoped by this section originally — traced their real consumers before setting them (see below). `color1` → `#131A3E` (`--text-strong`; its only consumer is `palette_input_text`). `color2` → `#E3D6C5`, matching `--color-border`, for its genuinely decorative consumers (`drawer_border_color`, `popover_border_color`, `product.json` divider ×2).
- **`color2`'s two remaining consumers needed a different value, not the palette's.** `palette_input_border` and `palette_variant_border` are interactive-component boundaries, and `#E3D6C5` measures 1.37:1 on paper — fails WCAG 1.4.11's 3:1 minimum badly (see `DESIGN-TOKENS.md` §1 for the full check across the gold scale). Per instruction, didn't compromise the shared palette to fix this — overrode both settings directly to `#A07037` (`--gold-600`, 4.13:1), independent of `color_palette.color2`. Found and fixed the same problem in two more places outside the palette mechanism entirely: `templates/password.json`'s `input_border_color` (same setting, different template) and `templates/cart.json`'s cart-page order-summary `background_color`, which was reading `color2` as a *background* — checked the design source's `CartDrawer.jsx` directly, which sets the drawer's own background to `var(--paper)`, not gold; `#E3D6C5` there would have been visibly wrong, not just under-contrast. Both corrected to literal values (`#A07037`, `#FBFAF7`) rather than a `color_palette` reference.
- Heading colour: added `h1, h2, h3, h4, h5, h6 { color: var(--text-strong); }` to `lambruk-tokens.css` — **bare tag selector only**, not widened to `.h1`–`.h6` classes as first considered. Reason: Horizon's generic `text` block never renders a real `<hN>` tag regardless of `type_preset` — it's always a `<div>`/`<p>` — so the bare selector only ever catches genuine semantic headings (blog article titles, drawer/modal headings, hidden a11y `<h1>`s), all safe to recolour navy. Every heading actually authored this build so far uses `type_preset: "custom"` (class `custom`, not `h1`–`h6`), so it needed an explicit `text_color: "#131A3E"` on the block instead — done for all five `media-with-content` headings. **Quote-panel's `.h3 text-display-3` div was the one place a class-based widening would have looked right and been wrong**: it already gets correct colour by inheriting a *locally scoped* `--color-foreground` from `contrast-override` (its own `background_color`/`text_color`/`accent_color` settings, independent of the global palette). A `.h3` class rule at `:root` scope would have matched that div directly and beaten the inherited value — repainting the quote navy-on-navy, invisible. Left untouched; confirmed no other component currently combines a Horizon `.h1`–`.h6` utility class with a `contrast-override`-scoped surface, so the bare-tag-only rule has no other gaps to close right now.
- **Footer text colour was a related gap, found while checking this — not in the original plan.** The footer's brand blurb, all three link-list columns, the copyright bar, the policy links, and the social icons had no explicit `text_color`/`icon_color`, so they inherited whatever the global foreground became. Checked contrast of the new `#4A5478` on the footer's navy `#131A3E` background: **~2.2:1**, far under AA. The design source (`Footer.jsx`) uses `--text-on-dark-muted` (`rgba(247,245,242,.72)`) for exactly this content. Set `text_color`/`icon_color` to the hex+alpha equivalent, `#F7F5F2B8`, on all six affected blocks in `sections/footer-group.json`. The three column headings (`#AE7A3C`) were already explicit and needed no change.
- `--color-border-rgb` also needed redefining alongside `--color-border` — `product-grid.liquid`'s card border reads it through `rgb(var(--color-border-rgb) / var(--product-card-border-opacity))`, a separate custom property from `--color-border` itself, set in `color-palette.liquid` from `page_text_color.rgb`. Set to `227, 214, 197` (the `#E3D6C5` channels), matching the existing comma-separated convention `color-palette.liquid` itself uses for `--color-foreground-rgb`/`--color-background-rgb`.

**Verified 2026-08-20 against the live theme (`shopify theme dev`, computed styles read in-browser, not `theme check`).** Confirmed correct: body background/text (`#FBFAF7`/`#4A5478`), `--color-border`/`--color-border-rgb` at `:root` (`#E3D6C5`/`227, 214, 197` — the `layout/theme.liquid` load-order fix above holds), the Cafe-intro heading (`rgb(19, 26, 62)` = `#131A3E`), all six footer text/icon colours (`rgba(247, 245, 242, 0.72)` on a confirmed navy `#131A3E` footer background), `--color-input-border` (`#A07037`), and the cart drawer (paper background, gold `#E3D6C5` border-left).

**One real gap found, not anticipated by the consumer trace above: native product-card borders are navy, not gold.** `sections/main-collection.liquid` calls `contrast-override` with only `background_color` (no `border_color`), so `contrast-override.liquid`'s smart-contrast branch picks `--palette-darkest` for the border instead of reading our global `--color-border` at all — an entirely separate mechanism from the direct consumers traced earlier (accordion, header-drawer, search-modal, etc.), which do read `--color-border` directly and are unaffected. `--palette-darkest` is computed by `color-palette.liquid` as the darkest of the four `color_palette` entries, which is now `color1` (`#131A3E`) — so product cards render a 1px navy border, confirmed via computed style (`rgb(19, 26, 62)`) on `.product-grid__item` on `/collections/all`. Not fixed: forcing gold here means either passing an explicit `border_color` into `main-collection.liquid` (a further core-file edit, needs sign-off) or repointing `--palette-darkest` globally, which would also change the smart-contrast text-colour fallback used elsewhere and risks a different regression. Navy is a legitimate, on-brand hairline on a paper card, just not what §1 specified — flagged for a decision rather than silently left or silently changed.

---

## 2. Surfaces: native `background_color` + `contrast-override`, not a CSS layer

**Decision:** every new theme block that needs a non-default surface (paper-2, navy, etc.) gets its own `background_color` block setting, defaulting to the correct surface hex, following the pattern already used by `blocks/_card.liquid` — not a blanket CSS class system we invent.

**The existing pattern, as found in `blocks/_card.liquid`:**
```liquid
{% liquid
  if block_settings.background_color != blank
    render 'contrast-override', background_color: block_settings.background_color, section_id: block.id
  endif
%}
<div class="card{% if block_settings.background_color != blank %} color-custom-{{ block.id }}{% endif %}">
```
```json
{ "type": "color", "id": "background_color", "label": "t:settings.background_color", "placeholder": "t:settings.default" }
```
`_card.liquid` leaves the setting blank by default (inherits the ambient page background) and only engages `contrast-override` — which auto-picks a readable text colour from the palette extremes — when a merchant explicitly sets one.

**Worked example — one block, before applying this broadly**

Our navy quote panels (`Promise.jsx` / `WhyLambruk.jsx` — `--surface-dark`, `--text-on-dark`, gold eyebrow) are the surface furthest from Horizon's light default, so they're the clearest test of the pattern. Two things differ deliberately from `_card.liquid`'s convention:

1. **We give the setting a real default**, not a blank placeholder — this block's identity *is* navy; a merchant clearing the field shouldn't silently fall back to white.
2. **We pass explicit `text_color`/`accent_color`, not just a background.** `contrast-override`'s own doc comment says it *skips smart contrast entirely* when `text_color` is provided — meaning Horizon already has a first-class path for "don't guess, use this exact colour," not just auto-contrast. We use it, because "readable" isn't the bar — matching our approved gold-on-navy pairing is.

```json
{
  "type": "color",
  "id": "background_color",
  "label": "t:settings.background_color",
  "default": "#131A3E"
},
{
  "type": "color",
  "id": "text_color",
  "label": "t:settings.text_color",
  "default": "#F7F5F2"
},
{
  "type": "color",
  "id": "accent_color",
  "label": "t:settings.accent_color",
  "default": "#C6A06C"
}
```

```liquid
{% liquid
  render 'contrast-override',
    background_color: block_settings.background_color,
    text_color: block_settings.text_color,
    border_color: block_settings.accent_color,
    section_id: block.id
%}
<div class="quote-panel color-custom-{{ block.id }}">
  <p class="quote-panel__eyebrow" style="color: {{ block_settings.accent_color }}">{{ block_settings.eyebrow }}</p>
  <p class="quote-panel__text">{{ block_settings.text }}</p>
</div>
```

A merchant gets a real colour picker per instance (on-brand default, override allowed), `contrast-override` still emits the standard `--color-foreground`/`--color-border` variables scoped to `.color-custom-{{ block.id }}` for any child markup that doesn't set its own colour explicitly, and none of it required a bespoke CSS class system. This is the pattern for `paper-2` band sections, the alt-paper category tiles, and any other non-default surface — each gets its own `background_color` (+ `text_color`/`accent_color` where the surface isn't just light-on-light) with our surface's hex as the default.

Once this one example is confirmed to look right in the editor, apply the same shape to the other surfaces before building further sections against it.

**Bug fixed 2026-08-20 — quote-panel rendered narrow with paper gaps either side, on a `section_width: "full-width"` custom section.** Two separate causes stacked:
1. `templates/index.json`'s `section_why_lambruk` wrapper was still `"section_width": "page-width"` — a plain oversight, corrected to `"full-width"`.
2. That alone wasn't enough. `quote-panel.liquid`'s outer div carried both the `color-custom-{{ block.id }}` background *and* `max-width: 46rem; margin-inline: auto` — so the navy paint only ever covered the readable-text column, not the section. Fixed by splitting into two elements: the outer div keeps the background/padding and now has no width constraint of its own; a new inner `.quote-panel__content` div carries the `max-width`/centering, wrapping just the eyebrow/quote/illustration.
3. Splitting the div wasn't enough either — verified live and the background was still shrinking to ~816px on a 1280px viewport. Root cause: `section.liquid`'s flex wrapper (`layout-panel-flex--column`) uses `align-items: flex-start` when a section's `horizontal_alignment_flex_direction_column` setting is unset (as ours was), not `stretch` — a block without its own `width: fill` mechanism shrinks to its content's natural width instead of spanning the container. `quote-panel.liquid`'s schema has no `width` setting at all (unlike `group`, which does), so there was nothing to opt into fill with. Fixed by adding `width: 100%` directly to the outer div's inline style — a deliberate, block-specific choice, not a general pattern: this block is always meant as a full-bleed colour band, so hardcoding full width is correct here in a way it wouldn't be for a block meant to size to content.

Verified live at each step, not assumed from the diff — each of the three causes looked like "the" fix until measured, and stacking them was the only way to confirm nothing else was still constraining it. Worth checking any other full-width custom section that uses a block without its own `width` setting for the same shrink-to-content problem.

---

## 3. Accept Horizon's values where close

Three deliberate concessions, recorded with reasoning in `design/DESIGN-TOKENS.md` at their respective sections (§2, §8, §9) as well as here:

| Ours | Horizon's | Gap | Accepted because |
|---|---|---|---|
| 720px breakpoint | **750px** (`base.css`, hardcoded) | 30px | Imperceptible in practice; matching it would mean editing Horizon's own CSS for zero visible gain. |
| 20px mobile gutter | **16px** (`--page-margin` below 750px, hardcoded) | 4px | Below the threshold most people notice in a side margin; it's the one mobile spacing value Horizon ships for free — overriding it means touching `base.css` directly for a 4px difference. |
| Display 5 · Card at 26px | **24px** (`type_size_h5` nearest fixed option) | 2px | Not visually distinguishable on a card-caption-scale heading; every step expressible as a plain setting is one less custom rule to carry across future Horizon updates. |

The common thread: each gap is small enough to be invisible or near-invisible, and closing it would require editing a Horizon-owned file (`base.css`) rather than adding to ours. Per the customisation hierarchy in `CLAUDE.md`, that's exactly the trade the hierarchy asks us to make — settings and CSS additions first, Horizon's own files last resort, and these three don't clear the bar for "last resort."

**Not a template for every gap.** The 200px difference between our `--container` (1240px) and Horizon's nearest `page_width` option (`narrow` = 1440px) is *not* being accepted the same way — see §5.

---

## 4. Motion: our own tokens, scoped to our own components — Horizon's spring system untouched

**Decision:** do not override `--hover-transition-duration`, `--ease-out-quad`, `--ease-out-cubic`, or any `--spring-d*-b0-duration` variable. Define our own timing tokens and apply them only inside CSS we write for our own blocks/sections.

```css
:root {
  --lambruk-dur-fast: 140ms;
  --lambruk-dur-base: 240ms;
  --lambruk-dur-slow: 420ms;
  --lambruk-ease-out: cubic-bezier(.22, .61, .36, 1);
}

/* used only within our own component selectors, e.g.: */
.quote-panel__eyebrow { transition: color var(--lambruk-dur-fast) var(--lambruk-ease-out); }
```

**Why not just override Horizon's variables globally:** Horizon's animation system is partly spring-physics-based — `--spring-d220-b0-duration` etc. name a duration *and* a bounce coefficient as a matched pair, tuned for specific motion curves. Swapping the duration alone (via a blanket variable override) would desync it from the bounce it was tuned against, producing motion that's neither our calm/editorial curve nor Horizon's intended spring — worse than either. Our components get our exact motion; Horizon's own interactions (drawers, quick-add, page transitions) keep behaving as Horizon intended.

---

## 5. Everything else: one new stylesheet, layered over Horizon — never edits to Horizon's files

Every remaining Category 3 item from the settings-mapping review becomes a CSS custom property in a **single new asset** (`assets/lambruk-tokens.css`), loaded after Horizon's own CSS. One file, with clearly marked TOKENS and UTILITIES sections rather than splitting custom properties and classes across two assets, to avoid a second global request. Nothing here edits `base.css` or any other Horizon-owned snippet other than the two lines noted in "Upgrade-tracked modifications" below.

**Correction, 2026-08-20 — the stylesheet had to move from `snippets/stylesheets.liquid` to `layout/theme.liquid`.** The original plan (loading it there, right after `base.css`) works for tokens Horizon never touches again, but not for `--color-border`: `color-palette.liquid` sets that same custom property from `page_text_color`, and it's rendered *after* `stylesheets` in `layout/theme.liquid` (`{%- render 'stylesheets' -%}` then, four lines later, `{%- render 'color-palette' -%}`). Same specificity (`:root` in both), later source wins — our override would have been silently overwritten back to whatever `page_text_color` resolves to, with no error. Moved the `stylesheet_tag` line itself to `layout/theme.liquid`, directly after `{%- render 'color-palette' -%}`, so it loads last and actually wins. This does not affect scoped surfaces like quote-panel's `contrast-override` — those set `--color-border` on a class selector rendered even later, deep in `<body>`, which still naturally wins over both.

Confirmed contents so far:

- `--text-muted: #686D84` (§1)
- `--color-border` override to `#E3D6C5`, plus `--product-card-border-width`/`--product-card-border-opacity` to activate the dormant card border (§1)
- `--lambruk-dur-fast/base/slow`, `--lambruk-ease-out` (§4)
- `--section-y: 96px`, `--measure-prose: 820px` — no Horizon setting exists for either; theme-level, not per-block, since Horizon's own vertical rhythm is controlled per-section rather than globally
- `--container: 1240px` — Horizon's `page_width` options (1440/1920/2400px) are too far from our value to accept as a concession the way §3's gaps were; this needs an explicit override on our section wrappers (`max-width` set directly, not relying on `page-width-*` classes) rather than picking the nearest native option
- `--shadow-whisper`, `--shadow-card`, `--shadow-lift` — no product/content-card shadow setting exists in Horizon at all (only `card_hover_effect`, a transform, and drawer/popover shadows elsewhere); these become pure CSS, applied via our own card component styles
- `--icon-accent: #8A6A32` (recorded in the now-archived `design/_archive/Lambruk Pantry Design System/tokens/colors.css` and reconfirmed against `design/design_handoff_website/` on 2026-08-20 — needs the equivalent added to the theme's own new stylesheet when icons are built)
- Hero/card image-protection scrims (the two `linear-gradient()`s from `DESIGN-TOKENS.md` §1) — no native equivalent, straight CSS

**Still open, deliberately not decided here:**
- Custom font upload for Instrument Serif and Geist — `font_picker` settings support uploading a custom font through the theme editor, which would be the native path, but that's an Admin action, not something resolvable from source. The font files are staged locally (`~/Downloads/Geist/`) and ready whenever that step happens; Instrument Serif isn't yet confirmed staged the same way.
- Focus-ring colour — no dedicated setting found beyond the button-specific `--color-primary-button-focus-outline`; needs a decision once we're building actual interactive components, not before.
- [x] **Quote-panel illustration placement — closed for both "The Lambruk Promise" and "Why Lambruk," 2026-08-27.** `design/design_handoff_website/` adds `&nbsp;` directly before each inline illustration in the quote text ("Made with real fruit&nbsp;<img>...") — confirmed on both the Promise and Why Lambruk bands, desktop and mobile — meaning the illustration sits *inside* the sentence, glued to the preceding word, not trailing the whole quote as a separate element the way the block originally rendered it.

  **Mechanism**: `blocks/quote-panel.liquid` gained a second, additive rendering mode. `quote_part_1`-`_4` (richtext) plus `illustration_1`-`_3` (image_picker) render up to three inline images interleaved with up to four text segments, chosen via `{% if quote_part_1 != blank %}`; leaving `quote_part_1` blank keeps every other instance on the original single-`quote`-plus-one-trailing-`illustration` path, byte-for-byte unchanged. Segments are `richtext`, not plain `text`, specifically so Why Lambruk's `<em>less sugar</em>` will work once it's populated. Populated for **Promise only** this pass (`templates/index.json`'s `quote_panel_lambruk_promise`) — Why Lambruk's own two illustrations (a preserve jar, a peach) aren't identified yet, so its settings stay blank and it keeps rendering exactly as before. One known limitation, disclosed rather than silently generalized: the segmented template hardcodes its forced `<br>` between part 2/illustration 2 and part 3/illustration 3, matching Promise's own shape; Why Lambruk's break falls in a different place (after illustration 1, not 2), so wiring it up will need a small, one-line template adjustment, not a rebuild — the schema's field *count* already accommodates it, just not the exact break position.

  **Two real bugs found during verification, both pre-existing, not introduced by this change:**
  1. **Font-size was silently pinned to 32px on every viewport, including desktop — affecting the plain `quote` path too, not just the new segmented one.** The block's quote text carries both `.h3` (Horizon's own utility, `assets/base.css`) and `.text-display-3` (this project's fluid-clamp token). Horizon's own rule targets `.h3.h3` — two class selectors, specificity (0,2,0) — while `.text-display-3` is a single class, (0,1,0). Horizon's flat `--font-h3--size` (`2rem` = 32px, no viewport interpolation) was winning regardless of the intended fluid `--display-3-size` clamp(32px, …, 40px), on specificity alone, at every viewport including desktop. Confirmed live before the fix: computed `font-size: 32px` at a 1280px viewport, where the clamp should resolve to 40px. Font-size itself was *not* changed from the already-reviewed `--display-3-size` token (`DESIGN-TOKENS.md` §2 already documents 44/30 raw values deliberately absorbed into this step) — only line-height is overridden per-instance in `quote-panel.liquid` now, since Display 3's own 1.08 line-height is right for a short heading but wrong for this quote's wrapping, multi-line sentence, which the source gives 1.45 desktop / 1.5 mobile instead. Where the specificity fix itself lives moved during the follow-up below — see that entry for the current state.
  2. **Inline illustrations had no `vertical-align`, defaulting to `baseline`** — sat low relative to the surrounding text instead of visually centred on it. Design specifies `vertical-align: middle` explicitly on all three images, both breakpoints. Added to the shared `.quote-panel__illustration` rule (harmless for the legacy trailing-illustration usage, which has no other inline content on its own line to align against).

  **Verified live** on 182395437357, desktop and mobile, via computed styles: font-size 40px desktop / 32px mobile (32 vs. the source's raw 30 is the same already-approved Display 3 consolidation, not a new gap); line-height 58px desktop (40 × 1.45) / 48px mobile (32 × 1.5) — both exact; icon height 76px desktop / 52px mobile; `vertical-align: middle` on all three; margins 14px/14px (illustration 1), 14px/6px (illustrations 2 and 3) desktop, uniform 6px all three mobile; container 1000px; no comma after "fruit," no trailing full stop; centred text with the two-line break landing exactly where the source's `<br>` puts it. Screenshotted both breakpoints against the design side by side — visually matching.

  **Follow-up, same day — checked whether the `.hN.hN` collision is general, then moved the fix to the token layer.** Asked (and answered with live computed styles, not just reasoning from the CSS) whether the same collision hits `.text-display-1/2/4/5` or any `.text-1/2/3` utility anywhere they're combined with a Horizon `.hN` class. It doesn't, today: `grep`ing the whole theme found `.text-display-1/2/5` and `.text-1/2/3` don't exist as CSS classes anywhere — every other use of those scale steps is a direct inline `style="font-size: var(--display-N-size)"` on the individual component, which is immune to this collision regardless (inline styles beat any class-based specificity trick). `.text-display-3` was the only class with a live collision, in exactly the two lines just fixed above. `.text-display-4` was defined in `assets/lambruk-tokens.css` but, per the same audit, applied as a class nowhere in the theme — its own `--display-4-size`/`--display-4-line-height` tokens are very much alive (8 live consumers: `templates/index.json` ×3, `templates/product.json` ×4, `templates/page.cafe.json`, plus two of this session's own scoped success-state stylesheets), every one of them via the same inline-style pattern, never via the class.

  The underlying *mechanism*, though, is general: `assets/base.css` defines all six Horizon heading rules the same way, `.h1.h1` through `.h6.h6`, each pairing two class selectors with a flat non-fluid `--font-hN--size`. Any single-class utility combined with any `.hN` would lose the same way — this isn't a quote-panel quirk, it's a property of how Horizon's own heading utilities are written.

  **Decision: deleted `.text-display-4` rather than hardening it.** Asked to keep or delete a defined-but-unused class that would otherwise need the same specificity workaround as `.text-display-3` — an unused utility that requires a non-obvious trick to use safely is exactly the trap being closed, not one to leave lying around for the next person to inherit. Given every real consumer of the underlying token already uses the inline-style pattern, and none use the class, deleting it costs nothing today and removes one more place the same silent failure could recur later. `.text-display-3` stays — it has a real, live consumer with no inline-style equivalent (the quote text is a `<div>` spanning multiple interleaved segments and images, not a single settings-driven value) — and its own rule in `assets/lambruk-tokens.css` is now the doubled-class `.text-display-3.text-display-3`, with a comment explaining why the doubling exists so it doesn't read as a mistake later. `blocks/quote-panel.liquid`'s own local `.quote-panel__quote.quote-panel__quote` font-size/line-height override (added in the fix above) was removed as redundant now that the token itself carries the fix — confirmed live afterward that font-size/line-height are unchanged (40/58px desktop, 32/48px mobile) with the local override gone, so the token-layer fix alone is sufficient.

  Filenames on upload matched exactly what was expected — `fruit-bowl-gold.png`, `australia-gold.png`, `seedling-gold.png`, no renaming — confirmed via the resolved CDN URLs in the rendered `<img src>` values, not assumed.

  **Second follow-up, same day — Why Lambruk populated, closing this entry fully.** The jar turned out to already be uploaded: the instance's existing legacy `illustration` setting (`shopify://shop_images/Lambruk-Illustration-9.png`) was fetched and confirmed to be the gold jar, pixel-identical to the design package's own `preserve-jar-gold.png` — moved to `illustration_1` rather than re-uploaded. The peach had no equivalent anywhere in Shopify Files; uploaded from `design/design_handoff_website/assets/illustrations/peach-gold.png`, filename matched exactly on upload (confirmed via the resolved CDN URL), same as the three Promise assets.

  **The `<br>`-position limitation flagged when Promise shipped turned out to be two separate things, only one of which was real.** The worry was that Why Lambruk's break falls after illustration 1 rather than illustration 2, and the segmented template hardcodes the break at the illustration-2 position. That part resolves for free: the schema already skips any blank segment/illustration, so mapping jar → `illustration_1`, leaving `quote_part_2`/`illustration_2` blank, and peach → `illustration_3` reproduces the source's exact structure — the hardcoded `<br>` lands correctly because slot 2 is empty. No template change was needed for the break's *position* in the sentence.

  What did need a real fix: margin. The original fix's `.quote-panel__illustration-1/-2/-3` classes tied margin shape to slot *number* — slot 1 symmetric (14/14), slots 2/3 tight (14px word-side/6px other). That was **a coincidence of Promise's own shape, not the actual rule** — Why Lambruk's icons need the mirror of it (jar tight, peach symmetric), which a naive slot-based mapping would have gotten backwards for both. Checked against all five known icons (Promise's three, Why Lambruk's two) and found the real rule: 14px on any side facing a word, 6px only on a side immediately before a `<br>` or where nothing follows at all. Rewrote the margin CSS around `:has(+ br)` and `:last-child` instead of slot-numbered classes — confirmed live to reproduce Promise's original margins unchanged (14/14, 14/6, 14/6 desktop) before relying on it for Why Lambruk. **Do not reintroduce per-slot margin classes** — the numbering looks tidy but encodes a pattern that only held by accident.

  The other real fix: Why Lambruk's mobile source has no forced break at all (one flowing paragraph, natural wrapping), unlike Promise's mobile source, which breaks at the same relative point as desktop. Added `hide_break_on_mobile` (checkbox, default off) — off by default means Promise's own settings needed no change; Why Lambruk's instance turns it on. Implemented by giving the `<br>` a modifier class and hiding it via `display: none` under a `max-width: 749px` query only when the setting is checked.

  **Verified live** on 182395437357, desktop and mobile. Why Lambruk: font-size 40/32px, line-height 58/48px (1.45×/1.5×, both exact); jar margins 14px/6px, peach margins 14px/14px desktop, both icons uniform 6px mobile; `<br>` present (`display: inline`) on desktop, hidden (`display: none`) on mobile; `<em>less sugar</em>` computed `font-style: italic`; trailing "for a more honest pantry." with its period intact. **Promise re-checked on both breakpoints after the margin refactor** — the regression risk this change actually carried — and confirmed byte-identical to before: 40/58px desktop, 32/48px mobile, 14/14 and 14/6 margins, `<br>` still showing on both breakpoints (Promise's own `hide_break_on_mobile` was never touched, stays off).

---

## 6. Upgrade-tracked modifications

Horizon-owned files we've had to touch directly, rather than layer over. Each entry is a real upgrade burden: a future Horizon theme update can silently overwrite these and drop whatever we depended on, with no error — CSS custom properties that go missing just fall back to unset/initial rather than failing loudly. Check this list after every Horizon update.

| File | Change | Risk | What to check after a Horizon update |
|---|---|---|---|
| `layout/theme.liquid` (edit 1) | Added one line loading `assets/lambruk-tokens.css`, directly after `{%- render 'color-palette' -%}` — moved here from `snippets/stylesheets.liquid` (see §5) specifically so it loads after `color-palette.liquid`'s own `:root` block and can actually win the `--color-border` override | A Horizon update can reorder or remove the `color-palette` render call, silently dropping our line with it — everything depending on `--display-*` tokens, `.text-display-*` utilities, or the gold `--color-border`/heading-colour overrides would lose them with no visible error, just Horizon's own values re-asserting themselves | Confirm the `lambruk-tokens.css` line is still present immediately after `{%- render 'color-palette' -%}`; re-add in the same position if the update overwrote it |
| `layout/theme.liquid` (edit 2) | Added one inline `<script>` (+ comment) as the very first thing in `<head>`, before everything else — suppresses `scroll-behavior: smooth` only when the incoming URL already has a hash, restores it after first paint | A Horizon update that changes `.page-wrapper`'s structure, or moves scroll-behaviour off `html`/`.page-wrapper` entirely, would make this script inert (harmless — it'd just stop doing anything) or, less likely, could interact oddly with a future Horizon script doing something similar in the same window; low risk either way since the script fully self-contains its own state | Confirm the script still runs before any other `<head>` content; re-verify against `assets/base.css`'s current `scroll-behavior` rules if Horizon's own scroll/layout CSS changes |
| `sections/footer-utilities.liquid` | Added two entries to the section's `"blocks"` allowlist — see below | A Horizon update to this file's block list would either silently drop our two entries (if it replaces the array wholesale) or conflict cleanly (if it's a structural merge) — the footer's legal bar would fall back to Horizon's native `footer-copyright`/`footer-policy-list` with no visible error, just the wrong content/shape | Confirm `lambruk-copyright` and `lambruk-policy-links` are still present in the `"blocks"` array; re-add if the update overwrote them |
| `sections/main-collection.liquid` (edit 1) | Added `border_color: settings.color_palette.color2` to the section's `contrast-override` call (§1's product-card-border gap, closed in §19 item 9) | A Horizon update to this call's parameter list is unlikely to break silently (it's additive), but a full rewrite of the section's contrast/colour handling could drop it with no visible error beyond the border reverting to navy | Confirm `border_color: settings.color_palette.color2` is still present on the `contrast-override` call; product cards on Shop All should have a gold (`#E3D6C5`), not navy, border |
| `sections/main-collection.liquid` (edit 2) | Added one `content_for 'block', type: 'lambruk-tag-filter', id: 'lambruk-tag-filter', ...` call, immediately after the existing `filters` call (§20) | This section has no open block slot — both existing block calls are hardcoded, so a Horizon restructure of this file (new block-call shape, different insertion point, a rewrite of the `collection-wrapper` markup) could silently drop this line, deleting the whole pill-filter row with no error, just its absence | Confirm the `lambruk-tag-filter` content_for call is still present right after `filters`; re-add in the same position if the update overwrote it |
| `sections/product-recommendations.liquid` | Added `border_color: settings.color_palette.color2` to the section's `contrast-override` call (found and fixed in passing, §19 item 8 — same bug as `main-collection.liquid` edit 1, same fix) | Same as `main-collection.liquid` edit 1 — additive, low risk, but check after a rewrite of this section's contrast/colour handling | Confirm `border_color: settings.color_palette.color2` is still present; the Product page's "You may also like" cards should have a gold border, not navy |
| `blocks/_product-card.liquid` | Added `{"type": "product-category-label"}` to the section-level `"blocks"` allowlist (§25) — signed off before writing, since this is the one shared file every product card on the site renders through | A Horizon update that replaces this allowlist wholesale would silently drop the entry — the occasion collection template's product cards would lose their category eyebrow with no visible error, just its absence. Low collateral risk otherwise: the block is additive and only actually wired into `templates/collection.occasion.json`'s own card instance, so nothing else renders it even though it becomes editor-addable everywhere | Confirm `product-category-label` is still present in the `"blocks"` array; occasion collection pages should show a category eyebrow (e.g. "SAUCES & CHUTNEYS") above each product title |
| `snippets/product-grid.liquid` | Added `border_color: settings.color_palette.color2` to the snippet's own `contrast-override` call (§30 — a third instance of the exact same missing-`border_color` bug as `main-collection.liquid` edit 1 and `product-recommendations.liquid`, one of the "over 40 Horizon files carry the same latent pattern" flagged but not fixed in §19) | Same as the other two instances — additive, low risk, but check after a rewrite of this snippet's contrast/colour handling | Confirm `border_color: settings.color_palette.color2` is still present on the `contrast-override` call at the top of the file; product grid *items* (the `<li>` wrapper, not just the card inside it — see §30) on any collection or search-results page should have a gold border, not navy |
| `blocks/custom-liquid.liquid` | Added a `class="custom-liquid-block"` to the block's own wrapper `<div>`, plus one new `{% stylesheet %}` block containing `.layout-panel-flex--column > .custom-liquid-block { align-self: stretch; width: 100%; }` — fixes the block's wrapper div having no width styling at all, which collapses toward zero width when it's the sole/dominant child of a column-direction `group` (hit twice independently: the Cafe booking buttons, patched by dropping a forced `flex:1` split, and the Cafe map embed, initially patched with a local `:has()` override before this consolidation). Signed off in conversation before writing, since this is the shared file every custom-liquid block on the site renders through. Rule is scoped to column contexts only — confirmed safe against all 11 existing custom-liquid instances sitewide, including two (`row_hours`/`row_phone` on the homepage Cafe intro) that are direct children of a *row*-direction group sitting next to a fixed-width icon, which the column-scoped selector deliberately does not touch | A Horizon update that changes the block's own wrapper markup (removes the `<div>`, restructures it) would silently drop the `custom-liquid-block` class and this rule would simply stop matching anything — no error, the affected instances (map embed, booking buttons, and any future column-nested custom-liquid content) would quietly collapse back to near-zero width | Confirm `class="custom-liquid-block"` is still on the block's wrapper div and the `{% stylesheet %}` rule is still present; the Cafe page's map embed and "Call to Book"/"Text to Book" buttons should still fill/sit correctly in their columns |

**`layout/theme.liquid` edit 1 went in without the layer-5 sign-off `CLAUDE.md`'s customisation hierarchy calls for; edit 2 (this one) got explicit sign-off first, with conditions, before anything was written** — the correction from that earlier feedback actually held the second time it mattered, not just noted and forgotten.

**`layout/theme.liquid` — pristine vs. current, both edits together.** Confirmed against `git diff b2d7c4b -- layout/theme.liquid`: the working tree's only differences from the pristine Horizon baseline are the two additions below — nothing else in the file has been touched, in either edit.

Pristine (`b2d7c4b`), lines 8, 42–46:
```liquid
  <head>
    {%- if settings.favicon != blank -%}
    ...
    {%- render 'scripts' -%}
    {%- render 'theme-styles-variables' -%}
    {%- render 'color-palette' -%}

    {% if request.design_mode %}
```

Current:
```liquid
  <head>
    <script>
      (function () { ... suppress/restore scroll-behavior around an initial hash ... })();
    </script>

    {%- if settings.favicon != blank -%}
    ...
    {%- render 'scripts' -%}
    {%- render 'theme-styles-variables' -%}
    {%- render 'color-palette' -%}
    {{ 'lambruk-tokens.css' | asset_url | stylesheet_tag }}

    {% if request.design_mode %}
```

**Why edit 1 is here and not `snippets/stylesheets.liquid`, where the stylesheet load was originally placed (§5):** `color-palette.liquid` sets `--color-border` from `page_text_color` in a `:root` block rendered *after* `stylesheets` (`{%- render 'stylesheets' -%}` then, four lines later, `{%- render 'color-palette' -%}`). Same specificity, later source wins — loading our stylesheet from inside `stylesheets.liquid` meant `color-palette.liquid`'s block would always overwrite our `--color-border` override back to blue-grey, silently, with no error. Confirmed live: before the move, `--color-border` computed to the foreground colour on the running theme; after, it computed to `#E3D6C5` as intended. There's no lower-layer way to reorder two `{%- render -%}` calls in `<head>` — this was judged to genuinely need a core-file edit, not a workaround, but that judgement is exactly what should have been raised in conversation first.

**Why edit 2 is here and not somewhere lower in the customisation hierarchy:** confirmed (§13) that Shopify's `{% form %}` tag auto-appends `#{{ form_id }}` to the form action, and that `assets/base.css` sets `scroll-behavior: smooth` unconditionally on `html`, plus again on `.page-wrapper` at desktop widths ≥990px (Horizon's real scroll container there, since `html` gets `overflow: hidden` at that breakpoint). Reliably suppressing the native fragment-scroll's animation means running before the browser attempts it, which happens as soon as the target element exists in the DOM — no block or section, however early in `<body>`, can guarantee winning that race; only `<head>`, before any body content parses, can. `.page-wrapper` itself doesn't exist yet at that point either, so the script uses a self-disconnecting `MutationObserver` to catch it the instant it's added, rather than a fixed delay. Restoration after `load` is skipped entirely when `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` is set, so the fix doesn't fight that preference — worth noting Horizon's own unconditional `scroll-behavior: smooth` on `html` (`assets/base.css` line 29) doesn't itself respect that preference on every code path (a second, `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`-guarded copy exists further down the file and wins via source order for users *without* the preference set, but the top one still applies for users who *do* set it, on every scroll, not just this one). That's a pre-existing Horizon inconsistency, out of scope for this fix, not introduced or worsened by it — flagged here in case it's worth its own fix later.

**`snippets/stylesheets.liquid` is no longer a tracked modification.** The line originally added there (§5's plan) was removed as part of this move; `git diff b2d7c4b -- snippets/stylesheets.liquid` is now empty — the file is byte-for-byte pristine Horizon again, so it's been dropped from this table rather than carrying a stale entry.

**`footer-utilities.liquid`'s `"blocks"` array — pristine vs. current.** This is a closed allowlist of block *types* the section accepts, separate from and in addition to its `max_blocks: 3` count limit (see the QA note in §7 below on why neither surfaced through `theme check`). Neither the type restriction nor a way to add to it exists at a lower layer of the customisation hierarchy — a new block (layer 3) is the correct place for new footer content, but it still has to be named in this array before Horizon's own `footer-utilities.liquid` will accept it, which makes this a minimal, deliberate layer-4 edit rather than a full section override (the array is the only thing touched; the section's grid/layout/rendering logic is untouched).

Pristine (confirmed against `git show b2d7c4b:sections/footer-utilities.liquid` — **not** `8df8a53`, which predates the Horizon theme files entirely; `b2d7c4b` "Add pristine Horizon theme baseline" is the actual baseline commit for diffing):
```json
"blocks": [
  { "type": "footer-copyright" },
  { "type": "footer-policy-list" },
  { "type": "social-links" }
],
```
Current — purely additive, the three original entries untouched, two new ones appended:
```json
"blocks": [
  { "type": "footer-copyright" },
  { "type": "footer-policy-list" },
  { "type": "social-links" },
  { "type": "lambruk-copyright" },
  { "type": "lambruk-policy-links" }
],
```
Both new types are `lambruk-`-prefixed deliberately, so anyone diffing this array against a future Horizon update can immediately see which entries are ours without cross-referencing this table. `footer-copyright` and `footer-policy-list` are left in the allowlist unused — removing them wasn't necessary to fit our content and would have turned this into a mixed add/remove diff instead of a clean, purely-additive one.

**`blocks/custom-liquid.liquid` — pristine vs. current.** Confirmed against `git diff b2d7c4b -- blocks/custom-liquid.liquid`: purely additive, nothing removed.

Pristine (`b2d7c4b`):
```liquid
<div>
  {{ block.settings.custom_liquid }}
</div>

{% schema %}
```
Current:
```liquid
<div class="custom-liquid-block">
  {{ block.settings.custom_liquid }}
</div>

{% stylesheet %}
  .layout-panel-flex--column > .custom-liquid-block {
    align-self: stretch;
    width: 100%;
  }
{% endstylesheet %}

{% schema %}
```
This closes the same gap `assets/base.css` already documents for `group-block`: `.layout-panel-flex--row > .group-block--width-fill { flex: 1; }` exists, but there's no column-direction equivalent, so a `fill`-width block nested in a column relies entirely on its own content to establish width. Native blocks work around this individually (`text` sets a literal `width` CSS property on itself regardless of context; `group` gets the row-only flex rule). `custom-liquid` had nothing — its wrapper `<div>` carries no width styling of any kind — so any block-level content without its own intrinsic width (an aspect-ratio box, a flex row with no wide children) collapsed toward zero the moment it sat in a column context. Confirmed safe against all 11 custom-liquid instances live in the theme at time of writing (three `<style>`-only blocks, one empty anchor div, two row-context icon+label pairs on the homepage Cafe intro, and five column-context blocks including the map embed and booking buttons) before writing this — the row-context pair is the one case where an unscoped fix would have broken something, which is why the rule is direct-child-scoped to `.layout-panel-flex--column` rather than applied unconditionally.

## 7. Verification gap: `theme check` doesn't validate JSON against a section's own schema

Found 2026-08-20 when three edits to `sections/header-group.json`, `sections/footer-group.json`, and `templates/404.json` all passed `shopify theme check` clean, then were rejected outright on real upload to theme `182395437357` (surfaced via the local preview at `127.0.0.1:9292`, then reproduced independently against a second `shopify theme dev` session):

- `footer_utilities_jLGE8U` had 4 blocks against `footer-utilities.liquid`'s declared `"max_blocks": 3`.
- `header-group.json`'s `"order"` array referenced a section id no longer present in `"sections"` — a leftover from a prior edit.
- `templates/404.json` set `padding-block-start`/`padding-block-end` past `main-404.liquid`'s declared range `max: 100`.
- Two more of the same shape, only visible after re-verifying against the live theme rather than trusting a clean sync log: `divider_color`/`background_color_top` used `rgba(…, .28)` / `rgba(…, .92)` — Shopify's colour-setting validator rejects a decimal with no leading zero (`0.28`, not `.28`), even though it's valid CSS a browser parses fine.

**Why `theme check` missed all five.** Checked its rule list (`shopify theme check --list`) — the closest candidates are `ValidSchema`, `ValidSettingsKey`, and `JSONMissingBlock`. What they actually validate: that a section's *own* `{% schema %}` block is syntactically well-formed, that setting IDs referenced in Liquid exist in that schema, and that a JSON template's block *types* are valid. None of them cross-reference **instance data** in a template or section-group JSON file (`sections/header-group.json`, `sections/footer-group.json`, `templates/404.json`, `config/settings_data.json`) against the *runtime constraints* declared in the section/block schema it's instantiating — `max_blocks`, a setting's `min`/`max` range, or platform-level value-format rules like valid CSS colour syntax. `theme check` lints Liquid and JSON *structure*; it doesn't simulate what Shopify's own admin-side schema validator does when a theme is actually synced. That validator is authoritative for this whole error class, and nothing local reproduces it.

**The check that would have caught it: an actual sync.** `shopify theme dev --theme 182395437357` (or `theme push` to the same, unpublished, theme) performs the real server-side validation on every file it uploads — that's literally how these five were found. There is no local, offline equivalent; the checks that matter here only exist on Shopify's side.

**Practice change.** `shopify theme check` remains necessary (it catches things a live sync doesn't — translations, deprecated tags, accessibility) but is no longer sufficient on its own for changes to section-group JSON (`header-group.json`, `footer-group.json`), template JSON, or any settings touching schema-declared ranges/max_blocks/colour values. Those changes need a real sync — `shopify theme dev` running, or a one-off `theme push` — checked for upload errors before considering the change done, not just a clean `theme check` run. Worth folding into the `/qa` skill and `workflow/PIPELINE.md` directly rather than relying on this note being remembered per-session.

---

## 8. `.theme-check.yml`: `MatchingTranslations` disabled deliberately

Lambruk Pantry is English-only. Horizon ships 21 other locale files (`ar`, `de`, `fr`, … — stock translations of Horizon's *default* strings, never authored or maintained by us) alongside `en.default(.schema).json`. `MatchingTranslations` requires every key in the English default files to also exist in all 21 others. Every key we add — including every `lambruk_*` setting from this session's footer work — permanently fails this check across ~21 files with no actual bug behind it: by the time footer work finished, this had inflated `theme check` to 352 errors, all of them this one check, none of them real.

**Considered, not chosen: deleting the 21 unused locale files instead.** Would solve the same noise problem (nothing left to mismatch against) and more thoroughly, but is a real deletion — recoverable via git history since they're tracked from the pristine Horizon baseline, but not a config toggle — and throws away Shopify's own maintained translations of Horizon's *stock* strings as a starting point, if the client ever wants a second storefront language later. The `.theme-check.yml` override is smaller, fully reversible, and the standard idiom for English-only Shopify builds — chosen over the deletion for that reason, not because the tradeoff is one-sided.

**`ValidSchemaTranslations` stays on, deliberately, and must not be disabled alongside this.** It's a different check — it validates that every `t:` reference in a block/section schema resolves to a real key in `en.default.schema.json`, which catches an actual bug class (a typo'd locale key). Nothing about the `MatchingTranslations` override affects it; confirmed separately that it's still active after the override was added. If `theme check` ever needs touching again here, disable only `MatchingTranslations` — don't reach for `theme-check:all`'s translation category as a whole.

---

## 9. Design-system type on generic blocks: `font_size: var(--display-N-size)`

Every section built from Horizon's generic `text` block (the composable pattern §5 and the media-with-content work both lean on) needs headings sized to our canonical scale, not Horizon's own `type_size_h1`–`h6` settings — those are a different, uncoordinated scale (see quote-panel's original h3 problem: flat 32px, no fluid step, because Horizon's own fluid algorithm only activates above a 48px cutoff). Rather than solve this per-section, or build a bespoke block every time a heading needs correct sizing, the fix is one setting value.

**The technique:** on a `text` block, set `type_preset: "custom"` and `font_size: "var(--display-N-size)"` (or any raw CSS value, including a literal `clamp(...)` string) instead of one of the schema's enumerated px options.

**Why this works, confirmed empirically, not just by reading the code — this session already had two cases where static reasoning missed a real server-side validation rule (`max_blocks` type allowlist, the `rgba()` leading-zero requirement), so this was tested against a live sync before relying on it:**
- `font_size` is declared as a `select` in `text.liquid`'s schema, with a fixed list of px options — but that only constrains the theme-editor's dropdown UI. Confirmed the platform's schema validator does **not** reject a value outside that list when set directly in JSON, unlike `range`/`color`/`url` settings (both of which we've hit real rejections on this session).
- `snippets/typography-style.liquid` tries to parse `settings.font_size` as a bare rem number (`split: 'rem' | first | times: 1.0`) to decide whether to build its own clamp. That parse fails silently on a non-numeric string like `"var(--display-3-size)"`, evaluates to `0`, falls under the fluid cutoff, and hits the plain `else` branch — which just echoes the raw setting value into `--font-size:` verbatim. A test block set this way and synced to the live theme measured **40px at desktop, 32px at mobile (375px)** — genuinely fluid, not a coincidence at one viewport.

**The caveat: `line-height` does not get the same free ride, and this is deliberate, not an oversight to fix reflexively.** Unlike `font_size`'s passthrough, `typography-style.liquid` builds `--line-height` by *string-interpolating* your selection into a fixed variable name — `--line-height--{type}-{tight|normal|loose}` — never a raw echo. `type` here is inferred from the same (failed) font-size parse, defaulting to `body`, so a heading set this way gets `--line-height--body-normal` (1.4) rather than Display 3's actual 1.08. Visually looser than intended on any heading that wraps to multiple lines; a non-issue on ones that don't. Left unsolved deliberately on the five `media-with-content` headings (2026-08-20) — pending a look at the real preview, since which headings wrap and which don't determines whether this is even worth solving, not something to fix pre-emptively.

**Adopt this as the standard.** Any generic `text` block needing correct heading size going forward should use `font_size: var(--display-N-size)` rather than Horizon's native presets or a fixed px value — solved once, here, rather than re-decided per section.

---

## 10. Icon + heading + copy trio: three instances, one composition, three settings profiles

The design has three distinct icon+heading+copy 3-up rows — Product page trust row ("Sustainably sourced" / "Freshness secured" / "Low sugar"), Wholesale page "Wholesale Benefits" (undocumented in `DESIGN-TOKENS.md` before this), and Our Story "How we make things" — each with a different icon treatment (badge, bare-icon card, ink illustration) and different mobile behaviour. No native Horizon icon-list/repeater exists (only single-icon `icon.liquid`, no heading/copy fields), so all three are built the same way as the existing Wholesale checklist and Cafe-intro icon row: `group` (row) → 3× `group` (column) → `icon` block + heading `text` block + body `text` block. Same structure, different settings per instance — not a new block.

**Correction found live, 2026-08-20 — `type_preset: "h5"` is not Display 5 · Card scale.** The build initially used `"type_preset": "h5"` for all three trios' headings, reasoning from `DESIGN-TOKENS.md` §2's approved concession ("Display 5 · Card moves to Horizon's native 24px"). Verified on the live theme and got **14px**, not 24px — `type_size_h5` is currently `"14"` in `settings_data.json`; H5/H6 serve Horizon's "Subheading" role (small UI labels), a different scale than the design's Display 5 · Card step, and the concession's own reasoning was never actually wired to a live setting. Corrected to `"type_preset": "h4"` — `type_size_h4` is `"24"`, fixed on every viewport, `type_font_h4` maps to the `heading` role (Instrument Serif), which *does* land on the intended 24px concession value. Re-verified live: 24px, Instrument Serif 400, navy. Worth revisiting if any other build reaches for `h5`/`h6` expecting Display-scale sizing — it won't get it.

**Custom uploaded assets, not Horizon icons, for two of the three trios.** The Product-row icons (`icon-sustainably-sourced-leaf.svg`, `icon-low-sugar-grape.svg`, `icon-freshness-secured-sparkles.svg`) and the Story-trio illustrations (`Lambruk-Illustration.png` seedling, `-4.png` peach, `-8.png` jar) are client-supplied, matched sets — all seven filenames verified live via `shopify://shop_images/` before use (real CDN URL with a version hash, not Horizon's placeholder), same discipline as the earlier logo/illustration population. The trust-row SVGs already bake in their own rounded badge background at 64×64 — no separate badge wrapper needed, simpler than first proposed. Wholesale Benefits uses native Horizon icons instead (`leaf`, `box`, `serving_dish` — no exact match exists for a house/roof glyph, `serving_dish` is the nearest semantic fit for "Formats for every service").

**Icon colour fixed to `--text-accent` (`#986A34`), not the raw design value `#BF8C45`.** `#BF8C45` measures ~2.98:1 on white — just under WCAG 1.4.11's 3:1 minimum for graphics. `--text-accent` (already resolved and defined, see §1) clears it comfortably. The pre-existing Wholesale checklist (`media_with_content_traceable`, built earlier this session) had the same raw `#BF8C45` on its check-box icons and the identical problem — flagged here as out of scope at the time, then fixed the same session once asked (all 7 icons across both sections now `#986A34`, verified live). **Correction, 2026-08-20 — this paragraph said "not fixed" after it no longer was; the fix landed later the same session and this note was never updated.** Caught while auditing the Wholesale page for missing sections — worth remembering that a "flagged, not fixed" note needs striking the moment the fix actually lands, not left to go stale.

**Two documented concessions, not fixed:**
- **Icons stay visible on mobile everywhere**, rather than being dropped as the Product-row and Story-trio designs specify. Neither `icon` nor `group` blocks expose a visibility or custom-CSS-class setting, so hiding a specific block on mobile isn't reachable below a section override (layer 4). A visible icon at mobile width is a minor deviation, not a functional problem.
- **Story-trio items have no border**, unlike the design's `border-top` divider. `group`'s `border` setting is all-sides only, no per-side control — an all-sides border would look wrong, and the design's own mobile view drops the divider anyway, so no border was judged the closer match.

**Verified live, 2026-08-26** — the Our Story instance (`templates/page.our-story.json`'s `how_we_make_things` section), via `?view=our-story` (the same alternate-template mechanism already used for the other deferred pages; the real `our-story` template assignment itself is still blocked, see the Go-live checklist). All three icons (`Lambruk-Illustration.png`/`-4`/`-8` — seedling/peach/jar) resolve to their real CDN URLs and render correctly against their matching headings, first confirmation since this section was built. Previously verified only by pattern-matching against the Product and Wholesale instances; this closes that gap.

## 11. Flat navy CTA band: a second pattern, not quote-panel with fewer parts

The design has two distinct navy-band patterns that shouldn't be conflated. `quote-panel.liquid` (eyebrow + long serif statement + optional inline illustration, no button — a genuine custom block, since nothing native offers that combination) is one. The other — heading + button(s), no quote, no illustration — is flatter and structurally simpler, and has no button setting to reach for on `quote-panel` at all.

**Two unique copy instances, three renderings, both genuinely `#131A3E`-flat, confirmed against both full-site mockups:**
- **Shop page** — eyebrow ("Not sure what to pick") + heading + one button ("Shop Gifts & Samples"), two-column (text left, button right) on desktop. **Desktop only in the source** — checked the mobile mockup's Shop page in full, it ends at the product grid with no navy CTA at all. Judged an omission, not a deliberate mobile absence (majority of this store's traffic is mobile — see `CLAUDE.md`), so built with the same stacked mobile treatment as the Story instance below, not dropped.
- **Story page** — no eyebrow, heading + two buttons ("Shop Now", "Become a Stockist"), two-column desktop → stacked full-width column on mobile in the source.

**Structure — native `section` + `group` + `button` blocks, no new Liquid, same shape as §10's trio:**
```
section (type: section, background_color: #131A3E, content_direction: row → stacks on mobile)
  [eyebrow text block] (Shop page only)
  heading text block        — type_preset "custom", font_size var(--display-3-size), explicit text_color #F7F5F2
  group (row → stacks on mobile) or single button
    button block(s)         — style_class "button-custom", custom_button_background/text/border
```
`sections/section.liquid` ships a `rich_text_section` preset that's already this shape (heading + body + button) — used as the starting point, not built from scratch.

**Button colours, read directly from `Button.jsx`'s `variants` object, not approximated:**
- `onDark` (used by "Shop Now" and "Shop Gifts & Samples"): background `var(--gold-50)` `#F7F5F2`, text `var(--brand)` `#131A3E`, border `#F7F5F2`.
- "Become a Stockist"'s explicit override (desktop in the source, applied to mobile too — see `DESIGN-TOKENS.md` "Flagged inconsistencies" #8 for why): background `#FBFAF7` (paper, not gold-50 — a deliberate distinct value from `onDark`, preserved as such), text `#131A3E`, border `#FBFAF7`.

**Mobile stacking:** both instances use `vertical_on_mobile: true` on the section and the button group, plus `width_mobile: "custom", custom_width_mobile: 100` on every button — matches the source's `full-width="{{ true }}"` buttons on the Story page's mobile view, extended to the Shop CTA per the decision above.

**New template, not an edit to a Horizon file — but the same upgrade risk in a different shape.** The Shop CTA needed scoping to the "All products" collection only (see conversation — the design's own IA treats "Shop" as one filtered catalog page, not one per category, so putting this on the shared `templates/collection.json` would repeat it on Tea/Condiments/Pantry too). Since Shopify's collection alternate-template mechanism doesn't bind by handle — it's a manually-assigned Admin dropdown option, same as the three page templates — the only way to scope it is a new file: `templates/collection.all.json`, a full duplicate of `templates/collection.json`'s existing title/filters/product-grid structure with the CTA section appended. **This is not a Horizon file modification** (nothing existing was touched), but it carries the same class of risk as one: if Horizon ships a change to its default collection template, `collection.all.json` won't inherit it — it'll silently diverge, since it's a static copy, not a reference. Tracked below as a fork to watch, distinct from the upgrade-tracked edits (those are diffs against a Horizon file; this is a duplicate of one).

**Fork to watch (not an upgrade-tracked edit — nothing in this file existed in Horizon before):**

| File | What it forked from | Risk | What to check after a Horizon update |
|---|---|---|---|
| `templates/collection.all.json` | A full copy of `templates/collection.json`'s title/filters/product-grid sections, as they stood 2026-08-20, plus one appended CTA section | If Horizon changes the default collection template (new block, changed setting, a fix), `collection.all.json` doesn't inherit it automatically — it'll quietly fall behind and the "All products" listing will diverge from every other collection page in ways no diff will surface unless this file is checked specifically | Diff `templates/collection.all.json`'s non-CTA sections against the current `templates/collection.json` after any Horizon update; port forward any changes to the shared sections manually |

## 12. Product card: three real shapes in the source, one canonical build

The design's reusable `ProductCard.jsx` component only covers the Shop grid and the product-page cross-sell rail. Two more product-card treatments exist as bespoke, non-component markup: the Home "Seasonal Spotlight" rail (4:5 image, no card frame, title+price on one row, no quick-add) and the mobile Shop grid (1:1 image, 10px radius, sans title, floating pill quick-add over the image). Full research and per-shape spec in conversation, 2026-08-20.

**Decisions taken:**
- **Canonical framed card** (bg `#FFFFFF`, border `1px #E3D6C5`, radius 16px) applied to every grid context: `templates/collection.json` (the shared default — reaches Tea/Condiments/Pantry too, since the card *style* should be consistent everywhere even though the Shop CTA content shouldn't, per §11's collection.all.json reasoning), `templates/collection.all.json`, and `templates/product.json`'s recommendations rail (which had no card frame at all before — `border: "none"` — despite the design's cross-sell rail using the same framed `ProductCard.jsx` as the Shop grid).
- **Home rail**: converted `templates/index.json`'s `product-list` from `layout_type: "grid"` to native `"carousel"` (`carousel_on_mobile: true` too — the source scrolls horizontally on both breakpoints), image ratio to `"portrait"` (Horizon's native 4:5, an exact match, not a concession), card stays frameless (already `border: "none"`).
- **Mobile Shop grid**: collapsed to the same responsive card as desktop, not replicated — logged in `REVIEW-NOTES.md` for a phone-review judgement call, flagged there as a different class of gap from the other two mobile omissions found this build (a fully specified alternative design, not an absence).
- **Stars**: added Horizon's native `review` block to both the cross-sell rail and the Home rail (the design showed stars desktop-only in both places; decided to always show, same reasoning as the CTA button-colour fix in §10). **The block renders nothing until reviews data exists** — it reads `product.metafields.reviews.rating`/`rating_count` (Judge.me-compatible; the design's own mockup caption says as much: *"Content shown is sample data until the store is connected"*) and outputs nothing at all when the metafield is blank, live or in the editor. Adding it now is zero-risk — no visible regression — but it won't actually show anything until a reviews app is installed and populated.
- **Quick-add on the Home rail**: the design has none there, but Horizon's quick-add is a single global toggle (`settings.quick_add`/`mobile_quick_add`) with no per-section override — there's no native way to suppress it on just this rail without a section override. Left on rather than escalate for a secondary, low-stakes interaction; flagged here rather than silently building something incomplete.

**Correction found while verifying `product_corner_radius`/`card_corner_radius` before using them, per instruction — these do NOT reach the product grid at all.** Both are real theme settings (`config/settings_schema.json`, under the **Search** category), but the only place either is actually consumed is `snippets/search-modal.liquid`'s own scoped style block — feeding the predictive-search/quick-search-results UI, not the main collection grid or product-list sections. `_product-card`/`_product-card-gallery` each carry their own independent, per-instance `border_radius` setting instead, set per template/block — confirmed by reading `blocks/_product-card-gallery.liquid`, `blocks/_product-card.liquid`, and `config/settings_schema.json`'s section header directly, not assumed from the setting names. The 16px radius above was therefore applied per-template, not as a global settings change — the original proposal's "fix these two globally" would have done nothing to the actual grid cards.

**Genuinely global settings, confirmed reaching `_product-card-gallery.liquid`/`card-gallery.liquid` directly:** `card_hover_effect` (`none` → `lift`), `badge_position` (`top-right` → `top-left`), `badge_corner_radius` (`100` → `4`) — all in `config/settings_data.json`, one change each, reaching every card site-wide.

**Skipped, logged for later:** the one-off "Bronze Medal" award badge (a single sample product, not a commerce state Horizon's native sale/sold-out badge logic can drive) — see `CLAUDE.md` Open questions.
