## 13. Wholesale page — finished in one pass, all five sections

Audited against the full design source, 2026-08-20: five sections exist in the design, in order — Hero, Wholesale Benefits, Traceable to the grower, Who we supply / Packaging Formats, Partnership Enquiry. Only two were built (Benefits and Traceable), and in the wrong relative order (Traceable was first, Benefits second — reversed from the source). Built the remaining three and fixed the order in one pass rather than leave the page part-done.

**Structure** — every new section is the same native `section` + `group` + `text`/`button`/`icon` composition already established (§10–§12), no new blocks except the enquiry form (below). One new technique: Horizon's generic `section` has no per-side border setting (all-sides only, like `group`), so the design's `border-top`/`border-bottom` hairlines between sections are built with a standalone `_divider` block placed first (for a top border) or last (for a bottom border) in the section's own block list, rather than faked with the section's own border setting, which would add unwanted side borders too.

**The Hero's "Submit Enquiry Form" button needed a real anchor to the enquiry section, and neither obvious way to give it one works.** Checked first: an `id` attribute in a richtext `text` setting — rejected outright by the platform (`Attribute 'id="..."' is not permitted on tag '<p>'`), confirmed by a real upload attempt, not assumed. No block in this theme has a custom-anchor/CSS-id setting either. The only real anchor available is Shopify's own auto-generated section wrapper id (`shopify-section-{template_id}__{section_key}`), read directly from the live DOM: `shopify-section-template--25873630921005__wholesale_enquiry`. The button's `link` is hardcoded to `#shopify-section-template--25873630921005__wholesale_enquiry` — the numeric prefix is the specific template's own stable id (doesn't change on ordinary edits, only if the template were deleted and recreated), but it's still a hardcoded value with no platform guarantee, not something derived at render time. **If this link ever stops scrolling to the right place, re-check the enquiry section's actual rendered id and update the hero button's `link` setting to match.**

**New token**: `--display-1-size` (`clamp(38px, 29.741px + 2.118vw, 56px)`, DESIGN-TOKENS.md §2) for the Hero heading — the first heading this build needed at Display 1 · Hero scale. Note: `type_size_h1` in `config/settings_data.json` already happens to be `56`, matching the target exactly, and might be fluid natively (Horizon's fluid cutoff is 48px, and 56 clears it) — used the custom token anyway rather than the native `h1` preset, for the same reason every other heading this build does: headings are authored `type_preset: "custom"` so `text_color` reaches them (the CLAUDE.md rule), and reaching for a native preset here would be the one heading in the whole build that doesn't follow that pattern.

**New block: `blocks/lambruk-wholesale-enquiry-form.liquid`.** The design's enquiry form has six required fields (business name, contact name, email, phone, business type, location) plus a multi-select "interested in" and a message textarea — Horizon's native `contact-form.liquid` only has `name`/`email`/`phone`/`body`, no split business/contact name, no business type or location, nothing for a multi-select. Built by extending the same pattern `contact-form.liquid` itself uses — `{% form 'contact', id: form_id %}`, the `contact[id]` hidden field, `form.errors`/`form.posted_successfully?` for state — with the extra fields added as their own `contact[Field Name]` inputs. Shopify's contact form forwards any `contact[...]` key into the notification email natively, so this is additive to the native mechanism, not a rebuild of it. "Interested in" is a plain checkbox list (`contact[Interested in][]`), not the design's pill/tag picker — the user's call, made deliberately for the accessibility win, not a fallback. Success state replaces the form fields entirely with the design's exact confirmation copy, rather than native `contact-form.liquid`'s behaviour of showing the success message above the still-visible form.

**Open item, not a theme decision — see `CLAUDE.md`.** The native contact form sends to one address, set globally in Admin (Settings → Notifications). Wholesale enquiries and general customer service currently land in the same inbox unless that's addressed before go-live — flagged there, not fixed here, since there's no theme-side lever for it.

**Known gap:** the Partnership Enquiry section's award callout (Bronze Medal, Melbourne Royal 2024) has no image — same photography dependency already tracked in Open Questions above, not pointed at a placeholder or invented substitute.

**Attempted to verify a real submission live; couldn't complete it — and this time pinned down exactly why, not just "an automated-testing limitation."** Filled and submitted the form repeatedly — JS `.click()`, `form.requestSubmit()`, a genuine OS-level mouse click on the real button (confirmed landing correctly via `elementFromPoint`), on both the local dev proxy and the real `preview_theme_id` Shopify domain. None produced a `/contact` POST in the network log. Ran a control test first: Horizon's own **unmodified native** contact form (`templates/page.contact.json`) fails identically under the same conditions, which rules out the block itself. Then went one step further and bypassed the browser entirely — POSTed directly to `/contact` with `curl`, replicating the exact form fields, using a real preview session cookie. **Got a `403` back: Cloudflare's own "Verifying your connection" managed challenge**, sitting in front of the whole store. That's the actual, confirmed cause — automated, non-browser-fingerprinted POSTs to `/contact` are challenged before they ever reach Shopify's backend. Real human visitors in a real browser aren't affected by this; it's the same protection any Shopify store has. **Still needs one real, human-driven submission to see the live error/success states firsthand** — I've now confirmed why I can't get there myself, not just that I couldn't.

**Bug found this way, fixed 2026-08-20 — the error state showed an icon with no message text.** Root cause: the block's error markup was copied verbatim from `blocks/contact-form.liquid`, which hardcodes `form.errors.messages.email` — the *only* field Horizon's native 4-field form ever needs to report on. This form has six required fields; if the actual failing field is anything other than email, `form.errors` is truthy (icon renders) but the hardcoded `.email` lookup comes back blank (no text) — exactly the symptom reported. Horizon has the correct, general pattern elsewhere in the same theme (`snippets/blog-comment-form.liquid`): iterate `{% for field in form.errors %}`, reading `form.errors.translated_fields[field]` + `form.errors.messages[field]` per field, so every erroring field gets named, not just one. Rebuilt the error block on that pattern instead — added `role="alert"` for screen readers (also missing before), a visible heading ("We couldn't send your enquiry — please fix the following:"), a list item per erroring field, and `aria-invalid`/`aria-describedby` wired to each of the four Shopify-recognized fields (`name`/`email`/`phone`/`body` — the only ones Shopify's contact form validates server-side; the custom fields like Business name have no server-side validation to report on, only the client-side `required` attribute). Colour uses Horizon's existing `--color-error` token rather than a new one.

**Two more bugs, both only surfaceable by a real human submission (Cloudflare blocks automated ones — see above) — reported 2026-08-20, fixed the same session.**

1. **Blocking: the form could not be submitted at all.** `contact[Interested in][]` — array-style field naming for the checkbox group — got rejected server-side with "Interested in is invalid." Shopify's contact form endpoint expects a flat hash of string values per field; sending an array for one key breaks its validation rather than being silently accepted or ignored. No other field in this theme uses array-style naming, so nothing else was at risk of the same bug. Fixed by dropping the checkboxes' `name` attribute entirely (pure UI, nothing submitted for them directly) and adding one hidden `contact[Interested in]` field that a small inline script populates with the checked values joined into a single comma-separated string on submit — a single string field, which is what the endpoint actually accepts.

2. **Severe UX: every field was wiped after a rejected submission.** Shopify's `form` object only echoes back the four fields it natively recognizes — `form.name`/`form.email`/`form.phone`/`form.body` — confirmed against Shopify's own Liquid docs and this theme's native `contact-form.liquid`, which relies on exactly those four and nothing else. There is no server-side echo for arbitrary `contact[...]` fields, so the three custom fields (Business name, Business type, Location) and the interests checkboxes had no way to survive the redirect-back-with-errors round trip — confirmed by checking whether Shopify's `form` drop exposes anything more general than the four named accessors; it doesn't. Fixed two ways: (a) the four native fields now set `value="{{ form.xxx }}"` on the input, the same echo mechanism `contact-form.liquid` already relies on, just missing from this block before; (b) the three custom fields and the interests checkboxes are cached to `sessionStorage` (keyed to the block's own id, so multiple instances never collide) on every submit attempt, and restored from there when the page re-renders with `form.errors` present, then cleared once `form.posted_successfully?` is true. Client-side only, scoped to this one block — not a new site-wide form-handling pattern.

**What I could verify myself vs. what still needs the real submission you're about to do.** Cloudflare's challenge (see above) means I still can't get a real `form.errors` response server-side — so I directly exercised the client-side logic instead, which is fully testable without a server round trip: filled the form, dispatched the `submit` event, and confirmed the hidden `contact[Interested in]` field joined to `"Tea range, Relishes & chutneys"` (a single string, not an array) and that `sessionStorage` captured the three custom fields plus both checked interests in the expected shape. Then cleared the visible fields and ran the exact restore branch from the block against that cached data — all three custom fields and both checkboxes came back correctly. That confirms both fixes' logic is correct. What it can't confirm: that Shopify's backend actually *accepts* the joined string (theory-based on the "is invalid" error being array-vs-string, not empirically re-tested against the real endpoint), and that the real `form.errors`-present page render triggers the restore branch the same way my manual simulation did. Both need your real submission to close out.

**Success state built, 2026-08-20 — replaces the form and title entirely, inside the same card, using resolved tokens throughout.** The "Apply for a commercial account" title was previously a sibling `text` block in `templates/page.wholesale.json`, outside the custom block, so the success state (rendered inside the custom block only) couldn't make it disappear. Folded the title into the block's own Liquid, conditional on the same `posted_ok` flag as the form fields, and removed the now-redundant sibling block from the JSON. Two new tokens added to `lambruk-tokens.css`, both first-time real consumers of values only previously *documented* as needed:
- `--icon-accent: #8A6A32` — flagged back in §5 as needing this exact addition once a consumer existed; this is that consumer. Checked against WCAG 1.4.11's 3:1 non-text threshold (not the 4.5:1 text bar, since this is a decorative icon, not body copy) — 3.51:1 already confirmed against the darker gold-200 (`#E3D6C5`) elsewhere in the design; clears by a wider margin on the lighter gold-100 badge fill here.
- `--icon-badge-background: #EFE9E1` — the design's gold-100/`--divider` hex, given its own name since it's filling a badge here, a different role than the divider role that hex already has a name for.

The eyebrow now uses `var(--text-accent)` (`#986A34`) instead of the raw `#A07037` in the source markup — per the already-resolved finding that the raw value fails AA at small sizes (`DESIGN-TOKENS.md` §1). Heading uses `var(--display-4-size)`/`var(--display-4-line-height)`, the same fluid token already established for this scale, not a flat px value. Verified live by temporarily forcing `posted_ok = true` in the block (reverted immediately after) and reading computed styles directly: badge 56px circular, `rgb(239, 233, 225)` / `rgb(138, 106, 50)` (exact `--icon-badge-background`/`--icon-accent` matches), eyebrow `rgb(152, 106, 52)` (exact `--text-accent` match, not the raw value), both the title and the form grid confirmed absent from the DOM in the success state.

**Scroll-jump on submit — root cause confirmed, fix not yet applied, needs sign-off.** Checked rather than assumed, per instruction: `assets/base.css` sets `html { scroll-behavior: smooth; }` unconditionally at line 29, and again (redundantly) inside a `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference` guard at line ~1832 — plus a third copy on `.page-wrapper` at desktop widths ≥990px (Horizon's actual scroll container at that breakpoint, not `html`). Shopify's `{% form %}` tag automatically appends `#{{ form_id }}` to the form's `action` — confirmed in the rendered HTML (`action="/contact#WholesaleEnquiry-...__form"`) — so after the POST-redirect, the browser lands on a URL with that hash and performs its native "scroll to fragment" on load. With `scroll-behavior: smooth` globally active, that native scroll animates from wherever the page happens to render first (the top) down to the target, instead of landing there instantly — exactly the "jumps to the top and then anchors back down" symptom, and exactly the mechanism guessed before checking.

**Fixed, 2026-08-20 — approved with conditions, then implemented in `layout/theme.liquid`.** The diff landed as proposed (self-disconnecting `MutationObserver` added to also catch `.page-wrapper` as Horizon's real scroll container at desktop widths, and a `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` check so restoration is skipped for users who've set that preference — both per the sign-off conditions). Full change record, pristine/current diff, and the reasoning for why this needed a core-file edit rather than a lower layer live in §6, not duplicated here.

---

**Success-state centering bug — found 2026-08-20, fixed same session.** Reported after a real submission: the success state's content sat slightly left of centre inside the card rather than centred, with the explicit instruction to check rather than assume and verify with computed positions, not by eye. Same class of bug as the earlier quote-panel fix (§ above), confirmed independently rather than taken on the user's hypothesis alone.

Root cause, confirmed via source: `form_col` (`templates/page.wholesale.json`) sets `content_direction: "column"` but only the row-variant alignment settings (`horizontal_alignment`, `vertical_alignment`) — not the column-variant ones (`horizontal_alignment_flex_direction_column`, `vertical_alignment_flex_direction_column`) that `snippets/layout-panel-style.liquid` actually reads once direction isn't `"row"`. Left unset, that setting falls back to `blocks/group.liquid`'s own schema default, `"flex-start"` — which `assets/base.css`'s `.layout-panel-flex--column` rule maps straight to `align-items`, the cross-axis property. The enquiry-form block's own outer `<div class="wholesale-enquiry-form spacing-style">` had no explicit width, so as a flex item in a non-stretch column it sized to shrink-to-fit rather than filling the card — invisible in the ordinary form state, where the two-column input grid's own intrinsic width happens to exceed the available space and gets clamped down to fill it anyway (confirmed live: 527px measured either way), but visible in the success state, where the content's natural width (bounded by `.wholesale-enquiry-form__success-body`'s own `max-width: 32rem`) is narrower than the 527px available — measured at 512px before the fix, sitting flush against the card's left padding (37px) while leaving 52px on the right.

Fixed the same way as quote-panel: added `width: 100%;` directly to the block's own outer div, rather than touching `form_col`'s alignment settings (fixing the column-variant alignment to `center` would only mask the real gap — a `width`-less flex item still wouldn't fill the card, just centre a narrower box inside it). Verified by temporarily forcing `posted_ok = true` (reverted immediately after) and reading computed positions directly: before the fix, the success badge's centre sat 0px right of the card's true centre with asymmetric 37px/52px left/right gaps; after, `.wholesale-enquiry-form` measures exactly 527px — matching the available column width — with symmetric 37px/37px gaps and the badge centre landing exactly on the card centre. Re-checked the ordinary form state afterward to confirm no regression: still exactly 527px wide, same symmetric gaps, form title still renders, success markup still absent.

---

## 14. Text 1 / Text 2 body-copy layer — a gap identified at the start of the build, never closed until now

**This sat unbuilt for the whole project.** The original Horizon settings mapping identified early on that body copy needed a two-step split — Text 1 · Large (16–17px fluid) and Text 2 · Body (fixed 15px) — because Horizon has exactly one native paragraph preset (`type_size_paragraph`, a five-value select: 10/12/14/16/18px, no 15 or 17 option anywhere in it) and the design's actual body-copy sizes don't land on any of those five values. The layer needed to reach those two in-between steps was never actually written. `type_size_paragraph` sat on Horizon's untouched stock default, `"14"` (`config/settings_data.json`), for the entire build up to this point. Confirmed by grepping `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `lambruk-tokens.css` for any prior trace of it before starting this pass — zero hits; it wasn't even logged as a known gap, just silently absent. Every `text` block across every page that used `type_preset: "rte"` or `"paragraph"` without its own `font_size` override — the default way to author body copy — was rendering at 14px instead of 15–17px, discovered 2026-08-20 while investigating a Wholesale-page spacing complaint that turned out to trace back to this.

**The fix, following §9's established technique.** Two new tokens in `assets/lambruk-tokens.css`:
- `--text-1-size: clamp(16px, 15.541px + 0.118vw, 17px)` (DESIGN-TOKENS.md §2's Text 1 · Large, recomputed independently from the 390→1240px viewport range rather than trusted from the doc — matches)
- `--text-2-size: 15px` (Text 2 · Body, fixed, no interpolation, per the same source)

Applied via the same passthrough §9 already proved: `type_preset: "custom"` + `font_size: "var(--text-1-size)"` or `"var(--text-2-size)"`, which `typography-style.liquid` echoes verbatim into `--font-size` on its failed-rem-parse fallback path. Line-height doesn't get that same free ride (§9's own caveat) — but for body-scale text the caveat is actually harmless: the `type` variable both `text.liquid` and `typography-style.liquid` compute from a failed parse defaults to `'body'` regardless, which is already the correct bucket here (unlike a heading passed through the same mechanism, which would incorrectly land in `'body'` too). `line_height: "loose"` was set on every instance — `--line-height--body-loose` is `1.6` (`theme-styles-variables.liquid`), an exact match for Text 2 and close enough to Text 1's 1.65 target (0.05 off) that a new token wasn't worth adding for line-height alone.

**Per-instance size decisions were checked against the design source, not assumed from the step name.** For every block, the actual raw `font-size` in `design_handoff_website/LambrukPantry Desktop.dc.html` was grepped and matched against DESIGN-TOKENS.md §2's "raw sizes absorbed" table (raw desktop 17/18/19 → Text 1; raw desktop 15/16 → Text 2) rather than guessed from context. Full list, by file:

| File | Block(s) | Raw source px | Step |
|---|---|---|---|
| `page.wholesale.json` | Hero body | 17 | Text 1 |
| `page.wholesale.json` | Wholesale Benefits × 3 card bodies | 15 | Text 2 |
| `page.wholesale.json` | Traceable paragraph | 16 | Text 2 |
| `page.wholesale.json` | Spec pairs × 4 (title+desc combined) | 17/15 mixed | Text 2 (see note below) |
| `page.wholesale.json` | Enquiry intro, award, stocked, direct value, dispatch value | 16/15/15/16/16 | Text 2 |
| `index.json` | Cafe-intro body ("Join us in the heart of Ballina…") | 17 | Text 1 |
| `page.cafe.json` | Both intro paragraphs | 16 / 16 | Text 2 |
| `page.our-story.json` | 3× "Meet Paige" paragraphs | 17 / 17 / 17 | Text 1 |
| `page.our-story.json` | 3× "How we make things" trio bodies | 15 / 15 / 15 | Text 2 |
| `product.json` | 3× trust-row bodies | 15 / 15 / 15 | Text 2 |
| `sections/footer-group.json` | Footer tagline | 15 | Text 2 |
| `collection.json` / `collection.all.json` | `{{ closest.collection.description }}` | dynamic, no source value | Text 2 (default — general prose, no per-instance value to check) |
| `404.json` | Custom 404 copy ("The pantry is still full…") | not in the mockup (no 404 page there) | Text 2 (default) |

**The Wholesale spec pairs are a known compromise, not an oversight.** Each pair is one combined RTE block rendering `<p>title</p><p>description</p>` — the design wants title at 17px and description at 15px, but a single block's `font_size` setting applies uniformly to both paragraphs, and splitting them into two independently-sized blocks is item 5 from the spacing/rhythm review (explicitly out of scope for this pass — Root Cause 1 only). Set to Text 2 (15px) for now: exact for the description, 2px short for the title, both an improvement over the prior 14px on both. Revisit when item 5's structural fix (splitting the block) lands.

**What was deliberately left alone, and why — checked per-file via `git diff` first, then per-instance via the actual text content, since diff scope proved too narrow on its own** (two genuinely-authored blocks — the homepage cafe-intro body and the 404 copy — were committed in earlier sessions and didn't show in the working-tree diff, so relying on `git status` alone would have missed them; caught by re-scanning every `rte`/`paragraph` hit sitewide for real Lambruk-voiced copy rather than trusting the file-level diff signal):
- **Native Horizon elements** — `product-title`, `collection-title`, `price` blocks all carry their own explicit `font_size` (usually `1rem`) already; not part of this bug (never defaulted to 14) and not body-scale copy in the Text 1/2 sense (titles/prices have their own scale). Left untouched in `cart.json`, `collection.json`, `collection.all.json`, `search.json`, `list-collections.json`, `product.json`, plus `blocks/product-card.liquid` and `blocks/product-recommendations.liquid` (both pristine, unmodified Horizon files — confirmed via `git status`, out of scope regardless of content).
- **Dynamic product/page description already at 16px** — `product.json`'s `{{ closest.product.description }}` and `page.contact.json`'s `{{ closest.page.content }}` both already carry an explicit `font_size: "1rem"` (or, for contact, no build/content at all — the page isn't customized). Not exhibiting the 14px symptom; left as-is rather than folded into this pass.
- **Meta-text, correctly still 14px by design.** DESIGN-TOKENS.md §2 documents a "Text 2 · Body meta-text exception" — nav links, addresses, timestamps stay fixed at 14px, distinct from the 15px prose default. `sections/footer-group.json`'s two nav-link-list blocks (Shop column, Company column) and its contact block (phone/email/address) all render as `rte` at 14px — that's the correct, intentional value, not this bug. Confirmed against the doc before leaving them, not assumed from "well it's already 14."
- **`password.json`'s newsletter line** ("Sign up for our newsletter to be the first to know when we launch") — generic, not in the design mockup, reads as Horizon's own stock password-page copy rather than authored Lambruk content. Left alone; if this page gets a real content pass later, revisit.

**Item 1 (hero max-width) folded into this pass, per instruction — the em-based token was coupled to the exact bug this layer fixes.** `max_width: "narrow"` on a `text` block resolves to `--max-width--body-narrow: 22.75em` (`theme-styles-variables.liquid`), and `em` resolves against the block's own font-size — at the pre-fix 14px, that's 318.5px, confirmed live to the decimal against the rendered width. Fixing the font-size alone only recovers this to ~360–390px at 16–17px — still under two-thirds of the design's 620px target, because `max_width` is a closed three-value select (`narrow`/`normal`/`none`) with no free-text passthrough the way `font_size` has: the CSS variable it references has to exist under the literal name the enum string produces, so an arbitrary value doesn't route through the same fallback `typography-style.liquid` gives font-size. Added one new token, `--max-width--body-wide` (originally `620px`, later raised to `640px` — see §15), and one new (unlisted, but unvalidated-server-side per §9's own finding) enum value, `max_width: "wide"`, scoped to just the Wholesale hero body — inert everywhere else since no other block references it. **Verified empirically, not assumed from the mechanism working for font_size** — synced live and read `getComputedStyle(...).getPropertyValue('--max-width')`, got `620px` back exactly at the time, both the constraint applying (three lines instead of four at desktop) and gracefully falling back to `min(100%, 620px)` on mobile (measured 343px available at a 375px viewport, well under the cap, no overflow). §15 found the 3-line result was a sub-pixel boundary case, not a real shortfall in the 620px figure, and raised it to 640px for headroom.

**Verified live across every page touched**, not just spot-checked on Wholesale: Wholesale hero (17px + 620px max-width at the time, both desktop and mobile), Wholesale Benefits cards, Traceable paragraph, spec description (15px each); Our Story "Meet Paige" (17px) and trio (15px); Cafe intro ×2 (15px); homepage cafe-intro (17px) and sitewide footer tagline (15px, checked from the homepage since the footer renders on every page); product page trust row ×3 (15px); 404 page (15px). `shopify theme check --fail-level=error` run clean across all nine touched files afterward — 369 files inspected, no offenses.

**Not touched this pass, by instruction:** Root Cause 2 (vertical rhythm) and items 2–7 from the spacing review remain exactly as measured before this fix — deliberately, since those deltas were taken against 14px text and need re-measuring now that body copy is actually 15–17px before any spacing change is proposed.

---

## 15. Eyebrow tracking — the second specified-but-never-built gap found this pass, plus the hero max-width follow-up

**Reported 2026-08-20, initially as a correction to an earlier (wrong) description of the bug** — the user had the direction backwards in a first pass (thought our tracking was wider than the design's); asked to verify from source rather than take the correction at face value, per the project's standing rule. Checked independently: the design's eyebrow tracking (hero, "Who we supply", "Available specifications", and every other eyebrow — `design_handoff_website/LambrukPantry Desktop.dc.html`) is uniformly `letter-spacing: .22em`. DESIGN-TOKENS.md §2 already documents this exact value ("Eyebrow / overline — Text 3 · Micro (11–12px)... 0.22em letter-spacing") — so, same as Root Cause 1, the target was specified from the start and simply never reached a real token.

**Root cause, same closed-enum mechanism as `max_width`, not `font_size`'s free passthrough.** `letter_spacing` on a `text` block is a three-value select (`tight`/`normal`/`loose`), string-interpolated into `--letter-spacing--{type}-{value}`. Checked every value Horizon defines for all three types (display/heading/body) in `theme-styles-variables.liquid` — none exceed `0.03em` ("loose"). 0.22em was never reachable through any native option, and no eyebrow-specific token existed anywhere in `lambruk-tokens.css` before this (grepped for it — zero hits). Confirmed live before the fix: computed `letter-spacing` on the hero eyebrow, "Suitable Business Partners", and "Packaging Formats" was identically `0.36px` at 12px font-size — exactly `0.03em`, ~7.3× narrower than the 0.22em target, and identical everywhere by construction (every eyebrow block copies the same settings).

**Fixed with a new named token, `--letter-spacing--body-eyebrow: 0.22em`, not by redefining the existing `loose` step.** Redefining `--letter-spacing--body-loose` directly was ruled out: the Text 1/Text 2 body-copy blocks from §14 also use `line_height: "loose"` (a *different* variable, `--line-height--body-loose`, unaffected by this), but if any future body-copy block ever needed loose *tracking* too, redefining the shared step would leak this eyebrow-specific value into unrelated content. `letter_spacing: "eyebrow"` — an off-enum value, same technique as `max_width: "wide"` in §14 — applied to all 17 real eyebrow instances found sitewide (see table below). Every eyebrow computes `type: "body"` in `typography-style.liquid`'s own parse (`0.75rem` → `0.75`, under every display/heading cutoff), so only the body-scoped token was needed.

**Explicitly not touched: the "01/02/03/04" numbered labels on Wholesale's "Who we supply" list.** These share the same `letter_spacing: "loose"` value as the eyebrows structurally, but the design tracks them at a *different* value — `letter-spacing: .16em` (source line ~790), not 0.22em. Conflating them with the eyebrow fix would have been wrong; left on `loose` (0.03em) for now, a separate, still-open gap of its own if it's ever worth chasing.

**Every real eyebrow found and fixed, by file:**

| File | Instances |
|---|---|
| `page.wholesale.json` | 8 — hero eyebrow, "Australian Provenance", "Wholesale Benefits", "Suitable Business Partners", "Packaging Formats", "Partnership Enquiry", "Direct", "Dispatch" |
| `sections/footer-group.json` | 3 — "Shop", "Explore", "General" column headings |
| `page.our-story.json` | 2 — "Meet Paige", "The Lambruk Promise" |
| `page.cafe.json` | 2 — "Saturday & Sunday Rituals", "Cafe" |
| `collection.all.json` | 1 — "Not sure what to pick" |
| `index.json` | 1 — "Introducing" |

**Verified live**, not assumed from the mechanism working for `max_width`: hero eyebrow computed `2.64px` (`12px × 0.22`, exact) after the fix; spot-checked "Introducing" (homepage) and the footer's "Shop" heading (checked scoped to `footer` specifically, since a first pass accidentally matched the nav's own "Shop" link instead and read `normal` — a test-script scoping mistake, not a real miss) — both `2.64px`. `shopify theme check --fail-level=error` clean afterward, 369 files, no offenses.

**Hero body max-width raised from 620px to 640px, same token, in the same pass.** §14 verified 620px matched the design's declared value exactly, but the resulting render still wrapped to 3 lines where the design's screenshot shows 2. Investigated rather than assumed short: measured the design's own line-1 break text ("...pantry essentials") at Geist 400/17px — it comes to `620.1px`, i.e. the design's own 2-line break sits 0.1px over its own declared 620px cap, a sub-pixel rendering-engine margin, not a real shortfall in the value. Raising the token to `640px` gives that word reliable headroom without deviating meaningfully from the design's own number. **Verified the break lands exactly where the design's does**: live line-detection (Range API, walking the rendered text node's per-character bounding boxes) at 640px produces `"We make small-batch teas, relishes, chutneys and table-ready pantry essentials"` / `"for boutique hotels, cafes, country stores and providores across Australia."` — an exact match to the design screenshot's own two lines, word for word.

## 16. Heading-size audit — 32px vs 36px, counted across the whole design package (data only, not yet acted on)

**Requested 2026-08-20 after the hero H1 (58→56px) and the two Wholesale "Who we supply"/"Available specifications" H2s (36→32px) were confirmed live-smaller than the design.** The H1 gap (2px, 3.4%) was waved off as genuine, invisible rounding — already the documented Display 1 · Hero consolidation (DESIGN-TOKENS.md §2's absorption table lists 58/56/54 raw all landing on the 56px canonical step). The two H2s (4px, 11% — a more noticeable gap) were held back for a count first, per instruction: **"If 36 clearly dominates, raise `--display-4-size` to 36 and let 32 round up. If both appear substantially and serve distinct roles, propose adding a step."** Counted every raw `36px`/`32px` heading in `design_handoff_website/LambrukPantry Desktop.dc.html` (desktop reference only — `Mobile.dc.html`'s own 32/36 hits are the *mobile* companions of other, larger desktop steps, e.g. Display 3 · Feature's 42px desktop / 32px mobile pair for "Traceable to the grower" — a different question, not counted here to avoid conflating steps). No fix applied yet — data only, as asked.

**Raw counts: 32px appears almost twice as often as 36px (9 vs 5) — the opposite of what "36 clearly dominates" would need.**

| Size | Count | Instances (page — heading text) |
|---|---|---|
| 32px | 9 | Homepage — "Explore by category", "The Seasonal Spotlight", "Curated for every occasion", "Loved at country tables", "Set another table"; Product — "What our customers say"; Cafe — "Reserve your table", "Inside Lambruk Kitchen"; Homepage — "Bring the Lambruk experience home" |
| 36px | 5 | Wholesale — "Who we supply", "Available specifications"; Contact/policy (unbuilt) — "Shipping", "Returns & Refunds", "Terms & Conditions" |

**But the two groups aren't the same role — they're structurally distinct, which is why a raw count alone doesn't settle it.** Every 32px instance introduces a **full-width section** (`max-width:1240px` container, single column) — almost always preceded by its own eyebrow ("New Releases", "Curated Occasions", "Customer Reviews", "Gallery"...) and followed by a horizontal rail or grid of cards spanning the full container width. This is one consistent, repeated pattern across Homepage, Product, and Cafe. Every 36px instance instead sits in a **narrower context**: the two Wholesale headings are each one column of a `grid-template-columns:1fr 1fr` two-column module (effective column width ~540px, well under the 1160px a 32px heading gets), and the three policy headings sit in an `max-width:820px` single-column prose page — a different container class entirely, closer to article measure than section-rail measure.

**This doesn't fully settle it either, though — there's a third data point that complicates "narrow column → bigger heading."** Other two-column module headings elsewhere in the design ("Traceable to the grower", "A home cook who kept going" / Meet Paige, "Tell us about your business" / Wholesale enquiry) are raw **42px**, already correctly built at `--display-3-size` (40px desktop) — i.e., *larger* than both 36 and 32, in the *same* narrow-column structural position as the Wholesale pair. So "narrow column" alone doesn't predict 36px either; something else (possibly: these two headings appear *paired side-by-side in the same row*, unlike the single dominant heading in the 42px cases) may be driving the smaller size, or it may just be inconsistency in the source itself — I can't confirm intent from the markup alone.

**Recommendation for you to weigh, not applied:** given 32px is the numeric majority *and* the two groups look like genuinely different roles (full-width rail intro vs. paired/narrow-column heading) rather than one drifting toward the other, this reads as the "propose adding a step" case rather than "raise to 36." But raising `--display-4-size` to 36 would also fix the Wholesale pair specifically if that's the priority and the 9 homepage/product/cafe instances turn out not to matter as much in practice (none of them are built yet, so there's no live regression risk either way — worth factoring into the decision). No token or JSON changed for this item.

## 17. Full audit — every proposal in this file and DESIGN-TOKENS.md checked against what's actually in the theme

**Requested 2026-08-20, after the eyebrow-tracking gap (§15) turned up the same way Root Cause 1 did (§14) — the user wanted to know if there were more.** Method: read every documented decision in this file and in `design/DESIGN-TOKENS.md` (the "original Horizon mapping"), then check each one's actual consumer(s) live or in the JSON/CSS source, rather than trusting the doc's own account of its status. Two genuinely new, live, previously-unflagged findings turned up — both bigger in practical impact than either of the previous two, because they hit the Product and Collection pages rather than one page's hero. Two smaller, dormant gaps also turned up. A short list of already-known, already-flagged items is included at the end for completeness, not as new discoveries.

**Finding 1 — the Product page's own title (not the card title) was never given the Display-scale treatment at all, and it's visibly wrong.** Checked every heading role on the site against the canonical type scale; the main Product Detail Page heading — literally `<h1>{{ closest.product.title }}</h1>` in `product.json` — turned out to be byte-identical to Horizon's pristine baseline (confirmed via `git show b2d7c4b:templates/product.json`, zero diff): `type_preset: "h3"`, an inert `font_size: "1rem"` left over from Horizon's own default JSON that has no effect (non-`"custom"` presets don't read `font_size` at all — `typography-style.liquid`'s whole capture block is gated on `preset == 'custom'`). Verified live on `/products/beetroot-relish-260g`: **32px flat, no fluid step** — Horizon's native `type_size_h3` (32, under the 48px fluid cutoff), not any Display step. The design's own raw value for this exact role is **46px** (source: `<h1 ...font-size:46px...>{{ product.name }}</h1>`), which by DESIGN-TOKENS.md §2's own absorption table belongs to **Display 2 · Title** (48px canonical) — a step that exists in the documented scale but was never once referenced anywhere in this file, and has no token in `lambruk-tokens.css` at all. This is the single most-visited page type on the storefront rendering its own product name at roughly two-thirds the intended size, and nobody had flagged it before this sweep.

**Finding 2 — a second, distinct bug on the same headings: colour, not just size, on every page using a *native* (non-`"custom"`) heading preset without an explicit `text_color`.** The existing rule in this file (`CLAUDE.md`, restated §1: "heading `text` blocks need an explicit `text_color`... without it, it silently inherits body-text colour") was reasoned through and verified only for `type_preset: "custom"` headings — "every heading actually authored this build so far uses `type_preset: custom`" (§1, written 2026-08-20, true *at the time*). This sweep found the rule doesn't cover *native*-preset headings (`h1`–`h6`) the same way, and several exist, unnoticed until now:

| Page | Heading | Preset | Colour rendered |
|---|---|---|---|
| Product (`product.json`) | `{{ closest.product.title }}` | `h3` | `rgb(74, 84, 120)` — body grey, not navy |
| Product (`product.json`) | "You may also like" | `h4` | Same mechanism, `text_color: ""` |
| Collection (`collection.json`/`.all.json`, every collection) | `{{ closest.collection.title }}` | `h2` | `rgb(74, 84, 120)` — confirmed live on `/collections/all`, font-size a coincidentally-correct 48px, colour still wrong |
| 404 (`404.json`) | "This page has left the shelf" | `h1` | `rgb(74, 84, 120)` — confirmed live, font-size a coincidentally-correct 56px |

**Root cause, traced to a specific, higher-specificity Horizon rule the bare `h1`–`h6` override in `lambruk-tokens.css` doesn't reach.** `assets/base.css:647-659` defines `h1, .h1.h1, .text-block.h1 > *, .text-block.h1 :is(h1, h2, h3, h4, h5, h6) { ...; color: var(--color); }` — a *combined* selector where the last branch (`.text-block.h1 :is(h1...)`) has specificity (0,2,1), well above `lambruk-tokens.css`'s bare `h1 { color: var(--text-strong); }` (0,0,1). `--color` is only ever set inline when the block's own `text_color` setting is non-blank (`text.liquid`'s style capture); when it's blank, `color: var(--color)` is invalid at computed-value time, and for an inherited property like `color` that means falling through to the parent `.text-block`'s own computed colour — which resolves through the *same* pattern (`color: var(--color, var(--color-foreground))`) down to `--color-foreground`, the global body-text colour. Confirmed this is specifically the *native-preset* path, not the `custom`-preset one already covered: a `custom`-preset heading has no competing `.text-block.h1 :is(...)`-shaped rule (its class is `custom`, not `h1`–`h6`), so the bare tag-selector override is the only rule touching it and wins outright — exactly why the existing §1 reasoning held for every heading built so far, and exactly why it stopped holding the moment a *native*-preset heading showed up.

**Not fixed in this pass — reporting only, per the audit's own scope.** The fix is mechanical once known (either add `text_color` to each of these four blocks, matching the existing convention, or add a lower-effort blanket rule — worth deciding which, since a blanket fix here risks the same kind of quiet regression `.h3 text-display-3`'s contrast-override case warned about in §1). Flagging for a decision rather than changing it unasked, consistent with how §1 handled the product-card-border finding.

**Finding 3 — motion tokens proposed in §4 with example code were never added anywhere.** `--lambruk-dur-fast`, `--lambruk-dur-base`, `--lambruk-dur-slow`, `--lambruk-ease-out`, and the worked example (`.quote-panel__eyebrow { transition: color var(--lambruk-dur-fast) var(--lambruk-ease-out); }`) — grepped `lambruk-tokens.css` and every block/section/snippet `.liquid` file: zero hits, anywhere. Unlike Findings 1 and 2, this one is currently **dormant, not live-wrong** — no block or section built so far uses a `:hover`/transition rule of its own (checked `quote-panel.liquid` and `lambruk-wholesale-enquiry-form.liquid` specifically, the two most interactive custom blocks; neither has one). This is arguably not a bug at all under `lambruk-tokens.css`'s own stated policy ("add new steps/tokens here as blocks actually need them — don't pre-build the full set before there's a consumer") — flagged so it isn't lost, not because it's currently causing anything wrong.

**Finding 4 — Text 3 · Micro's documented mobile shrink was never implemented; every eyebrow is a flat 12px, not `clamp(11px, ..., 12px)`.** DESIGN-TOKENS.md §2 lists Text 3 · Micro as fluid (11px mobile → 12px desktop), same fluid-step treatment as every Display step. Every eyebrow across the site (see §15's table — 17 instances) uses a literal `"font_size": "0.75rem"` — a fixed value, not a token, so there's no mobile reduction at all. Minor in practice (1px), but a real, confirmed gap of the same shape as the others — logged here rather than silently left, matching the pattern this whole audit is trying to catch.

**Checked and confirmed correctly implemented, not gaps:** the Display 1/3/4 clamp tokens, Text 1/2 (§14), eyebrow tracking (§15), `--text-accent`/`--icon-accent`/`--icon-badge-background`, `--color-border` + the product-card border activation, the three accepted-Horizon-value concessions (§3), and the icon+heading+copy trio's `type_preset: "h4"` technique (§10) — all of Wholesale Benefits' and Our Story's `h4`-preset card headings **do** carry an explicit `text_color`, unlike the four in Finding 2, so they don't share that bug.

**Already known, already flagged — listed for completeness, not new discoveries:** native product-card borders rendering navy instead of gold (§1, explicitly flagged as an open decision); the `custom-typography` line-height caveat left unsolved on the five `media-with-content` headings (§9); the Wholesale "01/02/03/04" numbered labels' own, different 0.16em tracking value, explicitly not folded into the eyebrow fix (§15); the still-open contact-form-notification-routing question (§13); the homepage hero/Seasonal Spotlight rail still missing (Open Questions).

---

## 18. §17's findings, fixed — Display 2, the heading-colour rule, the new Module step, and eyebrow's fluid size

**All four of §17's actionable findings closed 2026-08-20** (Finding 3, the motion tokens, correctly left alone — no consumers, matches the project's own build-on-demand policy).

**1. Product page title — mapped to Display 2, the token built.** Added `--display-2-size: clamp(34px, 27.576px + 1.647vw, 48px)` / `--display-2-line-height: 1.06` to `lambruk-tokens.css` — the canonical 48px value, not the raw 46px the product-title source line carries, same treatment DESIGN-TOKENS.md §2's own absorption table already gives every other step (Display 1 absorbs the hero's raw 58 the same way). `product.json`'s title block converted from `type_preset: "h3"` (Horizon's untouched default, 32px flat) to `type_preset: "custom"` + `font: var(--font-heading--family)` + `font_size: "var(--display-2-size)"` + explicit `text_color: "#131A3E"` — the same convention every other Display-scale heading in this build already follows, `line_height: "normal"` included (matches the existing precedent across every other `var(--display-N-size)` consumer, checked directly rather than assumed — see §9's line-height caveat, unchanged, still applies the same way here). Verified live on `/products/beetroot-relish-260g`: **48px, `rgb(19, 26, 62)`** (`#131A3E`) — both the size and (once Finding 2 below landed) the colour, fully correct.

**2. Heading colour on native presets — fixed at the rule level, and the first attempt was wrong, caught by re-verifying rather than trusting the plan.** Added a new rule matching `base.css`'s own `.text-block.h{N} :is(h1, h2, h3, h4, h5, h6)` selector shape (specificity 0,2,1) for all six preset classes, loading after `base.css` so it wins the cascade on the `color` property. First version used `color: var(--color, var(--text-strong))` — a fallback, reasoning that an explicit `text_color` should still be able to win. Verified live anyway rather than assuming the fallback logic held, and it didn't: the Collection page's `<h1>` still rendered `#4A5478` after this landed. Traced why: `--color` isn't actually undefined on these blocks. `contrast-override.liquid` — rendered by the section/group wrapping the heading (`main-collection.liquid`, the exact call already flagged in §1 for the product-card-border finding, same mechanism, different property this time) — sets `--color` on `.color-custom-{id} .text-block` whenever that section has *any* `background_color` set, even one visually identical to the plain page background. Confirmed via the actual generated `<style>` tag in the live page: `.color-custom-template--...__section .text-block { --color: #4A5478; }` — a real, defined value (the page's ordinary body-text colour), not an absent one. `var(--color, fallback)` only ever reaches its fallback when `--color` is truly unset anywhere in the ancestor chain, which this ambient section-level rule prevents. Switched to a flat `color: var(--text-strong)`, matching the pre-existing bare `h1`–`h6` rule's own already-proven approach (§1: "verified... no other component currently combines a Horizon `.h1`–`.h6` utility class with a contrast-override-scoped surface") — the same trade-off, deliberately re-applied rather than rediscovered as a surprise.

**Checked nothing else on the site relies on the old (wrong) behaviour, per instruction, before relying on the flat override:** every custom-preset heading with its own `text_color` carries the `custom` class, not `.h1`–`.h6` — this rule's selectors structurally can't match those blocks at all, override behaviour is irrelevant to them. `quote-panel`'s `.h3.text-display-3` element is hand-authored Liquid, not routed through `text.liquid`'s `.text-block` wrapper, so it was never a candidate for the `.text-block.h{N} :is(...)` branch either — confirmed live, still renders its own light-on-navy colour (`rgb(247, 245, 242)`) unaffected. Re-verified all four original Finding-2 cases after the flat-override fix: Product title, "You may also like", Collection `<h1>`, 404 `<h1>` — all `rgb(19, 26, 62)`.

**3. Heading scale — added `--display-module-size`, role-named, not renumbered.** DESIGN-TOKENS.md §2 gets a new row, "Display · Module" (28px mobile / 36px desktop / `clamp(28px, 24.329px + 0.941vw, 36px)`), inserted between Feature and Section rather than slotted in as a renumbered Display tier — named for the role (a narrower content module: one column of a paired grid, or a narrow single-column prose page) so the distinction from Display 4 · Section's full-width rail role stays legible without a footnote. Full reasoning and the corrected absorption table live in DESIGN-TOKENS.md §2 directly, not duplicated here — the short version: 32px is the numeric majority (9 vs 5) but the two sizes are consistently different roles, not one drifting toward the other, so folding 36 into 32 was the wrong call from the original mapping pass. `lambruk-tokens.css` mirrors the same token. Applied to both live consumers — Wholesale's "Who we supply" and "Available specifications" — swapping `font_size: "var(--display-4-size)"` for `"var(--display-module-size)"`, nothing else on those blocks touched. Verified live: both now **36px**, up from 32px, still `#131A3E`, still Instrument Serif.

**4. Eyebrows — flat 12px replaced with the documented fluid clamp.** Added `--text-3-size: clamp(11px, 10.541px + 0.118vw, 12px)` (DESIGN-TOKENS.md §2's own long-standing figure, unchanged, just finally wired up). All 17 eyebrow instances from §15's table updated from the literal `"font_size": "0.75rem"` to `"font_size": "var(--text-3-size)"` — same file list, same instances, no new ones found or missed (re-swept to confirm). Verified live: `12px` at desktop (1280px, unchanged from before — the clamp's max exactly matches the old fixed value, so no desktop regression), `11px` at a 375px mobile viewport (Wholesale hero eyebrow, checked directly) — the fluid step that was documented from the start and never reached a real token until now.

**Verified together, not just individually** — loaded the Product page, a Collection page (`/collections/all?view=all`), and the 404 page after all four fixes landed, reading computed `fontSize`/`color` (and `letterSpacing` on Wholesale's eyebrow, and the two Module headings' size) directly rather than trusting the CSS in isolation. `shopify theme check --fail-level=error` clean afterward — 369 files, no offenses.

## 19. Client review pass — Footer, Wholesale, Product page, Shop All (2026-08-20)

Every item checked against the design source before touching anything, per instruction; two items (hero divider position, the Shop All filter row) reported rather than built, also per instruction. One item (Packaging Formats title weight) contradicted the source and was flagged rather than followed literally.

**1. Footer policy links — now right-aligned.** Design's bottom bar (source: copyright left, `display:flex;justify-content:space-between`, policy links + social icons grouped together on the right) doesn't match Horizon's native 3-block `footer-utilities` layout, which lays out exactly 3 blocks as start/center/end — putting policy links (block 2) dead centre, not grouped with social (block 3) on the right. No CSS override cleanly reproduces a single right-hand cluster from two separate grid tracks. Folded the social icons into `lambruk-policy-links.liquid` (new `instagram_url`/`facebook_url` settings, icons via Horizon's own `render 'icon'`, matching `social-links.liquid`'s own pattern) and removed the separate `social-links` block, dropping the section to 2 blocks — which Horizon's own native start/end 2-block layout matches exactly, no override needed. Verified live: `utilities--blocks-2`, copyright at the left edge, policy links + both icons flush at the right edge.

**2. Wholesale hero divider — reported, not built, per instruction.** Confirmed again from source (not just recalled from earlier this session): the design's hero section genuinely has `border-bottom:1px solid #E3D6C5`, flush at the section's own bottom padding edge — 88px below the button, zero gap after. Ours currently sits with roughly equal gaps on both sides of the line (a `_divider` block mid-flow, followed by the section's own trailing padding) — "floating," matching the complaint. Diagnosed the exact same mechanism just fixed for item 4 below (divider's own `padding-block-start` should absorb the lead-in gap; the section's own `padding-block-end` should drop to 0 so the line sits flush and the next section's own top padding supplies the visual gap) — not applied here, reported for confirmation first as asked.

**3. Hero H1→body gap — 40px → 22px, matching source exactly.** Design: eyebrow-bottom-margin 22px, H1 margin 0, body top-margin 22px — both transitions are 22px. Ours used a shared section `gap: 24` plus each block's own `padding-block-start` (heading 8, body 16), compounding to 32px and 40px respectively. Reduced the shared `gap` to 22 and zeroed both blocks' own `padding-block-start` — hits 22px on both transitions (only H1→body was asked; the eyebrow→H1 fix is an accurate, harmless side effect of the same shared value, not separately requested). `button`'s own block type doesn't support `padding-block-start` (checked, not assumed) — no way to compensate the body→cta transition, which was untouched anyway; it moves from 24px to 22px as an incidental, unrequested, cosmetically negligible side effect. Verified live: `h1ToBody: 22`.

**4. Divider between Wholesale Benefits and Traceable — added.** Confirmed in source: Benefits' own section carries `border-bottom:1px solid #E3D6C5`; Traceable has no matching top border of its own (same `#F4F1EA` background continues, so the line is the only seam). Added a `_divider` block (same per-side-border-simulation technique as every other Wholesale divider this build) as the last block in `wholesale_benefits`, dropped the section's own `padding-block-end` from 64 to 0 so the line sits flush at the section boundary (same fix shape as item 2, applied here since this one wasn't held back). First pass used a computed padding value (68px) that didn't land — measured live rather than trusted the arithmetic, found the real gap was 105px against an 80px target, adjusted the one number (68→43) and re-verified: `gapCardToLine: 80`, divider flush with the section's own bottom edge exactly.

**5. Packaging Formats title weight — the ask contradicted the source; flagged rather than followed.** Checked the literal source styles for all four title/description pairs directly: neither carries a `font-weight` declaration anywhere — both default to 400, consistent with DESIGN-TOKENS.md §2's own finding that 28 of 34 body-scale paragraphs in the whole design carry no weight override. The visual distinction the design actually uses is **size + colour**: title 17px `#131A3E` (navy), description 15px `#4A5478` (grey) — a distinction our build wasn't reproducing at all, since both lines were one combined RTE block forced to a single uniform size (§14's documented compromise, flagged then as pending this exact fix). Split each `spec_N` block into a small two-child group (title/description, 4px gap between, 22px between pairs — matching source exactly) with independent sizing instead of adding a bold weight that isn't in the design. Verified live: title 17px/`rgb(19,26,62)`/weight 400, description 15px/`rgb(74,84,120)`/weight 400, 4px gap — matches source on every measured value, including weight being unchanged from normal.

**6 & 7. Product page — space above images, space before the trust row.** Confirmed live: the `product-information` section (`main`) had `padding-block-start: 0, padding-block-end: 0` — literally none, on both ends. The design reaches its own gap via a breadcrumb bar (`Home / Shop / {product}`, 28px padding) that this build doesn't have and wasn't asked to add — approximated the resulting *total* gap instead of replicating the breadcrumb structure, using `64px` on both ends to match this build's own established section rhythm (Wholesale, trust-row itself, and others all use 64 for a comparable role) rather than inventing a new value. Verified live: clear gap above the image gallery now visible below the header; 188px between the last image and the trust-row's "Sustainably sourced" heading (icon + heading + section padding all included in that figure) — no longer flush.

**8. "You may also like" — eyebrow and heading rebuilt; cards only partially checkable.** Replaced the single `type_preset: "h4"` (24px, no eyebrow) heading with the source's actual pairing — eyebrow "Goes well with" (`--text-3-size`, `eyebrow` tracking, matching every other eyebrow this build) + heading "From the same shelf" at `--display-4-size` (32px — this is a full-width single-column section intro, same role as "What our customers say" elsewhere on this same page, confirmed against §16's role split, not assumed). Verified live: eyebrow 12px/tracked/gold, heading 32px/navy.
   - **Star ratings**: already present — a `review` block was already in the card composition (added earlier, §12/§14's build), confirmed still in `block_order`.
   - **Category eyebrow on each card**: attempted (`{{ closest.product.type }}` in a `text` block, matching the design's `category` prop) — **rejected by Shopify's own template validator**: `Dynamic source 'closest.product.type' is invalid.` (confirmed via the actual sync error, not assumed from a schema read). `closest.product.title`/`.description` are valid dynamic sources elsewhere in this build; `.type` isn't in whatever allowlist the generic `text` block's dynamic-source validation uses. Reverted rather than leave a broken sync. Native product-title/price/review blocks bind their own product context internally and aren't subject to this restriction — the category label specifically needs a different mechanism (a native block type this composition doesn't have, or Horizon's own product-card component if one exists with this capability) — not solved this pass.
   - **Add-to-cart / quick-add**: **couldn't verify live.** `product-recommendations` loads its actual product data asynchronously via `data-url="/recommendations/products?limit=4"` (`data-recommendations-performed="false"` at load) — in this dev/preview context, no recommended products ever populated the rail (checked the section's own rendered HTML directly; the block scaffold is there, no card markup inside it), so there was nothing to hover or inspect. `settings.quick_add` is Horizon's own global toggle, defaulting `true` and not overridden anywhere in `settings_data.json` (checked directly) — quick-add should be active site-wide including here, but that's inference from the setting, not confirmation from a rendered card. Needs a real storefront visit (or products with actual order/view history) to close out.
   - **Found in passing, fixed**: `product-recommendations.liquid`'s own `contrast-override` call had the identical missing-`border_color` bug as item 9 below (confirmed via the section's own generated inline style: `--color-border: var(--palette-darkest)`) — fixed the same way, same one-line addition. Directly relevant here since it's this same card's own border.

**9. Shop All product tiles — dark stroke removed, root cause was the already-flagged §1 finding.** This *was* §1's "native product-card borders are navy, not gold" gap, left open pending a decision at the time — `main-collection.liquid`'s `contrast-override` call passes no `border_color`, so the smart-contrast branch falls back to `--palette-darkest` (navy) instead of reading the global gold `--color-border` override at all. Fixed with the one-line addition `border_color: settings.color_palette.color2` (already `#E3D6C5`, matching `--color-border`, per §1's own record of that setting) — no `--palette-darkest` repointing, no risk to its other smart-contrast consumers, exactly the fix §1 already scoped out, just not written until asked. Verified live: `rgb(227, 214, 197)` on the actual card frame (a first query grabbed an unrelated `product-card`-classed text element and returned the wrong colour — re-verified against the actual bordered element specifically, not the first match).
   - **Same bug confirmed systemic, not fixed everywhere.** Grepped every `render 'contrast-override'` call site missing an explicit `border_color` alongside a `background_color` reference: **over 40 Horizon section/block/snippet files** carry the same latent pattern. Each is only live-wrong if that specific instance actually sets a `background_color` in our JSON (most don't, so most are inert) — but this wasn't a two-instance bug, it's a general Horizon behaviour. Only the two instances directly implicated by this review (`main-collection.liquid`, `product-recommendations.liquid`) were fixed. The full list is a real, separate follow-up worth its own pass, not absorbed into this one.

**10. Shop All product images — inconsistent heights fixed, cropped to a consistent 4:3.** `image_ratio: "adapt"` (natural per-image ratio — confirmed the cause: every product photo has its own source dimensions, so "adapt" produced a different tile height per product) swapped for `"square"`, then re-pointed via a scoped CSS override (`[data-image-ratio="square"] { --gallery-aspect-ratio: 4/3; }`) to the design's actual 4:3 (confirmed against both `ProductCard.jsx`'s literal `aspectRatio:'4/3'` and DESIGN-TOKENS.md §3's own table). `card-gallery.liquid`'s `image_ratio` is a hard `case`/`when` over exactly four values with no passthrough for a fifth (unlike `font_size`) — "square" was confirmed unused anywhere else in the build before repurposing it, same technique as `max-width: "wide"` / `letter-spacing: "eyebrow"`, just via a data-attribute selector instead of a named CSS variable since this mechanism doesn't interpolate the raw setting at all. Scoped to Shop All only (`collection.all.json`) as asked — `collection.json` (Tea/Condiments/Pantry) has the identical `"adapt"` setting and wasn't touched, since it wasn't asked about; flagged here as the same bug, not yet fixed there.

**11. Shop All filter row — reported, not built, per instruction.** Confirmed from source: filters are `All`, `Tea`, `Sauces & Chutneys`, `Pantry & Gifts` — pill buttons (`padding:9px 18px;border-radius:999px;font-size:14px`, active = navy fill/cream text/navy border, inactive = transparent/navy text/gold border), left-aligned as a group, with a right-aligned `"{N} products"` count, the whole row bottom-bordered. The design's own mock implementation is **client-side filtering within one page** (`filter: s.filter` state, `PRODUCTS.filter(p => p.cat === filter)`) — not navigation between separate collection pages. That's the key store-side question before building: Shopify's native equivalent of "one page, filtered by category" is faceted filtering by **product tag** (every product tagged `Tea`/`Sauces & Chutneys`/`Pantry & Gifts`), using Horizon's native `filters` block (already used on `collection.json`, confirmed via its `"type": "filters"` block) — restyled to match the pill look, which Horizon doesn't produce natively. The alternative — three separate real collections, pills linking out to each — is simpler to build but changes the page's actual behaviour from the design's single-page filter to multi-page navigation, and still needs the collections to exist and be populated (CLAUDE.md's Open Questions already flags this exact gap: the footer's Tea/Sauces/Pantry links are still placeholders pointing at `/collections/all`, pending real handles from the user). Needs a decision on which of those two directions, plus (for the tag route) confirmation that every product actually carries one of the three tags today — not something checkable without Admin access. Nothing built for this item.

**Verified together after every fix**: theme dev synced clean throughout except the one rejected dynamic-source attempt (caught immediately via the sync error, reverted); `shopify theme check --fail-level=error` clean at the end — 369 files, no offenses.

## 20. Shop All filter row — built, and one real store-side blocker found (2026-08-20)

**Answers this session's item 11 report closed on.** All three collections confirmed automated on real product tags (`tea` 34, `condiments` 14, `pantry` 4 — 52 total, matching the store). Approach approved as a new custom block, not a fork of `filters.liquid` — tracked below as a fork-to-watch, same footing as `collection.all.json`.

**`sections/main-collection.liquid` — new tracked core-file edit, signed off before writing.** That section has no open, merchant-addable block slot — `filters` and `_product-card` are both fixed `content_for 'block'` calls hardcoded in the file, confirmed by reading it directly, not assumed from the JSON shape. Inserting the new block required one additive `content_for 'block', type: 'lambruk-tag-filter', id: 'lambruk-tag-filter', ...` call placed immediately after the existing `filters` call — nothing else in the file touched. Add to §6's upgrade-tracked table: a Horizon update that restructures this section's block calls could silently drop this line; check for it the same way as the other tracked edits after any Horizon update.

**`blocks/lambruk-tag-filter.liquid` — built.** Self-contained: its own `facets-form-component`/`facet-inputs-component` wiring (the real Horizon filtering custom elements from `assets/facets.js`, not a reimplementation), radio inputs sharing one `name` per tag value for true single-select (matching the design's mutually-exclusive pills, unlike Horizon's native multi-select checkbox filters). Three settings — `tea_label`/`condiments_label`/`pantry_label` — map tag value to display label, matched case-insensitively on both sides per instruction (`collection.all_tags` confirmed live the real casing is inconsistent: `Condiments`, `pantry`, `tea` — capitalisation genuinely differs tag to tag, so the downcase-both-sides instruction wasn't precautionary, it was already true).

**Three bugs found and fixed while wiring it up — two from patterns already established this session, one genuinely new:**
- **`{% stylesheet %}` rejects Liquid variables outright** (`StaticStylesheetAndJavascriptTags`, confirmed via the actual theme-check error) — the section-scoped rule that hides the native filters block (`#shopify-section-{{ section.id }} ...`) needed `{% style %}` instead, which does allow per-render Liquid. Static pill styling stayed in `{% stylesheet %}`.
- **The outer block div, once again, needed explicit sizing to fill its container** — the recurring class of bug from CLAUDE.md's own standing note, but a new manifestation of it: `width: 100%` alone did nothing here, because the parent (`.collection-wrapper.grid`) is a CSS **grid**, not the flex layouts every previous instance of this bug lived in. A grid item's `width: 100%` resolves against its own (shrink-to-fit) track, not the grid's overall width — circular, no-op. Confirmed live: the row measured 56px wide against a 1200px container even with `width: 100%` set. Fixed by reading how the native `filters` block and `product-grid.liquid` solve the identical problem (`grid-column: var(--grid-column--desktop)`, with `--grid-column--desktop` set to the pre-defined `var(--centered)` custom property) and reusing that exact mechanism instead of inventing a new one.
- **`facet-clear-component`'s `clearFilter()` has an undocumented dependency on an ancestor `<details>` — found live, not in any doc.** The "All" pill was originally built on Horizon's own clear-filter mechanism (`facet-clear-component`, unchecks every input in its `facet-inputs-component` and calls `facetsForm.updateFilters()`), the same pattern `list-filter.liquid` uses at the end of its own accordion. Once tag filtering was enabled and there were real tag pills to test against, clicking "All" visually cleared the checked pills but never re-filtered — the count and product grid both stayed on the previous tag, confirmed by checking twice with a delay, not a one-off render race. Read `assets/facets.js`'s actual `clearFilter()` source rather than guessed: it unchecks inputs, *then* looks up `event.target.closest('details')`, and returns early — before ever calling `updateFilters()` — if that lookup doesn't find a `facet-status-component` inside. Every native consumer of this mechanism sits inside `list-filter.liquid`'s `<details class="facets__panel">` accordion; this block has no accordion by design (the pills are always visible), so the lookup always failed silently. First fix attempt wrapped the input group in an inert `<details open>` + hidden status component to satisfy the lookup — `display: contents` didn't fully dissolve `<details>` in practice (its own box and default marker persisted, confirmed live, breaking the row into a stacked layout), so reverted that too. Final fix: "All" is a fourth radio in the same input group, empty value, going through `FacetInputsComponent.updateFilters()` — the same `on:change` path the three tag pills already use, which has no `<details>` dependency at all. Checked the one real risk in this approach before committing to it — that an empty `filter.p.tag=` value might filter for products with a literal empty tag instead of clearing the filter — live, not assumed: confirmed it correctly returns all 52 products.

**The interactive verification — clicking each pill, confirming in-place filtering, the count updating, active state, "All" clearing back to 52 — completed in full, after Search & Discovery's tag filter was enabled (Source: Tags, not "Product tag" as originally guessed — the actual setting name, worth noting since it doesn't match the obvious search term). Sequence tested: Tea → All → Sauces & Chutneys → Pantry & Gifts, including one direct tag-to-tag transition (Sauces & Chutneys → Pantry & Gifts, not routed through All), each read twice — once immediately, once after a delay — after two checks returned stale data before the DOM had actually finished updating (this session's known browser-tool timing gap, not a real bug both times; the third case, "All", was real and is the bug described above).**

| Pill clicked | Count | Product grid | URL (via `pushState`, confirmed no reload) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tea | 34 items | Tea products only | `?filter.p.tag=tea` |
| All | 52 items | Full unfiltered set | `?filter.p.tag=` |
| Sauces & Chutneys | 14 items | Condiments products only | `?filter.p.tag=Condiments` |
| Pantry & Gifts (direct from Sauces & Chutneys) | 4 items | Pantry products only | `?filter.p.tag=pantry` |

Every transition's underlying `sectionRenderer` fetch confirmed directly in the network log (`GET .../collections/all?filter.p.tag=...&section_id=template--...__main → 200 OK`) — genuine AJAX section re-render, not inferred from the visible result. Native filters block's own controls (sort, grid density) confirmed still present in the DOM and still hidden, settings untouched. `shopify theme check --fail-level=error` clean throughout — 370 files, no offenses.

**Note for the record, not a theme concern:** Horizon is unpublished; Flux is still live, and the live collection pages currently have no filter UI at all — this entire change is invisible to real customers until go-live.

Applied the same fix shape already proven for item 4 (§19): divider `padding-block-start` set to `66` (with the shared section `gap` now `22` post-item-3, `22 + 66 = 88`, matching the design's exact button-to-line measurement) and the section's own `padding-block-end` dropped to `0` so the line sits flush at the boundary. Verified live: `gapCtaToLine: 88`, and `lineBottom === sectionBottom === nextSectionTop` (all `521.4`) — the line's bottom edge, the hero section's own bottom edge, and the following section's top edge are the identical pixel, confirming genuinely flush, not just visually close.

The border fix (§19 item 9) lives in `sections/main-collection.liquid` itself, not in either template's JSON, so it was never template-scoped in the first place — checked live on `/collections/tea`, `/collections/condiments`, `/collections/pantry` and all three already compute `rgb(227, 214, 197)` (`#E3D6C5`) on the card border, no further change needed or made. Changed to `"square"`, the same repurposed value from §19 (the global `[data-image-ratio="square"]` CSS rule in `lambruk-tokens.css` isn't template-scoped either, so no new CSS was needed) — reconfirmed on all three collections afterward, `275` uniformly.

**Found in passing, worth flagging even though not asked about: the new tag-filter pill row (§20) now also renders on Tea/Condiments/Pantry, not just Shop All.** `sections/main-collection.liquid`'s edit 2 (the `content_for 'block', type: 'lambruk-tag-filter'` call) is section-level, not template-scoped, so every page using this section gets the block — confirmed live on `/collections/tea`, which now shows its own "All" / "Tea" pills (only the tags present among that collection's own products, since `collection.filters` is naturally scoped per-collection). Not obviously wrong — arguably a reasonable, even welcome, side effect — but it wasn't the ask (§20 was specifically "Shop All filter row") and nobody signed off on it appearing three more places. ## 22. Filter row restricted to collections with 2+ known tags — self-managing, not a page check (2026-08-21)

**Ask**: §21 flagged the pill row appearing on Tea/Condiments/Pantry as an unintended side effect of the section-level insertion. Instruction back: don't hardcode a Shop All check — gate rendering on the tag filter genuinely having 2 or more of the three known values (`tea`/`condiments`/`pantry`), so the row is self-managing: absent wherever a collection only spans one category, present automatically if the client ever creates another multi-category collection sharing this section.

**Fix**, `blocks/lambruk-tag-filter.liquid`: the block's existing Liquid preamble already scanned `collection.filters` for the tag-type filter; extended it to count how many of its values match the three known tags (`known_tag_value_count`), then wrapped everything from the `<script src="facets.js">` tag through the block's own `{% style %}` rule (the one that hides Horizon's native filters block for this section instance) in `{% if known_tag_value_count >= 2 %}`. The `{% stylesheet %}` block (static pill CSS) stays outside the conditional, since it's compiled once per block type, not per render, and does nothing if the markup isn't there.

Consequence worth being explicit about: on Tea/Condiments/Pantry, since the pill row's own `{% style %}` rule no longer renders either, Horizon's *native* filters block (sort dropdown, grid-density toggle) reappears there instead of being hidden with nothing in its place — the natural fallback, not a separate decision.

**Verified live**, all four collection pages:

| Page | Pill row | Native filters block |
|---|---|---|
| `/collections/all` | present — All / Sauces & Chutneys / Pantry & Gifts / Tea | hidden (`display: none`) |
| `/collections/tea` | absent | visible (`display: block`) |
| `/collections/condiments` | absent | visible |
| `/collections/pantry` | absent | visible |

On Shop All, also re-confirmed the filtering itself still works post-edit (the conditional only wraps rendering, doesn't touch the JS): clicking "Tea" updated the grid to 34 items with the correct products and `checked` state; clicking "All" returned to 52 items. (A first attempt at this check used a synthetic `dispatchEvent(new Event('change'))` from a script — it updated the URL via `history.pushState` but never triggered the actual section re-render fetch or count update. Read as a limitation of synthetic events against this component's listener, not a regression, and confirmed by re-testing with an actual click, which worked correctly in every respect — worth noting so a future synthetic-event test here isn't mistaken for a real bug.)

`shopify theme check --path .` clean after the edit — 370 files, no offenses.
