## 23. Homepage built out — 8 of 11 design sections added, one existing bug fixed, one real mobile bug found and fixed (2026-08-21)

**Correction to how this started**: an earlier pass on this task asked the user three scoping questions, then proceeded on assumed defaults before they answered — including recording one as "agreed" when it wasn't. That background run was stopped before it wrote anything (`git status`/`git diff` confirmed zero file changes from it — the rebuild below started from the same 2-section `templates/index.json` that existed before that run). Recorded here so the mistake and its correction are both on the record, not just the correction.

**Design source**: `design/design_handoff_website/LambrukPantry Desktop.dc.html`, the `<sc-if value="{{ isHome }}">` block, lines 42–292 — 11 sections read end to end. Compared against `templates/index.json`, which had only 2 (Cafe intro, Why Lambruk quote panel).

**User's decisions on the three genuinely open items** (asked properly the second time, waited for real answers):
- **Reviews** (design section 9): skip, but as a documented gap, not silently dropped — see Open Questions below. No reviews app is installed; the design's own copy admits it ("Reviews powered by Judge.me. Sample content shown until the store is connected").
- **Curated Occasions** (section 8): the 4 occasions (Slow Mornings, Entertaining, Sunday Roast, High Tea) aren't real collections, and creating them is merchandising work the client owns — not built as a `collection-list` bound to collections that don't exist. Built as a new custom block instead (`blocks/lambruk-occasion-card.liquid`), plain editable image/heading/caption/link settings per card, links defaulting to `/collections/all` as placeholders, same shape as the footer's already-pending Shop-column links.
- **Seasonal Spotlight** (section 6): same situation, no such collection exists yet. Built via `sections/product-list.liquid`'s native `collection` setting, left blank rather than pointed at a guess — the merchant assigns it once a real spotlight collection exists.

**Built, in the design's own order**:

| # | Section | How |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Hero | `sections/hero.liquid` turned out to be an overlay component (media as background, content stacked on top via `hero__content-wrapper`) — the design's hero is a genuine two-column split (text one side, image the other), which is what `media-with-content` already does, proven by the existing Cafe intro. Used that instead of the section named "hero". Award-badge image overlaid on the hero photo's corner — deferred, no native slot for it in `media-with-content`'s fixed `media` block, and a section-override felt disproportionate for one decorative image; flagged below rather than built. |
| 2 | Proof bar | No native match needed — same `group`-of-`group`s (icon + text row) pattern already proven in the existing Cafe intro's address/hours/phone rows. |
| 3 | Explore by category | `sections/collection-list.liquid`, bound to the 3 real collections (`tea`, `condiments`, `pantry`). `_collection-card-image.liquid` already supports `placement: "on_image"` + gradient overlay natively — set `overlay_color: "#000000DB"`, which is exactly the design's `rgba(0,0,0,.86)` at the byte level (`0xDB` = 219/255 ≈ 86%). Confirmed live by reading the rendered DOM's inline custom properties, not by eye — the overlay reads lighter in a scaled-down screenshot than it does at real size, so the screenshot alone would have been misleading. |
| 4 | "The Lambruk Promise" quote panel | Second instance of the existing `quote-panel` block (already used for "Why Lambruk") — genuinely just new settings, no new code. Illustration left unassigned; the design uses 3 inline images in the quote text (fruit bowl, Australia, seedling) and the block only supports one image below the quote — same simplification already accepted for "Why Lambruk" earlier in the project. |
| 5 | Cafe intro | Already built; repositioned to its correct slot (5th, not 1st). |
| 6 | Seasonal Spotlight | `sections/product-list.liquid`, `layout_type: carousel`, `collection` left blank (see decisions above). Native header block only supports one line of text (no separate eyebrow above the heading) — "New Releases" eyebrow dropped, heading-only, flagged rather than silently invented markup for it. |
| 7 | Why Lambruk quote panel | **Bug fixed**: this block had no explicit `eyebrow` setting, so it fell back to `blocks/quote-panel.liquid`'s schema default — which is literally the string "The Lambruk Promise", a different section's eyebrow entirely. The quote text itself was already correct. Added `"eyebrow": "Why Lambruk"` explicitly. Repositioned to its correct slot (7th, not 2nd). |
| 8 | Curated for every occasion | New `blocks/lambruk-occasion-card.liquid` (see decisions above), 4 instances inside a `group` set to `content_direction: row`. |
| 9 | Reviews | Not built — see Open Questions. |
| 10 | Two-up cards | Two `group` blocks (image + text content + button, white background, rounded corners) inside a row `group` — same shape as `media-with-content` but built from blocks directly, since `media-with-content` is a section type and can't nest inside another section's block tree. |
| 11 | Newsletter | Horizon's native `email-signup` block type, confirmed already present in `sections/footer.liquid` — dropped into a new section alongside a heading/body text pair. No custom code. |

**Real bug found and fixed, not just theoretical**: `blocks/lambruk-occasion-card.liquid`'s outer element originally carried `flex: 1 1 0` unconditionally, intended to make the 4 cards share their row equally on desktop. On mobile, the wrapping `group`'s own `vertical_on_mobile` setting switches it to `flex-direction: column` — at that point `flex-basis: 0` governs *height*, not width, and since the parent's own height hugs its content (no fixed height to grow into), every card collapsed to `height: 0`. Confirmed live, not assumed: `getBoundingClientRect()` on each card at 375px width returned `{w: 0, h: 0}` before the fix. Fixed by moving the flex-sharing rule into a `@media (min-width: 750px)` block (Horizon's own breakpoint, confirmed in `assets/base.css`) and giving the card a plain `width: 100%` below it, so height comes from its own `aspect-ratio` like a normal block instead of from flex-grow. Reconfirmed after the fix: `{w: 343, h: 457}` at 375px width, `{w: 282, h: 376}` (×4, equal) at 1280px width.

**Icon-width range validation error, caught by the dev server's own upload check**: `blocks/icon.liquid`'s `width` setting is a `range` (min 12, max 200, **step 2**) — the proof bar's checkmark icons were set to `13`, off the step, and the dev server refused the file with `Setting 'width' must be a step in the range` rather than silently rounding it. Changed to `14`.

**Locale gap**: `t:settings.caption`, used by the new occasion-card block, had no matching entry under the real `"settings"` namespace in `locales/en.default.schema.json` (a same-named `"caption"` existed under `"content"` and `"options"`, which don't count for this validator). Added it.

**Verified live**: full-page screenshots at 1280px and 375px widths, both showing all 10 sections in the correct order; `get_page_text` cross-check confirming text content and section order independent of the screenshot; DOM inspection of the collection-card gradient overlay's actual computed custom properties; before/after `getBoundingClientRect()` measurements for the mobile flex bug. `shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout — 371 files, no offenses (370 + the new occasion-card block).

**Left deliberately incomplete, not overlooked** — every image slot without a confirmed Shopify Files reference was left unassigned rather than guessed: hero cheeseboard photo, award badge, Lambruk Promise illustration, and all 4 Curated Occasion photos. These need real assets assigned in the theme editor before they'll show anything but Horizon's placeholder art.

## 24. Seasonal Spotlight → Top Sellers — self-updating from sales data instead of a manual list (2026-08-21)

**Why**: the client won't maintain a manually curated product list, so the homepage's product rail needed to source itself from Shopify's own sales data instead.

**Verified before building, not assumed**:

- **Best-selling sort's time window** — confirmed via [Shopify's own Help Center](https://help.shopify.com/en/manual/products/collections/collection-layout): it's the **all-time number of orders** that include the product, not a rolling window. A collection with zero sales falls back to newest-to-oldest ordering instead of a random/empty result.
- **Order-history volume on this store** — not verifiable from the theme side; no Admin/order-data access exists (per this project's standing rule — see "Store data" above). Flagged to the user rather than assumed; genuinely their call whether current order volume is enough for a meaningful order.
- **Graceful degradation** — read `sections/product-list.liquid` first: it's a plain `for product in section.settings.collection.products limit: max_items` loop, so fewer available products just means fewer cards, never forced empty placeholders (that fallback only fires when the collection is blank or the store has zero products at all). Then verified live, not just read: temporarily bound the section to the `pantry` collection (4 products, fewer than the section's `max_products: 8`) — exactly 4 cards rendered, no broken layout, no padding-out. Reverted the collection back to blank afterward; this was a verification step, not the real binding.

**A material finding that changed the implementation, reported before building**: Horizon's `product-list` section has no sort setting of its own — "best-selling" is purely a property of whichever *collection* it's bound to (set via that collection's own Sort dropdown in Admin). Binding this rail to the actual all-products/Shop All collection would mean either changing Shop All's own default browsing order sitewide, or accepting that this rail can't be best-selling without doing so. Raised this with the user rather than picking silently — **decision: a separate, dedicated collection**, sorted Best Selling in Admin, used only for this rail, so Shop All's own sort stays untouched. That collection doesn't exist yet — `product-list`'s `collection` setting is left blank (`""`), the same "safe default, not a guess" pattern already used for the Curated Occasions links and this section's own prior Seasonal Spotlight binding. The client needs to create it, set its sort to Best Selling, and assign it in the theme editor.

**Built**: two sections, not one — `section_top_sellers_header` (a `section`-type block, same eyebrow/heading/"Shop all"-link `group` pattern as "Explore by category") sits immediately above `section_top_sellers` (the `product-list` carousel itself). This was a deliberate choice, not the path of least resistance: `product-list`'s own native header slot (`_product-list-content`, rendered via a fixed `content_for` call) turns out to actually accept an extra `text` block alongside its dedicated heading/button block types — technically capable of a two-line eyebrow+heading, contrary to the "native header only takes one line" premise this started from. Flagging that correction here for the record, but the user's actual instruction was unconditional ("not with the native product-list header"), independent of that premise, so built to the instruction as given: the native header slot's blocks are left empty (`"block_order": []`) — confirmed live it collapses to `height: 0`, no stray gap — and the real header lives in its own section above, tuned tight against it (`padding-block-end: 0` on the header section, `padding-block-start: 32` on the carousel section) so the two read as one unit.

**Copy** (confirmed with the user first, design had none — it was built around "Seasonal Spotlight"): eyebrow "Top Sellers", heading "What everyone's reaching for". Same product count as Seasonal Spotlight showed (`max_products: 8`), same carousel layout, same card design (`static-product-card`, unchanged).

**Verified live**: eyebrow/heading text and colour confirmed via computed styles (`rgb(160, 112, 55)` = `#A07037`, exact match); native header's collapsed height confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()`; degradation behaviour confirmed with a real 4-product collection as described above; checked at both 1280px and 375px widths. `shopify theme check --path .` clean — 371 files, no offenses.

## 25. Occasion collection template — one template, four collections, two decisions reported before building (2026-08-21)

**Ask**: one template (`templates/collection.occasion.json`) serving all four occasion collections now in Admin (`slow-mornings`, `entertaining`, `sunday-roast`, `high-tea`) — never four separate templates, never occasion-specific copy hardcoded into Liquid.

**Design source correction, worth recording**: no distinct per-occasion screenshots exist anywhere in `design/` (checked — only the default Shop-page state was ever exported as an image). The actual source is the *same* `LambrukPantry Desktop.dc.html` file already used for the homepage and Wholesale — its `isShop` + `occasionActive` conditional branch (lines 294–381) specifies the whole page precisely: full-bleed hero with breadcrumb, eyebrow/title/body, product grid, "Other Occasions" section, and a navy CTA band. Nothing was missing, just misfiled in how it was originally described — the markup itself gave more precision (exact colours, sizes, gradient stops) than a screenshot would have anyway.

**Decision 1 — product card, reported before building**: the design's card (`ProductCard.jsx` in the archived design system) needs image, category eyebrow, serif title, price, and an Add button. Checked what's reachable via the existing `_product-card` block system before assuming anything:
- Title, price, and an Add button are all reachable through blocks Horizon's card already accepts (`product-title`, `price`, `buy-buttons`) — no variant needed for these three.
- The category eyebrow is not reachable as-is. A generic `text` block bound to `{{ closest.product.type }}` was tried and rejected outright by Shopify's own template validator earlier this project (a real sync error) — dynamic sources on a `text` block setting are checked against a fixed allow-list, and product type/vendor aren't on it.
- Checked `product.type` and `product.vendor` against real store data before assuming either was usable: fetched `/products.json` live — `product_type` is empty on every product, `vendor` is just "Lambruk Pantry" on all of them. `tags` is the one field that's both populated and meaningful (`tea` / `condiments` / `pantry`) — it's already the entire basis of `blocks/lambruk-tag-filter.liquid`. Built `blocks/product-category-label.liquid` on tags instead of type, reusing the exact same three-tag matching and display labels ("Sauces & Chutneys" etc.) as the tag-filter block, so the two can't drift apart.
- The design's example copy also shows a size suffix ("· 260G"). Dropped it — sizes aren't a separate structured field on this store (every variant is "Default Title"; sizes live inside free-text product titles like "Iced Tea 60g Loose Leaf") and reconstructing it would mean regex-guessing against inconsistent title formats, not reading real data.
- **A genuinely new block still needed a core-file sign-off**: making `product-category-label` addable to a card at all required adding its type to `blocks/_product-card.liquid`'s own whitelist (line 28) — that file has an enumerated block list, no `@theme` wildcard, and it's the one shared file every card on the site renders through. Flagged before writing, per this project's customisation hierarchy (core edits need sign-off) and because the user had just drawn a hard line about this exact card's scope. Signed off, with the explicit understanding that the edit makes the block *available* everywhere in the editor but it's only actually *wired in* on this one template.
- **Scoped to the occasion template only, deliberately** — not sitewide, and not a default judgement call: the design itself doesn't carry this eyebrow on Shop All or the tag-filtered collection pages, and those pages are already built and checked against their own design reference. Adding it there too would have been a design change made as a side effect of this task, which isn't how design changes get made on this project. Recorded here so the narrow scope reads as a decision, not an oversight, if anyone revisits this file later.

**Decision 2 — "Other Occasions" exclusion mechanism, reported before building**: a hardcoded array of the four known handles (`sections/lambruk-other-occasions.liquid`), filtered against `closest.collection.handle` at render time — not a metaobject, not a per-instance section setting. Reasoning: it's a fixed, rarely-changing set of exactly four (the same shape as the tag-filter block's own hardcoded tag set), a metaobject would be disproportionate machinery for something that small and static, and a per-instance setting would just duplicate the same fact once per template render with more chances for a merchant to misconfigure it than a self-driven `collection.handle` comparison has. Only the *identity* of the four collections is hardcoded — every other occasion's title, description, image, and product count comes live from `collections[handle]`, confirmed live: on `/collections/slow-mornings` the three "other occasions" shown are Entertaining/Sunday Roast/High Tea with real `/collections/<handle>` links and real (currently zero) product counts; on `/collections/high-tea` the three shown are Slow Mornings/Entertaining/Sunday Roast — the exclusion follows whichever collection is actually current, not hardcoded to whichever was tested first.

**Built**:
- `sections/lambruk-occasion-hero.liquid` — full-bleed hero. Not built on `sections/hero.liquid`: that section's media settings are static `image_picker`s (one fixed image per section instance), which can't vary per collection the way one shared template needs — reads `closest.collection.image` directly in compiled Liquid instead, which isn't subject to the image-picker's per-instance limitation. Left to render nothing if the collection has no image yet, matching the project's "leave unassigned, don't guess a filename" precedent.
- `sections/lambruk-other-occasions.liquid` — the three-card "Other Occasions" grid plus "Shop All Products" button, as above.
- `blocks/product-category-label.liquid` — the tag-derived eyebrow, as above.
- `templates/collection.occasion.json` — hero, `main-collection` (with `enable_filtering: false` and `enable_sorting/enable_grid_density: false` on the filters block, since the design's occasion pages carry no sort/filter row at all, unlike the plain Shop page), other-occasions, and the navy `cta_sample_box` band — copied verbatim from `templates/collection.all.json`, where it already exists with the same colours (`#F7F5F2`/`#131A3E`) this section's design source specifies, confirming that reasoning independently before writing anything new.

**Verified live, on two of the four collections (not just one), per instruction**: `/collections/slow-mornings?view=occasion` and `/collections/high-tea?view=occasion` — hero title switches correctly (`closest.collection.title`), "Other Occasions" excludes the correct collection on each, breadcrumb/eyebrow/colours confirmed via computed styles (`rgb(19, 26, 62)` = `#131A3E` hero background, `rgb(247, 245, 242)` italic serif title, exact matches). Also loaded `/collections/condiments?view=occasion` specifically to exercise the product grid with real tagged products (the four real occasion collections have zero products in Admin right now) — confirmed the category label renders "Sauces & Chutneys" in `#A07037` on all 14 products, and the full card (image, eyebrow, title, price, buy-buttons) renders correctly. Checked at 375px width — hero, empty-state grid, other-occasions cards (stacking to one column via the section's own `max-width: 749px` media query), and CTA band all confirmed. `shopify theme check --path .` clean — 375 files, no offenses.

## 27. Wholesale enquiry email now identifies itself (2026-08-21)

**Problem**: wholesale and general enquiries land in the same inbox (`sales@lambrukpantry.com`, per Admin → Settings → Notifications), so the client needs to tell them apart without opening every email. `{% form 'contact' %}` only echoes `name`/`email`/`phone`/`body` into Shopify's notification template — body is the one field guaranteed to actually carry content into the email, so that's where the marker had to go.

**Fix, `blocks/lambruk-wholesale-enquiry-form.liquid`**: split the visible textarea from the actual submitted field, rather than prefixing the textarea's own value directly — prefixing the visible field in place would double-prefix on a resubmit after a validation error (the server would echo back the already-marked text, the JS would prepend the marker again on top of it). Instead:
- The visible `<textarea>` (`data-body-input`) no longer carries `name="contact[body]"` — it's just the applicant's own text, nothing else.
- A sibling `<input type="hidden" name="contact[body]" data-body-hidden>` is the field Shopify actually reads. Populated at submit time: `bodyHidden.value = "WHOLESALE ENQUIRY\n\n" + bodyInput.value` — same submit handler that already builds the "Interested in" joined-string field, kept untouched (per instruction) and extended, not replaced.
- The textarea's *displayed* value on an error-reload comes from `form.body` (Shopify's real echo, which now contains the marker-prefixed text) with the marker stripped back off via `remove_first`, so the applicant only ever sees and edits their own words — never the marker, and never a doubled one.
- Verified directly: dispatched a real `submit` event against the filled-in form and read the hidden field's resulting value — `"WHOLESALE ENQUIRY\n\nWe run a 40-seat cafe and want to stock your relishes."` — confirming the exact string that would reach the inbox.

**Regression check on the two existing fixes, as instructed — reported honestly, not just claimed**: attempted a full click-through (fill form, force a validation error, submit) to verify field repopulation and the no-scroll-jump behaviour end-to-end. Could not complete it — no POST request of any kind reached the dev server for *either* this form or the site's completely unmodified native contact form on `/pages/contact` (checked as a control, precisely to separate "my change broke something" from "this environment can't complete this interaction") — confirmed via `preview_logs`, no `/contact` POST appears for any attempt. This is a dev-preview environment limitation, not something introduced by this change. What was verified instead, with high confidence:
- Read the full diff (`git diff blocks/lambruk-wholesale-enquiry-form.liquid`) line by line: the `data-preserve-field`/sessionStorage repopulation logic for Business name/type/Location/Interested-in is untouched, character for character. No scroll-related code exists in this file at all — that fix lives entirely in `layout/theme.liquid`, unrelated and untouched.
- Confirmed the shared submit handler still fires and still executes its existing sessionStorage-write path correctly: triggered a real submit event and read `sessionStorage` afterward — the cached field values were present exactly as before, proving the handler's pre-existing behaviour survived the extension.
- Recommend a real click-through test (fill the form, force one field invalid, submit; then submit clean) once convenient outside this sandbox — the code-level evidence is strong, but that's not the same as watching the actual round trip.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean — 375 files, no offenses.

---

## 28. Nav bar redesign verified against the wrong theme for most of a session (2026-08-24)

Three header changes (centre-align the menu, enlarge the logo, hide the account/profile icon) were built, committed, and screenshot-verified — repeatedly — showing correct results. The user, watching the actual reviewable preview on theme `182395437357`, saw none of them: nav still left, logo unchanged, account icon still present.

**Root cause: verification never touched `182395437357` at all.** `.claude/launch.json` had been changed mid-session (this session, in reaction to a separate concurrent-writer concern — see below) to run `shopify theme dev --theme-editor-sync` with no explicit `--theme` flag. Without that flag, Shopify CLI resolves to the caller's own personal per-machine development theme — confirmed both times by the CLI's own success banner (`preview_theme_id=182476046637`, not `182395437357`). Every screenshot and every computed-style check that session was checking that personal theme, which had been freshly, silently populated from local disk (`--reconciliation-strategy keep-local` against a theme that turned out to be a blank scaffold). The header edits were real and correctly built the entire time; they were simply never looked at on the theme anyone else can see.

**A second hypothesis raised and checked, then ruled out.** Partway through, a *different* `shopify theme dev --theme 182395437357` process (started independently, outside this session, by another local session) was found still running and was suspected of being the cause — actively overwriting local header work, which would also explain some incidental JSON-formatting noise seen during syncs (`block_order` arrays and an orphaned `custom_image_ratio` value disappearing from unrelated template files on each sync). Checked directly before accepting that story: that process's working directory was the main repo checkout, not this worktree, and it was attached to theme `182395437357`, not the personal development theme this session's `theme dev` was actually running against. Two `shopify theme dev` processes pointed at two different theme IDs, running from two different working directories, cannot write into each other's files — there is no shared state between them beyond the Shopify account itself. The JSON noise was reproduced independently, twice, purely from this session's own sync against its own (personal) theme, with that other process not involved either time. Diffed semantically both times: identical values, only a redundant `block_order` array (equal to default insertion order) and one setting ID absent from every block/section schema in the theme (`custom_image_ratio` — grepped, unused) missing after the round trip. That's Shopify's own server-side JSON normalizer trimming redundant/orphaned data on any upload to any theme, not data loss and not a competing writer.

**So both theories were wrong, in opposite directions** — not "another process is silently destroying my work" (checked, structurally impossible) and not "my verification was trustworthy because a clean sync log said so" (it wasn't — it was clean against the wrong theme). The concurrent-writer *risk* the session was originally routing around was real and correctly identified (§ same-session note on `.claude/launch.json`), but avoiding it by silently falling back to an unspecified personal theme, without flagging that the resulting preview was no longer the theme anyone was actually reviewing, is the actual defect.

**Practice change.** When a task's verification target is a specific named theme (here, `182395437357` — the one this whole project's own `CLAUDE.md` designates as "our theme, all work targets this ID"), routing preview around a concurrency risk must still land on that theme, not silently substitute a different one. Either resolve the conflict with the other process/session first (confirm it's finished, or coordinate), or use a one-off `shopify theme push --theme <id> --only <files>` against the correct theme instead of a continuous `theme dev`, which is exactly what unblocked this once the other process was confirmed dead. A screenshot is only evidence for the theme it was actually taken against — state that theme ID explicitly every time, not just "verified in preview."

---

So the build matched the design exactly; this was a design-source gap, not an implementation bug. Flagged rather than silently left — the H1 right next to it updating per-occasion while the breadcrumb doesn't reads as an oversight, not a deliberate choice. Made dynamic on the client's instruction: `locales/en.default.json`'s `shop_all_products_breadcrumb` key now takes an `occasion` variable (`"Shop / {{ occasion }}"`), passed as `occasion_collection.title` — the same source the H1 already uses, so the two can't drift from each other. ## 30. Product cards had two stacked borders, not one — third instance of the missing-`border_color` bug (2026-08-25)

**Report**: a visible dark stroke around Shop All / category product cards, ~2px outside the card's own correctly-gold border. First pass checked the product-card block's own computed `border` and `box-shadow` and found only the correct `#E3D6C5` — genuinely couldn't reproduce a dark border at that layer, and said so rather than guessing at a fix. The user's follow-up correctly identified the gap: an `outline` on a wrapping element wouldn't show up in a border/box-shadow check, and outlines don't affect layout so they wouldn't surface in a box-model diff either.

**Actual finding, one level up from where the first pass looked**: not an `outline` — a second, genuine `border` on `.product-grid__item` (the `<li>` wrapping each card, `sections/main-collection.liquid` line ~75), one level above the product-card block itself. Traced the CSS custom property chain live (`getComputedStyle(...).getPropertyValue('--color-border-rgb')`) up through the DOM rather than guessing from source: correct (`227 214 197` / `#E3D6C5`) at the outer section wrapper, but switched to wrong (`19 26 62` / `#131A3E`) starting at a `<results-list class="... color-custom-...">` element — a *second*, independent `.color-custom-{section_id}` scope, nested inside the first, overriding it for everything below.

**Root cause**: `snippets/product-grid.liquid` line 14 has its own `render 'contrast-override', background_color: ..., section_id: section.id` call — no `border_color` passed, so it falls back to the dark palette-darkest default. This is the exact same bug as `main-collection.liquid` edit 1 and `product-recommendations.liquid` (§19/§6), already flagged in §19 as systemic ("over 40 Horizon files carry the same latent pattern... only the two instances directly implicated by this review were fixed. The full list is a real, separate follow-up") — this is that follow-up finding a third live instance, not a new class of bug. `.product-grid__item`'s own CSS (also in `product-grid.liquid`) reads the same `--color-border-rgb` variable the product-card block reads, but from a *different, closer* `.color-custom` scope than the one already fixed — so the two elements, sitting one inside the other, ended up with two different resolved colours from what looks like the same variable name.

**The "occasion pages don't show this" premise didn't hold up on direct check.** Both Shop All and the occasion template render through `main-collection.liquid` → `product-grid.liquid`, so the bug should affect both equally — and confirmed live it did: `/collections/condiments?view=occasion` showed the identical `rgb(19, 26, 62)` on its own `.product-grid__item` before the fix. Likely explanation: occasion pages had zero products in Admin for most of this build (§25, §29), so there was nothing rendering a `<li>` to show a border on when it was last actually looked at — not evidence the bug was absent, just evidence there was nothing to show it on at the time. Top Sellers, by contrast, genuinely is a different code path — confirmed live it uses `.resource-list__item`/`.resource-list__slide` (a carousel, via a different snippet entirely), never touches `product-grid.liquid`, and its wrapper's border stayed `0px` throughout, unaffected before and after.

**Fix**: added the same one-line `border_color: settings.color_palette.color2` to `snippets/product-grid.liquid`'s `contrast-override` call — third instance of an already-proven fix, not a new mechanism. `snippets/product-grid.liquid` is only rendered from two places (`sections/main-collection.liquid`, `sections/search-results.liquid`), both checked before applying — the fix also correctly reaches the site search results page as a bonus, not a scope concern.

**Verified live after the fix**, all via computed `border-color` on the actual `.product-grid__item` element, not by re-reading the CSS: Shop All, Sauces & Chutneys, and Pantry & Gifts all now `rgb(227, 214, 197)` / `#E3D6C5` (matching the product-card's own border — single line, not two); the occasion template (`/collections/condiments?view=occasion`) also now correct, confirming the fix covers the page the original "doesn't show this" claim was about; Top Sellers reconfirmed unaffected (`0px`, `resource-list__item`, no regression). `shopify theme check --path .` clean.

## 31. Product card is two structurally different treatments, not one colour bug (2026-08-25)

**Report**: the collection-grid product card's dark stroke (§30) was assumed to be the whole story. It wasn't — the underlying premise was wrong. The design has **two genuinely different card components**, not one card with a border-colour mistake:

- **Card A** — bordered, `background: var(--surface-card)`, price + Add-button row pinned to the bottom via `margin-top:auto`. Source: `design/_archive/Lambruk Pantry Design System/components/commerce/ProductCard.jsx`. Used on Shop All, Sauces & Chutneys/Pantry & Gifts/Tea, and occasion pages. Already signed off — §30's fix (the stroke) applies here and nowhere else.
- **Card B** — no wrapper border, background or padding at all; the *image* alone carries the corner and edge treatment (`border-radius:16px; border:1px solid #E3D6C5; aspect-ratio:4/5`); category eyebrow above the title; title and price share one row (`display:flex; align-items:baseline; justify-content:space-between`); no Add button; a reserved-but-unwired star-rating row below. Source: the Seasonal Spotlight/Top Sellers rail as rendered in `LambrukPantry Desktop.dc.html` lines 142–161 — **not** `ProductCard.jsx`, despite that file's own prompt note claiming to cover "the seasonal rail" (flagged as inaccurate in `design/DESIGN-TOKENS.md` entry #11). Used only on the homepage Top Sellers rail.

Before touching anything, checked whether all four page types render through the same underlying block — they do: both cards are built from Horizon's shared `_product-card` / `_product-card-gallery` / `product-title` / `price` block types, just with different per-template settings and wrapper structure (Card B additionally wraps title+price in a `_product-card-group` row, which Card A doesn't use at all). One block family, two template-level configurations — not two separate code paths.

**Card B rebuild** (`templates/index.json`, `static-product-card` block): wrapper set to `border: "none"`, zero padding/radius; `product_card_gallery` given `border: "solid", border_color: "#E3D6C5", border_width: 1, border_radius: 16, image_ratio: "portrait"` (the border/radius moved off the wrapper and onto the image, matching the design exactly); a new `category_label` block added (see below); a new `title_price_row` block (`_product-card-group`, row direction, `horizontal_alignment: "space-between"`) holding `product_title` and `price`; a new `review` block (native `review.liquid`, which already renders nothing when a product has no `reviews.rating` metafield — the correct native mechanism for "reserve the space, don't fake it," no new placeholder logic needed).

**Category eyebrow scope extended.** `product-category-label` was occasion-only (§25); both card treatments carry the eyebrow in the design, so it's now also wired into `templates/index.json` (Card B) and `templates/collection.all.json` / `templates/collection.json` (Card A, Shop All + Tea/Condiments/Pantry). Doc comment in `blocks/product-category-label.liquid` updated to match — it previously said "not used on Shop All/Tea/Condiments/Pantry," which became false the moment those two templates were edited.

**Superseded the same day — see §33.** The client revised scope again a few hours later: the eyebrow belongs on occasion pages and the Top Sellers rail only, not on Shop All or the three category pages. §33 has the current, final state; the two paragraphs above are left as-written for the history, not as a live description of what's wired in today.

**Two bugs found only once real product data (not the empty-collection editor placeholder) was loaded into the rail, matching the "Mango, Blackberry & Sparkling Sauce 240ml" long-title test case exactly as asked:**

1. **Price wasn't pinned to the title's first line on a wrapped title — it was vertically centred across both lines.** `_product-card-group`'s `align_baseline` setting is only `visible_if vertical_alignment == 'flex-end'`, and even then it only ever produces CSS `align-items: last baseline` (aligns to a wrapped item's *last* line) — there's no setting path to plain first-line `baseline`, which is what the design source literally specifies (`align-items:baseline`) and what "price stays with the first line" requires. No combination of native settings can express this. Fixed with a narrowly-scoped CSS override in `blocks/_product-card-group.liquid`'s own stylesheet (`.product-card .group-block-content.layout-panel-flex--row { align-items: baseline; }`) — scoped to `.product-card` because `_product-card-group` has exactly one caller in the whole theme today (this row), confirmed by grepping every template for the block type before writing a selector this specific.
2. **On mobile, title and price stacked instead of sharing a row — with `vertical_on_mobile: false` set correctly.** Root cause was two-fold, both confirmed by reading computed styles live rather than guessing from source: (a) `snippets/product-card-styles.liquid` has a generic `.product-grid__card .group-block > * { flex-direction: column }` rule below 749px, written for some other/future case, but the *only* `.group-block` descendant of `.product-grid__card` anywhere in the theme today is this exact row — so it silently won on specificity over `.layout-panel-flex--row`'s own `flex-flow: row`, despite the block's own setting saying not to stack on mobile; (b) even with direction corrected, `layout-panel-style.liquid` hardcodes `--flex-wrap-mobile: wrap` (not exposed as a setting), which let the title (`width: fill`, greedy, long intrinsic content width) push price onto a second flex line regardless of direction. Both forced back with the same scoped override (`flex-direction: row; flex-wrap: nowrap`), same specificity reasoning as above.

**Verified live on the review theme (182395437357), desktop and mobile, using the real "Mango, Blackberry & Sparkling Sauce 240ml" product** (client-added to the `sunday-roast` collection, temporarily bound to the Top Sellers rail's `collection` setting for this test — a real `theme push`, not a client-side injection, reverted and confirmed restored to `""` by reloading the homepage afterward, not just by re-reading the file): no wrapper border/background on the card (`0px none`); gallery image carries `border-radius:16px` / `1px solid #E3D6C5`; category eyebrow renders; title wraps to two lines and price sits pinned within 2px of the title's own top on both breakpoints (desktop: title top 4092.8/price top 4094.8; mobile: title top 624.3/price top matching the first line, confirmed via a second product too); on mobile specifically, `flexFlow` computed as `row nowrap` with title and price sharing one row (not stacked) after the fix. Card A reconfirmed unregressed afterward on all four collection page types (Shop All, Condiments, Pantry, and the `sunday-roast` occasion page) — single `1px solid #E3D6C5` border on both the card and its `.product-grid__item` wrapper (no return of the §30 stroke), category eyebrow present, title/price still stacked vertically (not pulled into Card B's row treatment).

## 33. Category eyebrow scope, final (2026-08-25, third revision)

Three revisions to this same question in one day, so this section states the end state plainly rather than requiring a reader to reconcile §25 against §31:

- **On**: occasion pages (`templates/collection.occasion.json`) and the homepage Top Sellers rail (`templates/index.json`).
- **Off**: Shop All (`templates/collection.all.json`) and the three category pages — Tea/Condiments/Pantry (`templates/collection.json`).

**This is a per-template distinction, not a per-card-type one.** Before touching anything, confirmed Shop All and the three category pages do *not* share a template with occasion pages — three separate template files exist (`collection.all.json`, `collection.json`, `collection.occasion.json`), never shared. Card A (bordered) and Card B (borderless, Top Sellers) both render through the exact same `_product-card` / `_product-card-gallery` / `product-title` / `price` block family Shop All and the category pages already use — the eyebrow's presence or absence has never been a property of the card component, only of which template's `block_order` happens to include the `category_label` block.

**Mechanism, and why nothing else was considered.** Removing/adding the block from a template's own `block_order` is the entire mechanism — no new setting, no conditional Liquid, no second card component, no dependency on collection metadata (tags, type, a custom field) that a newly created collection could fail to carry. A new collection shows the eyebrow or doesn't purely by which template it's assigned to in Admin (occasion vs. default) — the same existing mechanism that already separates the two card treatments, nothing added that could drift out of sync or break on a collection nobody remembered to tag.

**Change made**: removed the `category_label` block (and its `block_order` entry) from `templates/collection.all.json` and `templates/collection.json`, added earlier the same day (§31). `templates/collection.occasion.json` and `templates/index.json` untouched — they had it before §31 and keep it now. Doc comment in `blocks/product-category-label.liquid` rewritten to state only the current scope, cross-referencing §25/§31/§32 for history rather than re-explaining it inline.

**Verified live on 182395437357**: `/collections/all` (Shop All) and `/collections/condiments` (category page) — no eyebrow. `/collections/sunday-roast?view=occasion` and the homepage Top Sellers rail — eyebrow present on both. Four pages checked, eyebrow on exactly two. Worth noting for anyone re-checking this later: `/collections/sunday-roast` *without* `?view=occasion` renders the default `collection.json` template, not the occasion one — correctly shows no eyebrow, but it's easy to test the wrong URL and get a false negative for the occasion-page case.

## 34. Card A's corners were square — a second, radius-less border on the `<li>` wrapper (2026-08-25)

**Report**: Card A's border colour is correct, but its corners trace a right angle in a screenshot measurement, not the rounded corner `ProductCard.jsx` specifies (`borderRadius: var(--radius-lg)`).

**Checked settings first, per instruction, before any code.** `config/settings_schema.json` has two global corner-radius settings — `product_corner_radius` (default 0) and `card_corner_radius` (default 0, saved value 4 in `config/settings_data.json`). Neither is the cause: Card A's own `_product-card` block instance carries its own explicit `border_radius: 16` setting in all three templates (`collection.all.json`, `collection.json`, `collection.occasion.json` — confirmed by reading each file directly), which overrides any block-level default regardless of the two global settings above. This is layer 3 (a block's own setting), not layer 1 — the two global settings turned out to be a red herring, not the cause, and are otherwise unrelated to this component.

**Root cause, one level up from the card again — the same shape of bug as §30, different property.** Read computed styles live on the actual rendered elements rather than assuming from the JSON value: `.product-card__content` (the card's own styled div) correctly computes `border-radius: 16px`. But `.product-grid__item` — the `<li>` wrapper one level up, which independently paints its *own* `1px solid #E3D6C5` border (added deliberately, see the `--product-card-border-width`/`--product-card-border-opacity` comment in `assets/lambruk-tokens.css` — Horizon's own `.product-grid__item` rule in `product-grid.liquid` references those two properties but never defines them, so without that activation the border would be invalid at computed-value time and not render at all) — computed `border-radius: 0px`. Same colour as the card's own border since §30, sitting exactly 1px outside it, so the two borders read as one continuous line — until the corner, where the inner border curves and the outer square one doesn't. The square outer corner is what a screenshot measurement actually catches, which is exactly what was reported.

Unlike the width/opacity case, this isn't a property with a missing *value* — Horizon's `.product-grid__item` rule has no `border-radius` property in it at all, so there was nothing to activate, only something to add.

**Design source confirmed directly, not assumed**: `--radius-lg: 16px` in `design/design_handoff_website/design-system-tokens/spacing.css` — matches the `border_radius: 16` already set on every Card A template's product-card block instance exactly. The 16px value in the theme was already correct; only the `<li>`'s radius was missing.

**Image radius — Card A does not match Card B here, by design, and that's correct as built.** Read `ProductCard.jsx` specifically for this: the outer `<article>` carries the only `border-radius` (`var(--radius-lg)`) and `overflow:'hidden'`; the inner image wrapper (`aspectRatio:'4/3', overflow:'hidden'`) has no radius of its own — it's a plain rectangle that gets visually clipped into the article's rounded shape by the *outer* element's overflow, not by carrying a radius itself. This is the opposite of Card B (`Spotlight.jsx`), where there's no outer bordered wrapper at all, so the image itself has to carry `border-radius:'var(--radius-lg)'` directly. Our build currently sets `border_radius: 16` on *both* the product-card wrapper and its `card-gallery` block — redundant against the design source (which only needs it on the outer wrapper) but not wrong: both radii are the same 16px, so the visual result is identical either way, just doubly-specified. Left as-is rather than churned, since removing the gallery's own radius setting would change nothing visible and isn't part of what was reported broken.

**Fix**: added `--product-card-border-radius: 16px` next to the existing width/opacity tokens in `assets/lambruk-tokens.css`, and `border-radius: var(--product-card-border-radius);` to `.product-grid__item` in `snippets/product-grid.liquid`. Hardcoded, same as the two properties beside it, and for the same reason — a static CSS file can't read the block's own Liquid setting. Noted in-file that this needs updating by hand if Card A's `border_radius` setting ever changes from 16.

**Verified live on 182395437357, computed styles not CSS**: `/collections/all` (Shop All) and `/collections/condiments` (category page) — `.product-grid__item` now computes `border-radius: 16px`, matching `.product-card__content`'s 16px exactly, both borders now trace the same curve. `/collections/sunday-roast?view=occasion` — same result, confirming the fix reaches occasion pages through the same shared `product-grid.liquid`/`main-collection.liquid` path. Homepage Top Sellers rail (Card B) reconfirmed unaffected — no `.product-grid__item` in its markup at all (`.resource-list__item`, a different structure entirely, per §31), `border-radius: 0px` there both before and after, exactly as intended since Card B's own radius lives on its image, not a wrapper.

## 35. `.product-grid__item`'s wrapper border removed entirely — it was always the duplicate (2026-08-25)

**Two defects in a row from the same element** (§30 colour, §34 radius) were each fixed by making `.product-grid__item`'s wrapper border match `.product-card__content`'s own border property by property. Asked to assess whether that pattern itself was wrong — whether the wrapper border should exist at all, since the design (`ProductCard.jsx`) specifies exactly one border, on the card, not two.

**Checked before changing anything, as instructed:**

- **Dependents**: grepped every consumer of `.product-grid__item` theme-wide. No hover, focus, selected, or filter-grid state keys off its border anywhere — the class's other rules only touch `padding`, `height` (organic layout), grid-column sizing (zoom-out density view), and fade-in `opacity`/`transform` transitions. None reference `border`, `border-color`, or `border-radius`. Two unrelated files coincidentally reuse the class name for unrelated purposes (`product-media-gallery-content-styles.liquid`'s gallery grid, `product-card-styles.liquid`'s equal-height rule) — neither touches its box model.
- **Which border is "real"**: re-read §1's own history. The wrapper border was activated 2026-08-20 in the belief that it *was* "the" product-card border — before the `_product-card` block's own `border`/`border_color`/`border_radius` settings existed as a configured, per-instance thing. `ProductCard.jsx` has exactly one bordered element, the outer `<article>`, which maps 1:1 to `.product-card__content` — already editor-configurable, already matching the design's colour and radius exactly (§34). The wrapper's border has no design-source counterpart at all; it's a hardcoded CSS-token activation with no correspondence in `ProductCard.jsx`. It was always the accidental duplicate, not a second intentional border.

**Fix**: `--product-card-border-width` in `assets/lambruk-tokens.css` changed from `1px` to `0`. `--product-card-border-opacity` and the `--product-card-border-radius` added in §34 left defined (not deleted) so `.product-grid__item`'s border declaration stays valid rather than reverting to Horizon's original "undefined custom property, invalid at computed-value time" state (§1) — same *visual* result (no border), but explicit rather than accidental. Comment in `lambruk-tokens.css` rewritten to carry this whole history in one place rather than across three separate finding-comments.

**Verified live on 182395437357, computed styles, all three page types**: Shop All, Condiments (category), and `/collections/sunday-roast?view=occasion` all show `.product-grid__item` computing `border: 0px solid rgb(227, 214, 197)` and `.product-card__content` still computing `border: 1px solid rgb(227, 214, 197)` — one real border, not two. `border-radius: 16px` unchanged on both. Screenshots on Shop All and the occasion page confirm no visible difference from before the change, as expected — this removes an invisible duplicate, not anything that was contributing to the visible result.

---

## 36. Shop All build: filter order, divider, card padding — and a header block that's been inert this entire build (2026-08-25)

Four changes requested against the design: a full page-header module, filter chip order, a divider gap below the filters, and generous card text padding. Three landed; the fourth surfaced a real, previously-hidden architecture gap.

**Filter order (`blocks/lambruk-tag-filter.liquid`)**: pills were rendered via `for value in tag_filter.values`, trusting Shopify's own facet API order — alphabetical by tag value (condiments, pantry, tea), which put Tea last. Confirmed live before changing anything, not assumed from reading the loop. Fixed by iterating a fixed key order (`tea,condiments,pantry`) and looking up the matching facet value for each, rather than trusting the API's order. `forloop.index` (used for each pill's unique DOM id) replaced with an explicit counter, since the nested loop changes what `forloop` refers to.

**Divider gap**: the design specifies two distinct gaps — the filter row's own `border-bottom` + `padding-bottom:28px` (already correctly built), *and* a separate `margin-top:40px` on the product grid that follows it. Confirmed live the actual gap was 0px, not just "tighter than design" — the `.collection-wrapper` grid's own gap doesn't reach between these two elements at all. First fix attempt used a general sibling selector (`.lambruk-tag-filter ~ .product-grid`) reasoning from the section's Liquid source; checked live before declaring it done and found `.product-grid` isn't a sibling of `.lambruk-tag-filter` at all — it's nested two levels deeper inside an unnamed wrapper div. Rewritten as `.collection-wrapper:has(.lambruk-tag-filter) .product-grid { margin-top: 40px; }`, scoped by containment rather than exact sibling depth — also more robust against Horizon changing that intermediate markup later. Both self-scope to Shop All for free: `lambruk-tag-filter` only renders when 2+ of the three known tags are present (§ its own doc comment), never on Tea/Condiments/Pantry.

**Card text padding**: design (`ProductCard.jsx`) wraps category/title/price in one `padding:20px` container with `gap:6px` between them; our build has no equivalent wrapper — title and price are flat siblings under the card's own flex column, each with their own tiny/zero padding. Reconstructed the same visual result without inventing a new wrapper block: set `product_card_gap: 0` (removing the uniform inter-block gap) and gave each text block its own padding — first block gets `padding-block-start: 20` (the image→text gap), last gets `padding-block-end: 20` (the bottom gap), every block gets `padding-inline: 20`, and 6px `padding-block-start` on each block after the first (the internal row-to-row gap). Applied identically to `collection.all.json`, `collection.json`, and `collection.occasion.json` (the last also has `category_label` and `buy_buttons` as additional rows in the same stack, given the same treatment). Verified live via computed `padding-*` on the actual rendered title/price elements (not the CSS) on both a Shop All card and a category-page card, desktop and mobile.

**Header module — real content built, genuinely inert on the live URL it was meant for.** Built a new page-header for `collection.all.json`'s existing generic `section` (type `"section"`, Horizon's native flexible section already present — reused, not a new section type, exactly as asked before building): "Shop" eyebrow, a static H1 ("Teas, relishes and pantry favourites, made in small batches"), and static body copy, on a `#F4F1EA` background band. Read every value directly from `LambrukPantry Desktop.dc.html` rather than estimating (background hex, `padding:64px 0 56px`, eyebrow letter-spacing `0.22em`, H1 `52px`/`line-height:1.04`/`max-width:720px`, body `17px`/`line-height:1.6`/`max-width:640px`). Reused two already-established named tokens where they matched exactly (`letter_spacing:"eyebrow"` → `--letter-spacing--body-eyebrow:0.22em`; `max_width:"wide"` → `--max-width--body-wide:640px`, both from §14/§15/DESIGN-TOKENS.md §2) rather than inventing new ones for values the theme already names. `text.liquid`'s `line_height`/`letter_spacing`/`max_width` settings are a closed three-value enum (`tight`/`normal`/`loose` or `narrow`/`normal`/`none`) string-interpolated into a CSS variable name with no free passthrough — same mechanism §14/§15 already worked around by adding named steps for values the enum can't express. The H1's exact `720px`/`1.04` don't match any existing or reasonably-added step (the `em`-based `max-width--*` scale explodes past 1000px at a 52px font-size, and this project's own component only ever uses this value once, unlike the reused eyebrow/wide steps) — added a small `custom-liquid` block in the same section with a scoped `<style>` targeting `#shopify-section-{{ section.id }}` `h1` directly for just these two properties, rather than a new pseudo-scale-step token for a value that's a one-off in the design source too (no named token for it there either — it's a literal `line-height:1.04` in the mockup's own inline style).

**Pushed and checked live — the whole thing render as the theme's *previous* default: "Products" as a plain `<h2>`, `#FBFAF7` background, no eyebrow, no body copy — as if nothing had been pushed.** Ruled out the obvious explanations before accepting a stranger one: confirmed via `shopify theme pull` that the live theme's `templates/collection.all.json` genuinely has the new blocks and `background_color:"#F4F1EA"` (not a failed push); confirmed the "Horizon · Draft" preview bar was showing, and confirmed three *other* changes pushed in the same session (product-grid border-radius, product-grid border removal, category-eyebrow removal) were all correctly live on the same URL (not a wrong-theme mixup, not a stale CDN cache — a real cache would be stale for everything, not selectively for one section).

**Actual cause: `/collections/all` isn't rendering `collection.all.json` at all.** Confirmed decisively, not inferred: `collection.all.json` has a `cta_sample_box` section with unique copy ("Sell the moment, not the label") that no other template has — absent from the live page entirely. And `collection.json` (the *default* template, shared by Tea/Condiments/Pantry) has its own header block still reading `<h1>{{ closest.collection.title }}</h1>` — which for the "All" collection resolves to exactly "Products" (its Admin-configured title), matching what actually rendered, background colour included. The "all" collection's `template_suffix` was apparently never assigned to `collection.all.json` in Admin — a gap the go-live checklist already flagged as unconfirmed ("Cafe and All-products need a quick Admin check... not confirmed against the actual stored `template_suffix`") but hadn't been proven either way until now. Every prior "Shop All" verification this build session happened to look correct because every other setting this session touched (border colour, radius, category eyebrow, card padding) was kept identical across `collection.all.json` and `collection.json` — so both files always agreed, and there was never a divergence to expose which one was actually live, until this header, which is deliberately Shop-All-only content.

**Not fixed — this is an Admin-side template assignment, outside what a theme push can do, and CLAUDE.md's own standing rule requires asking before touching template_suffix at all** (writing it live-affects whatever theme is currently published, per the go-live checklist's own earlier caution). Filter order, divider gap, and card padding all live correctly today because they're rendered through files/blocks `collection.json` and `collection.all.json` both already share or that self-scope independent of which template is active (`lambruk-tag-filter`, `main-collection`, the product-card blocks). The header specifically needs a decision: (a) assign `collection.all.json` as the "All products" collection's template in Admin, which is what makes the rest of that file's content (including `cta_sample_box`, never seen live either) reachable for the first time, or (b) move this header's content into `collection.json` behind a `collection.handle == 'all'` check, accepting the coupling of Shop-All-specific copy living inside the file shared with Tea/Condiments/Pantry. Left for the client to choose rather than guessed at.

---

## 37. `/collections/all` was Shopify's automatic collection, not an assignable one — replaced with a real collection (2026-08-25)

§36 left the header's template mismatch as an open decision between two options: assign `collection.all.json` in Admin, or move the header's content into the shared `collection.json` behind a handle check. Neither was actually available as first framed — **`/collections/all` is Shopify's automatic "All products" pseudo-collection, which has no Admin record at all and so cannot be assigned a `template_suffix` in the first place.** This wasn't discoverable from the theme side; it's a fact about how Shopify itself handles the reserved `all` handle, confirmed by the client checking directly in Admin.

**Fix**: client created a real, standalone "Shop All" collection (Automated, sort A–Z) at the same handle, `all` — a real Admin resource overrides Shopify's automatic pseudo-collection at that handle once one exists. Verified live on 182395437357 immediately after: `/collections/all`'s `<h1>` now reads "Shop All" (was "Products," the automatic collection's implicit label) and products sort alphabetically (Beetroot → Blueberry → Chilli...), both matching the new collection's own configuration — confirms the real collection is now what's resolving at that URL, not the automatic one.

**`collection.all.json` is still not the active template — this is expected, not a new problem.** A brand-new collection has no `template_suffix` set by default, so it still renders through `collection.json` (the theme's bare default), just now backed by the new collection's real data instead of the automatic collection's. Confirmed live: no `cta_sample_box` section (unique to `collection.all.json`, still absent), no new header content (a false-positive check against a `<p>Shop</p>` turned out to be the *footer's* own "Shop" link-column heading, not the new header block — the real header block never rendered). **Every "Shop All" verification in this entire build, from the very start, was actually checking `collection.json`'s rendering — `collection.all.json`, including `cta_sample_box`, has never been confirmed live at any point.** Assigning the new collection's theme template to `collection.all.json` in Admin is the next, still-outstanding step — flagged to the client that the dropdown may not offer it at all today, since Shopify's collection template picker only lists templates that exist in the *published* theme, and Horizon (182395437357) isn't published; Flux is.

---

## 38. `collection.all.json` verified live for the first time, via `?view=all` (2026-08-25)

§37 left `collection.all.json` entirely unverified — every prior "Shop All" check this build, from the very start, was actually hitting `collection.json`. Confirmed directly this can't be fixed pre-publish: the Theme template dropdown in Admin only lists templates from the *published* theme (Flux), so `collection.all.json` isn't a selectable option today regardless of readiness (see the rewritten go-live checklist).

**Verified anyway, using the same alternate-template mechanism already relied on for occasion pages (`?view=occasion`) — `?view=all` renders `collection.all.json` without assigning it,** exactly the way `?view=<suffix>` works for any `templates/collection.<suffix>.json` file. First real render of this file at any point in the build. Checked everything from §36 against it directly, desktop and mobile, via computed styles:

- **Header band**: background `rgb(244, 241, 234)` = `#F4F1EA` exact, on the dedicated `.section-background` element (not the transparent section wrapper itself — Horizon paints section backgrounds via a separate absolutely-positioned child, checked the right element). `padding-block-start`/`-end` computed `64px`/`56px` exact.
- **Eyebrow**: text "Shop", `color: rgb(160, 112, 55)` = `#A07037` exact, `text-transform: uppercase`, `letter-spacing: 2.64px` = exactly `0.22em` at `12px` font-size, confirming the `--letter-spacing--body-eyebrow` token resolved correctly.
- **Heading**: `color: rgb(19, 26, 62)` = `#131A3E` exact, `font-family: "Instrument Serif", serif` exact, `max-width: 720px` exact (the `custom-liquid` scoped override), `line-height` computed to exactly `1.04×` font-size (the same override). `font-size` computed `48.33px` rather than the design's `52px` — expected, not a defect: `3.25rem` is above this project's `3.0rem` fluid-clamp cutoff (§ typography-style.liquid), so it fluid-scales with viewport width the same way every other custom-preset heading at this size does sitewide (hero's H1 included) — 52px is the value at the fluid clamp's upper viewport bound, not a fixed constant at every width.
- **Body**: `color: rgb(74, 84, 120)` = `#4A5478` exact, `font-size: 17px` exact, `line-height: 27.2px` = exactly `1.6×` (`--line-height--body-loose`), `max-width: min(100%, 640px)` (the `--max-width--body-wide` token) exact.
- **Filter order**: `All, Tea, Sauces & Chutneys, Pantry & Gifts` exact, both breakpoints.
- **Divider gap**: `40px` between the filter row's border and the grid, both breakpoints.
- **Card text padding**: title wrapper and price both `20px`/`6px` as built, both breakpoints.
- **`cta_sample_box`**: rendered — the navy "Sell the moment, not the label. Start with a sample box." band with its "Shop Gifts & Samples" button, full width, confirmed live for the first time this entire build.

Everything from §36 is now confirmed correct on the file that will actually matter once Horizon is published and the five collections in the go-live checklist are assigned. Nothing found wrong; no further code changes from this pass.

## 39. Cafe page built — 5 new sections, 2 new native mechanisms, 3 buttons deliberately dropped (2026-08-25)

**Read-and-report first, per instruction.** Read both `LambrukPantry Desktop.dc.html` and `LambrukPantry Mobile.dc.html`'s `isCafe` blocks in full before writing anything, checked `templates/page.cafe.json`'s existing content (two sections — High Tea, Pull Up a Chair — already correct, kept as-is) and `COMPONENT-INVENTORY.md` for reuse before proposing anything new. Full section-by-section plan and reuse mapping confirmed with the client before building.

**Reused, no new code**: `sections/hero.liquid` (page hero — first real instantiation of this section anywhere in the theme; `media-with-content` had been the pattern for the homepage's own hero, but that's a side-by-side split, not this page's full-bleed-image-with-centred-overlay shape, so hero.liquid was the correct match, not media-with-content again). `media-with-content` (High Tea, Pull Up a Chair — already built). `collection-list` + `_collection-card` with `placement: "below_image"` for the "Bring the Lambruk experience home" tiles — COMPONENT-INVENTORY.md classified this as "Adapt" (a 3rd variant of the homepage's scrim-on-image collection-card), but reading the actual markup showed no scrim or overlaid text at all, just a plain image then a separate label+arrow row below — `_collection-card.liquid` already has a native `below_image` placement mode built for exactly this, so it needed zero adaptation, just the existing enum value. The homepage's own "Subscribe to Seasonal Dispatches" section (`section_newsletter` in `templates/index.json`) copied verbatim — same block composition, same copy.

**New, additive only — `sections/lambruk-cafe-gallery.liquid` + `snippets/lambruk-cafe-gallery-image.liquid`.** The gallery's asymmetric mosaic (two `grid-column: span 2` tiles, one nested 2-row sub-grid) has no native Horizon equivalent — checked `blocks/image.liquid` directly before concluding this, confirmed it positions one image per block instance with no concept of grid spans. Eyebrow/heading/body are theme blocks (merchant-editable), the 8 tiles are fixed `image_picker` settings (position-specific, not a repeatable block list, matching `sections/hero.liquid`'s own `image_1`/`image_2` precedent). Every tile left unassigned — photography is placeholder for this whole build — reusing `blocks/image.liquid`'s own `image_url`/`image_tag`/`placeholder_svg_tag` mechanism rather than inventing a new placeholder path, so an empty slot still renders Horizon's real placeholder graphic, not a broken image.

**New — `settings.lambruk_cafe_phone` / `settings.lambruk_cafe_hours`, `config/settings_schema.json`.** The phone number and opening hours each appeared as literal hardcoded strings in two places already (`0458 370 757` on the homepage cafe-intro; `8:30 AM – 5:00 PM` on the homepage cafe-intro *and* this page's existing Pull Up a Chair section) before this page even added a third and fourth instance in the new booking panel. Client's own requirement: editable without a developer, and duplicated values are how they drift. Neither maps to any existing Horizon setting or this project's own prior custom settings, so added a new top-level group (`t:names.lambruk_store_info`, following the same `t:`-locale-key convention every other lambruk-* addition uses) with two `text` settings, defaulting to the values already in use. Retrofitted both pre-existing homepage instances to reference the new settings too, not just the new Cafe content — leaving them hardcoded would have defeated the point.

**Position in `settings_schema.json`, 2026-08-25 (revised same day).** First placed at the end of the file — the client asked me to confirm it would actually surface in the theme editor rather than get lost, and while I couldn't check the live editor directly (no Admin access), reading the schema's own array order showed it would land dead last, after all 17 of Horizon's native groups, findable only by scrolling the full sidebar or using the editor's settings search. **Moved to sit immediately after "Logo & favicon"** (index 2 of what's now 19 top-level groups) — the first custom group a client scanning top-to-bottom would hit, no scrolling required. Every native Horizon group's own relative order was left untouched; only this one entry moved. **If a future Horizon theme update reshuffles or regenerates `settings_schema.json` wholesale, this position is not guaranteed to survive the merge** — check that "Store info (Lambruk)" still sits near the top (not appended to the end again) after any such update, and re-move it if the update tool dropped it back at the bottom.

**Real constraint hit while wiring the settings in: `{{ settings.* }}` is rejected inside a `text` block's own `text` field at push time** (`'lambruk_cafe_phone' is not a resource setting` — a genuine Shopify platform validation, discovered only by attempting the push, not documented anywhere read in advance). `{{ closest.collection.title }}`-style resource references work fine in that field (already used elsewhere this project); global theme settings don't. Every instance needing the phone/hours settings — 5 total, the 2 retrofitted homepage ones plus 3 new ones on this page — was rebuilt as a `custom-liquid` block instead (confirmed exempt from this validation, since the Call/Text buttons' `tel:`/`sms:` hrefs already used `settings.lambruk_cafe_phone` in a custom-liquid block without issue), with the original block's typography reproduced via inline CSS rather than the native text-block settings system. Documented here so the next person doesn't rediscover this the same way.

**Call to Book / Text to Book buttons** are also `custom-liquid`, not native `button` blocks — `href="tel:{{ settings.lambruk_cafe_phone | remove: ' ' }}"` / `sms:` — so the booking destination and the displayed number can never drift out of sync with each other either, not just across pages.

**Booking widget mode: not built, by client decision, not a gap.** Bookings are phone/text only; the design's inert `bookingWidget` branch (date/time/guest-count fake inputs) was never a candidate. Closes `COMPONENT-INVENTORY.md`'s own open question on this.

**Three buttons dropped, not pointed at a placeholder — "View Menu" (hero), "View Cafe Menu" (Pull Up a Chair), "View High Tea Menu" (High Tea).** Checked the design source specifically for where these should go: none of the three has an `onClick` handler at all in either `.dc.html` file, unlike every other button in the same files, which link to a real route. Checked `Site Map.dc.html` and the package `README.md` too — no menu page, PDF, or route exists anywhere. Built the three sections without these buttons rather than linking to `#`, the homepage, or anywhere else — logged in `REVIEW-NOTES.md` as blocked on content, to be added back once real menu content exists.

**One native-block constraint accepted, not worked around: no divider under the pantry tiles' label row.** The design has a 1px bottom border under each tile's title+arrow row; `_collection-card.liquid`'s allowed block list is `text`/`spacer`/`button`/`group`/`collection-title` only — no `_divider`, no `@theme` wildcard to reach it another way. A full box border via `group`'s own border setting would put a border on all four sides, not just the bottom, which doesn't match the design either. Dropped the divider rather than force a wrong-shaped fix — a minor decorative simplification, not a functional gap.

**Map placeholder built exactly as designed, not finished** — a static box (pin icon, address, "Google Maps embed" label), matching the design source's own literal placeholder content. Added to the go-live checklist as a real decision the client owes (real embed vs. static image vs. address-only) rather than picked on the theme's behalf.

**`/pages/cafe` joins the deferred template-assignment list.** Checked live: the plain page currently renders through Horizon's generic default page template, not `page.cafe.json` — same situation as `collection.all.json` and the four occasion collections (§37/§38), for the same reason (Admin's template picker only lists templates from the *published* theme, and Horizon isn't published). Verified the actual page content via the same `?view=<suffix>` alternate-template mechanism already proven for Shop All and the occasion pages (`?view=cafe`) — every section confirmed correct this way, live, on desktop and mobile:
- **Hero**: eyebrow/H1/body colours and copy exact; the single "Book a table" button (no "View Menu") correctly links to `#cafe-booking`; placeholder illustration renders (no photography assigned) with the scrim overlay.
- **Booking panel**: hours and phone number both display from the new settings (confirmed rendering the schema default, not a hardcoded string); Call/Text buttons' `tel:`/`sms:` hrefs correctly derived and space-stripped (`tel:0458370757`); map placeholder box, address, and `[Parking and access details]` bracket placeholder all present; no dead buttons.
- **High Tea / Pull Up a Chair**: unchanged content confirmed intact; both dead buttons confirmed absent; Pull Up a Chair's hours line confirmed now reading from the shared setting.
- **Gallery**: all 8 tiles present; mobile grid re-checked via computed tile positions (not just a screenshot) after an initial visual misread — tile 2/3 do stack correctly within their shared column, matching the responsive CSS as written.
- **Pantry tiles**: all three category tiles present and correctly linked (the middle tile reads "Sauces & Chutney", not "Chutneys" as in the mockup copy — the real Shopify collection's actual title, not a bug).
- **Subscribe band**: heading and email field present, matching the homepage's own instance.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout (two schema errors caught and fixed before push: a section name 5 characters over Shopify's 25-character limit, and an icon `width` value one unit off its `step: 2` grid).

## 40. Our Story page — the file was already ~60% built; added the missing hero and full-bleed image (2026-08-26)

`templates/page.our-story.json` turned out not to be empty — it already carried real, previously-built content for three of the design's five sections (Meet Paige 2-up bio, "How we make things" icon trio, "Taste the range" CTA band), copy matching `LambrukPantry Desktop.dc.html`'s `isStory` block verbatim. Only the hero intro and the full-bleed photo below it were missing. Confirmed via `COMPONENT-INVENTORY.md:107-115`, which had independently classified all five sections as reusing existing patterns before this session re-checked against current build state.

**`hero_our_story`** — plain `section`, centered eyebrow/H1/body text blocks, structurally identical to `hero_wholesale` (`templates/page.wholesale.json:11`) minus its CTA button and divider. `var(--display-1-size)` for the H1 resolves to exactly 56px desktop / 38px mobile — both match the design's own raw values (`Desktop.dc.html:873`: 56px; `Mobile.dc.html:673`: 38px) precisely, confirming that token is the correct match, not an approximation.

**`story_image`** — a plain `image` block, `image_ratio: "landscape"` (confirmed in `blocks/image.liquid:8-9` to resolve to exactly `16/9`, not the `"custom"` value already flagged elsewhere in `REVIEW-NOTES.md` as unhandled by this same block). No image assigned — same placeholder-photo policy as the other four `media-with-content` bands.

**Mobile aspect-ratio override, approved before writing.** The design specifies `16/9` desktop and `4/3` mobile for this same photo — a genuine ratio *change*, not a scaled version of one value. `blocks/image.liquid`'s `image_ratio` setting has no responsive variant, so this needed a scoped `custom-liquid` sibling block: `#shopify-section-{{ section.id }} .placeholder-image, ... .image-block__image { aspect-ratio: 4/3; }` inside a `max-width: 749px` media query — the third use of this exact scoped-override pattern this build (after the Cafe map embed and the footer menu heading style). Targets both `.placeholder-image` (today's unassigned state) and `.image-block__image` (the future state once a real photo is uploaded) so it doesn't need revisiting when that happens. Recorded in `DESIGN-TOKENS.md` §9 (mobile vs. desktop deltas).

**`how_we_make_things` mobile behaviour — deliberately left inconsistent with its own page's mobile mockup, for consistency with its sibling instances.** `Mobile.dc.html:687-700` drops this section's icons and its "The Lambruk Promise"/"How we make things" header entirely on mobile, unlike the identical icon+heading+copy trio's other two instances (Product sourcing row, Wholesale Benefits), which keep both on every breakpoint. Decision: keep them here too. Logged in `REVIEW-NOTES.md` as a closed design-source discrepancy, not a bug, so it isn't re-flagged on a future pass.

**Verified live via `?view=our-story`** (theme 182395437357 — real template assignment still blocked, same as every other deferred page): desktop hero eyebrow/H1/body computed styles match design exactly (12px uppercase eyebrow at `#986A34`; 56px serif H1; 17px body, centered); full-bleed placeholder confirmed at exactly `16/9` with `16px` border-radius, full 1200px content width. Mobile: H1 clamps to 38px, image confirmed switched to `4/3` at 343×257px, icon trio confirmed stacking full-width with both icons and header intact. **This was also the first live render of the pre-existing `how_we_make_things` section** (previously only verified by pattern-matching against Product/Wholesale, per §16) — all three icons (`Lambruk-Illustration.png`/`-4`/`-8`) resolved to their real CDN URLs and rendered correctly against their matching headings.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout.
