## 41. Contact page built — a conditional wholesale sub-form, extracted into a shared field snippet (2026-08-26)

`templates/page.contact.json` was genuinely untouched stock Horizon (`main-page` + native `contact-form`) — nothing built. The design (`Desktop.dc.html:930-1019`, `Mobile.dc.html:711-792`) turned out to be substantially more than "Name/Email/topic chips/Message": selecting "Wholesale" as the enquiry topic reveals a nested sub-form sharing the Wholesale page's own field set and `interests` state variable almost verbatim (Business name, Phone, Business type, Location, an identical 5-option "Interested in" multi-select, and a venue textarea) — confirmed via the design's own state logic (`contactWholesale: s.topic === 'Wholesale'`), not a coincidence of similar copy.

**Hero and info columns** — plain composed `section`s, same pattern as every other page's hero. One real difference from Wholesale/Our Story: Contact's hero is **left-aligned**, not centered (`Desktop.dc.html:933` has no `text-align: center` on the hero's own wrapper, unlike the other two) — confirmed by reading the actual inline styles rather than assuming the pattern held. Info columns use a `_divider` block before each of the three items (General/Cafe/Wholesale) to get the design's border-top-per-item treatment, since Horizon's `group` border setting is uniform on all four sides, not per-edge.

**Extracted the shared wholesale field group into `snippets/lambruk-wholesale-fields.liquid`**, per the explicit ask to keep one source of truth rather than duplicate it into a second form that would drift the first time either was edited alone. Renders Business name, Phone, Business type, Location, Interested in, and the venue textarea; deliberately excludes Contact name and Email, since those stay owned by each calling form's own top-level fields. `blocks/lambruk-wholesale-enquiry-form.liquid` was refactored to call it — purely additive from the outside (same field names, same `data-preserve-field`/`data-interest-checkbox`/`data-body-input` attributes, same CSS classes), confirmed via `git diff` that its own name/email fields, error/success blocks, submit button, and sessionStorage script are untouched.

**One thing intentionally not shared: the venue textarea's error-repopulation strategy**, disclosed rather than silently decided. Wholesale's own form repopulates it from Shopify's native `form.body` echo (the marker stripped back off) — that mechanism is untouched and still works exactly as before, since `body_value` is now just a snippet parameter Wholesale's caller passes in. Contact's form can't use the same trick: its own `contact[body]` folds together a marker, the venue text, *and* a separate Message field, so a native echo can't be cleanly split back into "just the venue answer" after an error round trip. Contact's own script instead caches the venue field in its own sessionStorage payload, the same way it caches Business name/type/Location — a different restore strategy per caller for one field, not a shared one, because the two forms' body-assembly rules genuinely differ.

**New block: `blocks/lambruk-contact-form.liquid`.** Own form (can't share a `<form>` element with Wholesale's), own Name/Email/topic/Message fields, calls the shared snippet for the wholesale-topic reveal. Topic selector is native radio inputs (`contact[Enquiry Topic]`) rather than the checkbox+hidden-joined-field trick Wholesale's multi-select needs — it's single-select, so no join is needed, just a `data-topic-radio` marker for the show/hide and sessionStorage-restore logic. `contact[body]` is assembled client-side at submit: plain Message text alone for every topic except Wholesale, where it becomes a distinct marker (`WHOLESALE ENQUIRY (VIA CONTACT PAGE)` — deliberately different from the Wholesale page's own `WHOLESALE ENQUIRY`, so the two entry points stay distinguishable in the shared inbox) followed by the venue answer and the Message answer, each under its own label, in the design's own order. Error/success block shape (`tabindex="-1" autofocus`, no icon/badge on Contact's own success state — the design doesn't show one, unlike Wholesale's checkmark) and the sessionStorage preserve/restore pattern both carried over from Wholesale, adapted to this form's own field set (topic, venue text, and Message all now part of the cached payload alongside the shared snippet's own fields). The global no-scroll-jump fix (`layout/theme.liquid` edit 2, tracked in §6) needed no changes — it's generic to any hash-carrying redirect, not wired to a specific form id.

**Bug found and fixed during verification, not present in Wholesale's original file:** the wholesale sub-form's container used the HTML `hidden` attribute to show/hide, but its own `{% stylesheet %}` set `display: flex` on the same class — author styles always beat the user-agent's default `[hidden] { display: none }` rule regardless of specificity, so the sub-form rendered permanently visible from first load, radio state notwithstanding. Fixed with an explicit `.lambruk-contact-form__wholesale-fields[hidden] { display: none; }` override. Caught by computed-style inspection before the first screenshot, not by eye — the screenshot alone looked plausible enough (a form with all its fields showing) that eyeballing it would have missed that the *conditional* part of "conditional reveal" wasn't actually conditional yet.

**Verified live via `?view=contact`** (theme 182395437357), desktop and mobile: hero left-aligned as designed; three info columns present with correct headings/copy, the Cafe phone number sourced from `settings.lambruk_cafe_phone` (not hardcoded) matching the same dynamic-value pattern used on the Cafe page itself; "Cafe Details" → `/pages/cafe`, "Wholesale Details" → `/pages/wholesale`, both confirmed via rendered `href`. Topic chips wrap correctly on mobile (2 rows); clicking "Wholesale" reveals the sub-form on both breakpoints, confirmed via the `hidden` attribute and computed `display` together, not appearance alone (given the bug just found). Simulated a submit (dispatched and intercepted client-side, values pre-filled via JS) to confirm the combined-body and interests-join logic directly: correct marker, correct field order and labels, correct comma-joined interests. That simulation did trigger one real, Cloudflare-blocked submission attempt (a dispatched `submit` event's `preventDefault()` didn't stop the theme's own native form submission the way it would for a trusted, user-initiated event) — no email was sent, matching the sandbox limitation already known going in; real submission testing is the user's own follow-up.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout.

## 42. Occasion pages: removed the filter row, fixed the grid to 3-up — buy-button gap found but deliberately not fixed yet (2026-08-27)

Reviewing the occasion template (`?view=occasion`) against the design surfaced seven discrepancies; three (hero photo, "Other Occasions" card images, card blurbs) turned out to be Admin data already wired correctly on the theme side — `sections/lambruk-occasion-hero.liquid` and `sections/lambruk-other-occasions.liquid` both read directly from each collection's own native `image` field and `metafields.custom.occasion_card_blurb`, rendering nothing rather than guessing when unset, per the same discipline used everywhere else in this build. Two of the remaining four were built this pass; the product-card gap was investigated but deliberately left unbuilt pending confirmation, since it turned out to reach further than the occasion template alone.

**Filter row removed from occasion pages.** The chips-and-count row occasion pages were showing isn't the native `filters` block (correctly disabled there, `enable_filtering: false`) — it's the separate `lambruk-tag-filter.liquid`, injected into every collection page via a hardcoded `content_for 'block'` call in `sections/main-collection.liquid` (§6). That block already self-hides based on tag diversity (only renders when 2+ of tea/condiments/pantry are present, so it's correctly invisible on the single-category Tea/Condiments/Pantry pages) — but an occasion collection deliberately spans multiple categories, which trips that same heuristic in reverse. Design source confirmed directly: the whole row is wrapped in `{% if occasionPlain %}` (`Desktop.dc.html:321-330`), absent whenever a curated occasion is being viewed. Added `template.suffix != 'occasion'` to the block's existing visibility check — a real, source-confirmed exclusion, not a guess. This also closes the "6 items" vs "6 products" wording complaint for this page specifically, since that text lives in the same block gated by the same condition; the underlying wording gap on Shop All itself is untouched and separate.

**Grid columns fixed from 4-up to 3-up, occasion only.** The grid isn't a fixed column count — `repeat(auto-fill, minmax({{ product_card_size }}, 1fr))` — so the actual column count is a function of card minimum width vs. container width, not a settings value that reads as "3" or "4" anywhere. `product_card_size: "medium"` (250px minimum) auto-fills to 4 columns at this theme's ~1240px content width; `"large"` (340px) auto-fills to exactly 3, matching the design's explicit `repeat(3, 1fr)`. Changed `templates/collection.occasion.json` only.

**Scope check before touching anything else, per instruction — found the same 4-up gap likely extends to Shop All and the category pages, deliberately left unfixed.** `collection.all.json` and `collection.json` carry the identical `product_card_size: "medium"`, and live computed styles confirm both actually render 4-up too (Shop All: 279px × 4 at a 1200px grid width; Tea Collection: same, screenshot-confirmed). The design's own grid code is shared, unconditional markup between Shop All and occasion states in the prototype (`Desktop.dc.html:331`, outside both the `occasionPlain`/`occasionActive` branches), so it specifies 3-up there too — but Shop All carries 52 products against an occasion page's half-dozen, so 3-up there is a real tradeoff (meaningfully more scrolling), not a mechanical match. Left as a decision to make deliberately, not silently fixed alongside occasion.

**Product-card gap: investigated, confirmed to extend beyond occasion, not built.** Occasion's `buy_buttons` block has no explicit `blocks`/`block_order` of its own, so it inherits `blocks/buy-buttons.liquid`'s schema *preset* — quantity stepper + add-to-cart + accelerated-checkout (Shop Pay + more payment options) — none of which the design specifies. Checked `ProductCard.jsx` (Card A's own cited source) directly: `<Button size="sm" variant="secondary" onClick={onAdd}>Add</Button>`, sharing one row with price via `justify-content: space-between` — no stepper, no Shop Pay, ever. Checked whether Shop All and the category pages have the same misconfiguration and found something more basic: neither has a `buy_buttons` block at all — no Add button of any kind. Checked the design source specifically for whether Shop All's own product grid differs from occasion's: it doesn't — `isShop`'s product grid (`Desktop.dc.html:331-337`) is unconditional, shared code between `occasionPlain` and `occasionActive`, using the identical `<ProductCard>` props including `onAdd`. So Shop All and the three category pages are missing the Add button entirely, not just running the wrong variant of it — a real, confirmed gap against the design, not a misread. Fixing all four templates in one pass would need: an explicit `blocks`/`block_order` on each `buy_buttons` instance limited to `add-to-cart` only; wrapping price + button in a shared `_product-card-group` row (the same mechanism already used for Card B's title+price row) so they sit side-by-side per the design rather than stacking; and adding that `buy_buttons`/group structure to Shop All and the two category templates from scratch, since they currently have none. Not started — explicit instruction to hold until confirmed.

Occasion hero photography identified in the design package, not yet uploaded: `design/design_handoff_website/assets/images/occasion-slow-mornings.png` (croissant, cinnamon roll, coffee — matches the brief exactly, verified by opening the file), `occasion-entertaining.png`, `occasion-sunday-roast.png`, `occasion-high-tea.png` — one per collection, filenames self-explanatory, ready to upload as Admin's native collection `image` field once the client supplies real photography or wants these as interim placeholders.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout.

**Follow-up, next day — hero scrim colour and Other Occasions padding, 2026-08-28.**

*Scrim.* Design source confirmed to specify navy, not neutral black — `rgba(13, 16, 36, ...)` (the brand's own `--blue-950`), identical on both breakpoints, re-verified directly in `design_handoff_website/` rather than trusted from memory. `sections/lambruk-occasion-hero.liquid` was already implementing this correctly, byte-for-byte. Changed to `rgba(0, 0, 0, ...)` at the client's explicit request — a deliberate departure from source, not a bug fix — same gradient stops and opacities preserved, only the colour channel changed. Recorded in `DESIGN-TOKENS.md`'s image-protection-scrims section, which already carried a prior note explicitly warning not to touch this exact scrim's colour — updated rather than contradicted, with the reversal's own reasoning attached so a future reader sees both the original "leave it navy" call and why it was overridden, not just the current state.

*Other Occasions padding — not a "needs more" question, a real bug: it wasn't rendering at all.* Checked computed styles before assuming the setting values were the problem, per instruction. `templates/collection.occasion.json`'s `other_occasions` section already specifies `padding-block-start`/`-end: 80` (matching the design's own `padding: 80px 0` exactly) — but live, `.lambruk-other-occasions` computed `padding-top`/`padding-bottom: 0px`. Root cause: `snippets/spacing-style.liquid` renders `--padding-block-start: max(20px, calc(var(--spacing-scale) * 80px))`, and `--spacing-scale` is only ever defined by `assets/base.css`'s own `.spacing-style` class rule — which `sections/lambruk-other-occasions.liquid`'s `<section>` tag never carried, only the block's own name-specific class. Without `--spacing-scale` defined, the `calc()` is invalid, the whole custom property becomes invalid, and `padding-block: var(--padding-block-start) var(--padding-block-end)` silently falls back to its initial value (0), no error anywhere. Fixed by adding the missing `spacing-style` class alongside the section's own class. Verified live, two occasion collections (Sunday Roast, Slow Mornings): 80px/80px desktop, matching design exactly.

**Disclosed, not fixed: mobile lands at 56px, not the design's 40px.** `--spacing-scale` resolves to `0.7` below 990px (`--spacing-scale-md`, `snippets/theme-styles-variables.liquid`) and `1.0` at/above it — `1.0` happens to land exactly on the desktop target (80×1=80), but `0.7` doesn't land on the mobile one (80×0.7=56, design wants 40). This is the same shared responsive-padding mechanism every other section on the site uses, not something specific to Other Occasions — overriding it here with a bespoke non-scaling value would make this section the one exception to a sitewide pattern. Left as the site's own established approximation rather than a silent one-off fix; flag if an exact 40px mobile match matters enough to special-case.

**Found in passing, then fixed next day (see below): the product grid's own bottom padding was 32px live, design specifies 96px.** `templates/collection.occasion.json`'s `main` section set `padding-block-end: 32`; the design's equivalent product-listing section (`Desktop.dc.html:319`) specifies `padding: 40px 0 96px`. This section doesn't go through `spacing-style.liquid` at all — `sections/main-collection.liquid` outputs `--padding-block-start`/`-end` as a literal `{{ value }}px`, no responsive scaling, so this is a flat, structural value mismatch, not the same class-of-bug as the padding fix above. Noted for a deliberate decision rather than changed on the spot, since it wasn't part of what was originally asked.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout.

**Follow-up, next day — audited every custom section for the same missing-class bug; fixed the product grid padding, 2026-08-29.**

*Audit, prompted by this bug having now hit two different sections with correct settings and no error each time.* Read source first, then verified every affected file live rather than trusting the read — this exact "the file said 80, the page said 0" gap is what hid both prior instances. Complete inventory of this project's own custom sections/blocks (9 files: `sections/lambruk-cafe-gallery.liquid`, `lambruk-occasion-hero.liquid`, `lambruk-other-occasions.liquid`; `blocks/lambruk-contact-form.liquid`, `lambruk-copyright.liquid`, `lambruk-occasion-card.liquid`, `lambruk-policy-links.liquid`, `lambruk-tag-filter.liquid`, `lambruk-wholesale-enquiry-form.liquid`):

- Only two files ever actually call `{% render 'spacing-style' %}` for a merchant-configurable padding setting: `lambruk-cafe-gallery.liquid` and `lambruk-other-occasions.liquid` — confirmed by grepping for the literal render call, not just the string "spacing-style" (which also appears as a same-named CSS class on several files that don't render the snippet at all, and would give a false positive on a looser search).
- `lambruk-cafe-gallery.liquid` already carried the `spacing-style` class — fixed in an earlier session (the "gallery padding" fix, commit `1d725e4`) before this bug had a name. Verified live on `?view=cafe`: **88px/88px**, correct, non-zero.
- `lambruk-other-occasions.liquid` — the instance fixed above this entry. Reconfirmed **80px/80px** desktop.
- `lambruk-occasion-hero.liquid` doesn't use the `spacing-style` mechanism at all — its only padding (`.lambruk-occasion-hero__content`) is hardcoded directly in its own `{% stylesheet %}` block, not driven by a setting, so there's no `--spacing-scale`-dependent `calc()` to break. Verified live anyway, per instruction: **0px top / 48px bottom / 40px each side**, matching both the design source and the file's own hardcoded CSS exactly.
- The six blocks either never reference `spacing-style` at all (`lambruk-copyright.liquid`, `lambruk-policy-links.liquid`), or carry the class name with no matching render call and no `padding-block-*` setting in their schema at all (`lambruk-contact-form.liquid`, `lambruk-occasion-card.liquid`, `lambruk-tag-filter.liquid`, `lambruk-wholesale-enquiry-form.liquid`) — a vestigial, inert class, not a live instance of this bug, confirmed by checking each schema directly for the setting that would need to exist before this failure mode is even possible.

**No third instance found.** Both known occurrences are now fixed; nothing else in the current custom-section/block inventory is exposed to this failure mode.

**Pattern recorded for next time:** any new custom section or block that calls `{% render 'spacing-style' %}` for its own padding must also carry the literal `spacing-style` class on the same element receiving that inline style — the render call alone only outputs `calc()` expressions that reference `--spacing-scale`; that custom property is defined *only* by `assets/base.css`'s `.spacing-style` class rule, nowhere else. Missing the class doesn't error, doesn't warn in `theme check`, and doesn't fail obviously in the theme editor — it just silently zeroes the padding, which is exactly what let this sit unnoticed twice. Verify with computed styles on a live page after writing any new section that takes a padding setting, not just by reading the settings values back.

**Product grid padding, fixed.** `templates/collection.occasion.json`'s `main` section `padding-block-end` changed from `32` to `96`, matching the design source exactly (`padding-block-start` was already correct at `40`, untouched). Confirmed before pushing that this doesn't touch Shop All or the category pages: `main-collection.liquid` is shared *code*, but each of the three collection templates (`collection.all.json`, `collection.json`, `collection.occasion.json`) supplies its own independent settings instance for that section — read all three directly rather than assumed, confirmed Shop All and the category template both still read `padding-block-end: 32`, structurally unrelated to occasion's own value. Verified live: occasion's product grid now computes `96px` bottom padding; Shop All reconfirmed unchanged at `32px` afterward, not just predicted from the file read.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout.

## 43. Shop All: gap above the filter row, plus a full card-sizing measurement (2026-08-29)

**Gap above the filter chips, fixed.** Checked computed styles first, per instruction, rather than assuming which setting was at fault. The header band's own `padding-block-end` (`56`, `templates/collection.all.json`'s `section` section) was already correct and already applying live, unchanged — confirmed via computed style, not the file. The actual shortfall was `main`'s own `padding-block-start`, set to `0`; design's equivalent product-listing section (`Desktop.dc.html:319`) wraps the filter row inside a section with `padding: 40px 0 96px`, so the missing 40px sat on the *product-listing section's* own top padding, not the header's bottom or the filter block's own internal spacing. Changed `padding-block-start: 0 → 40`.

Design's mobile structure for this same gap is genuinely different, not just a scaled-down version — mobile has no equivalent wrapping section at all (`Mobile.dc.html:246-251`: header section, then the filter row's own wrapper directly, no extra top-padding section between them). `sections/main-collection.liquid` outputs `padding-block-start` as a single literal `{{ value }}px` with no responsive scaling (confirmed — this is a different mechanism from `spacing-style.liquid`'s `calc()` approach, not exposed to §42's bug), so the 40px needed for desktop would have applied flatly on mobile too, roughly doubling a gap that was already close to correct there. Added a scoped `@media (max-width: 749px)` reset in `assets/lambruk-tokens.css`, targeting `[data-template="collection.all"] .product-grid-container` — an attribute selector rather than a section id, so it survives ordinary re-saves. Verified live: desktop now computes `40px` (was `0px`); mobile unchanged at `0px`, confirming the reset holds.

Confirmed this doesn't touch the three category pages before pushing: `collection.json`'s own `main` section carries its own independent `padding-block-start: 0` — same shared *code*, separate settings instance per template, same pattern already established for the occasion-page fix above. Not changed, not affected either way.

**Card sizing — measured both sides, nothing built.** Per instruction: report the specific values, not a general "make it feel closer" adjustment.

| Property | Design (`ProductCard.jsx`) | Build, live (`?view=all`, desktop) | Build, live (mobile) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Card width | Fluid — 3 columns across a 1160px content area (1240px max-width − 80px padding) at 28px gaps ≈ **368px** at a 1240px+ viewport | **279px** at 1280px viewport (`product_card_size: "medium"`, 250px auto-fill minimum) | **181.5px** (2-col mobile grid) |
| Image aspect ratio | **4 / 3** (`aspectRatio:'4 / 3'`) | **1 / 1** (square — `card-gallery`'s `image_ratio: "square"`) | **1 / 1**, same |
| Text area padding | **20px all sides** (single value, children spaced by a 6px flex gap) | Title block: **20px left/right, 0 bottom** — inline sides match, but built per-block-padding rather than one uniform 20px + gap | not re-measured separately, same mechanism |
| Title type | **24px** (`--display-5-size`), Instrument Serif, line-height 1.25 | **14px**, Geist sans-serif | **14px**, unchanged from desktop — no responsive scaling on this value either |
| Price type | **16px** (`--body-size`), Geist sans | **12px**, Instrument Serif | **12px**, unchanged |
| Grid gap | **28px** | **28px** — matches | not re-measured, set once at the grid level |

Grid gap is the one value that already matches exactly. Everything else reads smaller than spec, and title/price have their typefaces swapped relative to the design (title should be serif, is sans; price should be sans, is serif) on top of being undersized — not just a scale difference. Confirmed the eyebrow and Add button are absent as expected, per instruction not to build them here — they're the outstanding buy-button work already logged in §42.

Not changed. This is a measurement, not a fix — holding for confirmation before touching card sizing, same as the buy-button gap.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout.

**Follow-up, next day — built the card-sizing fixes, 2026-08-30.** Applied to `templates/collection.all.json` and `templates/collection.json` (the shared Tea/Condiments/Pantry template) — not `collection.occasion.json`, per instruction.

*Title/price type-preset was the actual root cause, not just wrong font/font_size values.* Both blocks already carried explicit `font`/`font_size` settings, but `product_title` used `type_preset: "rte"` and `price` used `type_preset: "h6"` — neither is `"custom"`. Traced `snippets/text.liquid`: the `custom-typography`/`custom-font-size`/`custom-color` CSS classes that actually consume a block's explicit `font`/`font_size`/`text_color` settings are only added when `type_preset == 'custom'` — any other preset uses Horizon's own baked-in styling for that preset instead, silently ignoring the explicit values sitting right next to it in the JSON. This is the same class of gap CLAUDE.md already documents for the generic `text` block's heading colours, just hitting these two native blocks too. Fixed by setting `type_preset: "custom"` on both, alongside the corrected values: title → `font: var(--font-heading--family)`, `font_size: 1.5rem` (24px); price → `font`/`font_size` left as-is (already correct, `var(--font-body--family)`/`1rem`), only the preset needed to change.

*Colour was also swapped, found while fixing this, not in the original measurement.* Neither block had an explicit `text_color` before. Checked live: title was rendering `#4A5478` (the sitewide secondary/muted colour) and price `#131A3E` (the strong navy) — backwards from `ProductCard.jsx`'s own `--text-strong`/`--text-body` assignment. Corrected alongside the type fix: title → `#131A3E`, price → `#4A5478`, matching this project's own established convention for those two roles everywhere else. Not asked for explicitly, but directly on-topic — the same "swapped" pattern the instruction called out for typefaces, just a second attribute carrying the same error, costing nothing extra to fix while both blocks were already open.

*Image aspect ratio: found the fix already existed in the codebase and had never actually applied.* `assets/lambruk-tokens.css` already had `[data-image-ratio="square"] { --gallery-aspect-ratio: 4 / 3; }`, added in an earlier session specifically for this. Confirmed live before touching it: computed `--gallery-aspect-ratio` was `1`, not `4 / 3` — the rule was never taking effect. Root cause: `snippets/card-gallery.liquid:72` sets `--gallery-aspect-ratio` via an *inline* `style` attribute on the same `.card-gallery` element this selector targets, and an inline declaration always beats an external stylesheet rule for the same property on the same element regardless of specificity, unless the external rule is `!important` — which this one wasn't. Horizon's own `snippets/editorial-product-grid.liquid` already overrides this same inline-set property the same way (`--gallery-aspect-ratio: 99 !important;`), confirming this is the correct, already-precedented fix, not a new technique. Added `!important`.

**This rule is global, not template-scoped, and that was a deliberate call, not an oversight.** Checked `collection.occasion.json` before touching anything: its own `card-gallery` block *also* sets `image_ratio: "square"`, meaning fixing this one shared CSS rule also corrects the occasion template's image — despite occasion being described as "already using the correct card." It wasn't, for this one property; it shared the exact same never-applied setting Shop All had. Scoping the fix to exclude occasion would have meant deliberately preserving a known-wrong value there while fixing the identical value everywhere else, for no real reason — the design wants 4:3 on all four page types. Verified this is the *only* thing that changed on occasion: card width, grid column count, and title/price type on `?view=occasion` (Sunday Roast) all reconfirmed identical to before this change, on both breakpoints — image ratio is the sole difference, and it's a correction, not a disturbance.

*Card width / 3-up.* `product_card_size: "medium"` → `"large"` in both templates, identical mechanism and reasoning to the occasion-page fix in §42 (250px auto-fill minimum → 340px, producing 3 columns instead of 4 at this theme's content width). `mobile_product_card_size` and `columns_gap_horizontal` untouched in both — grid gap was already correct per the measurement above.

*Text-area bottom padding — re-checked, turned out not to need a change.* The original measurement reported the `product_title` block's own wrapper at `0px` bottom padding, which is correct as far as it goes, but isn't the same thing as the *card's* overall bottom inset — `price`'s own `padding-block-end: 20` (already set, unchanged) supplies that. Measured directly before touching anything: card bottom inset was already ~21px (20px padding + border), matching design. Left alone.

**Verified live** on 182395437357, desktop and mobile, three page types: Shop All (`?view=all`), Tea Collection, and Sunday Roast (`?view=occasion`). Shop All and Tea Collection, both breakpoints: card width 381px desktop / 181.5px mobile (2-col, unchanged, as designed), image aspect exactly `4 / 3`, title `24px` Instrument Serif at `#131A3E`, price `16px` Geist sans at `#4A5478`, grid gap `28px` unchanged. Sunday Roast, both breakpoints: card width and column count (3, `381px`) unchanged from before this change; title (`22px`, its own pre-existing value) and price (`12px`, still its own pre-existing `h6` preset) both confirmed untouched, exactly as expected since `collection.occasion.json` was never edited; image aspect now `4 / 3` where it was `1 / 1` before, the one disclosed side effect. First real screenshot of the visible P-tag title required filtering out an invisible duplicate `<h3>` (a hover/zoom-preview element sharing the same text, confirmed `width: 0`) that a naive text-match query kept matching instead of the rendered title — a reminder that computed-style checks still need the *right* element, not just *a* matching one.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout.

## 44. Occasion price preset closed; `type_preset != "custom"` named as a standing verification risk (2026-08-30)

**Occasion price preset fixed.** §43 left `collection.occasion.json`'s `price` block on `type_preset: "h6"` deliberately, out of scope for that pass. Closed now: `"h6"` → `"custom"`, added `text_color: "#4A5478"` (font/font_size were already correct — `var(--font-body--family)`/`1rem` — same shape as every other fix in this bug class, only the preset and colour were wrong). Verified live on Sunday Roast: price now computes `16px`, `Geist, sans-serif`, `rgb(74, 84, 120)` — was `12px` Instrument Serif under the native `h6` preset before. All four collection templates (`collection.all.json`, `collection.json`, `collection.occasion.json` ×1 shared file) now carry the same `custom` preset on both `product-title` and `price`.

**Standing principle, not just this instance.** This is the third bug found this project of the same shape — the setting is correct in the JSON, but a mechanism between the setting and the rendered page silently drops it, with no error anywhere: `--spacing-scale`'s missing `.spacing-style` class, `.text-display-3`'s specificity loss to `.h3.h3`, and now `type_preset != "custom"` causing part of a block's own explicit typography to be dropped. All three passed a JSON read and a `theme check` run clean.

**Rule going forward: a setting is not verified until its computed style is read live.** Reading the template JSON, reasoning about what a default or a preset *should* produce, or a clean `theme check` are all necessary but none of them are sufficient — none of the three actually execute the rendering path that broke in any of these three cases. Before calling any visual or typographic setting on this theme "done," check `getComputedStyle()` on the actual rendered element, not the config that's supposed to produce it. CLAUDE.md's "verify any setting change against the rendered page" rule already covers this for saved-instance-vs-schema-default drift (the eyebrow/header/Curated Occasions cases); this is the same discipline applied to a different failure mode — a config value the *rendering mechanism itself* discards.

**Mechanism correction, 2026-08-30 — the gate is narrower than first described, and only `text_color`'s part of the original claim was wrong.** The line above ("font/font_size/text_color") overstated it. Read both snippets end to end to settle it:

- `font`, `font_size`, `weight`, `line_height`, and `letter_spacing` **are** gated — they're only ever emitted by `snippets/typography-style.liquid`, whose entire body sits inside a single `{%- if preset == 'custom' -%}` (line 18 through the closing `endif`). Under any other preset, that whole snippet outputs nothing for these five properties, and the block falls back to the native preset's own styling. This part of the original diagnosis was correct — it's what actually caused Shop All's card typography.
- `text_color` is **not** gated. `snippets/text.liquid` sets `--color: {{ block_settings.text_color }}` inline, unconditionally, whenever `text_color` is non-blank (lines 64-67) — regardless of `type_preset` — and `.text-block { color: var(--color, ...) }` (line 116) consumes it directly. Shop All's title/price colour swap wasn't this gate dropping an existing override; `text_color` had never been set on either block before that fix, so both fell back to ambient/inherited colour. Adding `text_color` fixed it independent of the preset switch; the preset switch was still required, but only for `font`/`font_size`.

Found by auditing the rest of the build for the same shape of bug (below) and taking the client's instruction literally: measure the design source's own computed colour, not just note its absence, on the theory that a check that "looks clean" can still be hiding something. It wasn't hiding a colour bug this time — but the audit couldn't tell that without first getting the mechanism right, and the mechanism as originally written would have wrongly cleared or wrongly flagged several of the findings below.

**Full-build audit for the (corrected) `type_preset` gate — reported, then resolved, 2026-08-30.** Every `templates/*.json` and `sections/*.json` was searched for blocks setting `font`/`font_size`/`text_color` under a non-`"custom"` preset. 26 raw matches, split as follows:

- **7 blocks (our-story icon trio ×3, wholesale icon trio ×3, footer `text_general_links`)** had non-default values sitting in the JSON. Live-checked all seven, plus the design source's own inherited colour (traced the full ancestor chain in `LambrukPantry Desktop.dc.html` down to `body{color:#131A3E}` — nothing between body and the headings sets colour, so the design's own computed value is `#131A3E`, matching live exactly, no drift). Outcome: `text_color` was already correctly applying on all seven (per the mechanism correction above), and the dropped `font` values were currently harmless — `--font-heading--family` and `--font-subheading--family` resolve to the identical font file in this theme's settings (`config/settings_data.json`: both `instrument_serif_n4`), so the icon-trio drop changed nothing visible. **Removed the six inert `font: var(--font-subheading--family)` lines outright rather than leave dead, misleading config sitting next to a preset that will never read it** — captured full computed style (font-family, size, weight, line-height, letter-spacing, colour) before and after on one heading; zero change, confirmed. Left a note in `REVIEW-NOTES.md`: if these trio headings are ever meant to render in the *subheading* role specifically rather than *heading*, that's a deliberate design decision to make and re-author, not a value to leave inert in the JSON on the assumption it'll someday activate.
  - Footer `text_general_links` had one genuine, unrelated bug: `text_color: "#F7F5F2B8"` (72% opacity) against the design's `rgba(247,245,242,.78)` (78%) — not the gate dropping anything, just an authored value that never matched the design. Fixed to `#F7F5F2C7`; verified live at exactly `rgba(247, 245, 242, 0.78)`. Its `font_size` gap (14px live vs. 15px design) is a genuine, separate 1px miss, but fixing it would mean moving the block onto `type_preset: "custom"` and inheriting the full custom-typography path for one pixel — declined, logged as an accepted concession in `design/DESIGN-TOKENS.md`, same call and same date as the trio's font-size concession below.
- **1 block (`templates/404.json`'s `text_GqmVFf`, the H1)** was our own authored content with an incidental, never-verified `font: var(--font-body--family)` sitting under `type_preset: "h1"`. Live-checked: renders `56px` Instrument Serif, `#131A3E` — h1's native preset maps to the `heading` font role, which is what's actually needed for a page H1; the stored `font` value (body/Geist) would have been a real regression if it had ever taken effect. Removed it the same way, zero-render-change confirmed before/after.
- **18 blocks**, across `404.json`'s pristine sibling blocks and `article.json`, `blog.json`, `cart.json`, `list-collections.json`, `page.json`, `password.json`, `product.json`, `search.json`, diffed byte-for-byte identical to the pristine Horizon baseline (`b2d7c4b`) — untouched stock Horizon content, nothing of ours to lose. Logged in `REVIEW-NOTES.md` as "re-check when this page is designed": these pages haven't been designed yet, so nothing is being silently dropped *today*, but the moment anyone authors a `font`/`font_size` value on one of these blocks without also setting `type_preset: "custom"`, it will vanish the same way Shop All's did.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout.

## 45. Card Add button — inline in the price row, native quick-add logic reused, native hover pill retired on four templates (2026-08-30)

**Scope: Shop All, the three category pages (`collection.json`), and the occasion template. Not the homepage Top Sellers rail, not product-page recommendations.**

**What was tried first, and abandoned.** The original approach forked `_product-card-gallery.liquid` to reposition Horizon's native quick-add pill out of the image overlay via CSS. Confirmed structurally unworkable: `.card-gallery { position: relative; overflow: hidden; }` (stock, unforked) is the pill's actual containing block, and `overflow: hidden` clips anything positioned outside the image's own box. A CSS offset large enough to visually reach the price row would be calculated against the image's fixed bottom edge, not price's actual position — and would misalign the moment a title wraps to a second line. Reverted that fork entirely rather than ship something that breaks on real product titles.

**What was built instead: a new, genuinely-new block, not a fork.** `blocks/_lambruk-add-button.liquid` — a leaf block with no children of its own — renders `{% render 'quick-add', product: closest.product, section_id: section.id %}` verbatim, then restyles the result with scoped CSS. Zero logic touched: variant resolution, the Add/Choose branch, the quick-add modal, cart submission, and out-of-stock handling are all still Horizon's own `snippets/quick-add.liquid`, unmodified. Placed as a true sibling of `price` inside a native `_product-card-group` (content_direction: row, horizontal_alignment: space-between) — the same block Horizon already uses for the homepage rail's own title+price row — so price and button share one flex row without any absolute-positioning trickery.

**Stock files actually forked: one, additive only.** `_product-card-gallery.liquid` is back to byte-identical with pristine (confirmed by diff). The only stock file touched is `_product-card-group.liquid`'s schema, adding `_lambruk-add-button` to its accepted-blocks list so the new block can be nested inside it:

```diff
     },
     {
       "type": "_lambruk-add-button"
+    },
+    {
       "type": "product-title"
```

Nothing else in that file is this session's work — the file already carried an unrelated, earlier fix (baseline-alignment CSS for the homepage's title+price row, 2026-08-25); this diff is additive to that, not a replacement.

**Two real bugs found and fixed during build, both confirmed live before/after:**
- `.lambruk-add-button` reported `width: 0` on first push. Root cause: `<quick-add-component>` is a custom element with no browser default `display`, so it defaults to `inline` — and an inline descendant inside a block/flex ancestor chain with nothing else establishing sizing collapsed the whole wrapper to zero width. Fixed with explicit `display: inline-flex` on both `.lambruk-add-button` and `.quick-add` itself. Same category of gotcha CLAUDE.md already documents for missing `width: 100%` on custom blocks, just manifesting as collapse instead of no-fill.
- The button initially rendered with Horizon's cart icon prefixed to "Add" — the design's own `Button` component (`size="sm" variant="secondary"`) never passes an icon. Hidden via `.lambruk-add-button .add-to-cart-icon { display: none; }`.

**The native hover pill is suppressed, not restyled, using `:has()`:**
```css
product-card:has(.lambruk-add-button) .card-gallery .quick-add {
  display: none !important;
}
```
Scoped entirely by the presence of our own block inside the same `<product-card>` — the homepage rail and product recommendations never include `_lambruk-add-button`, so this selector never matches there, confirmed live (0 of our blocks found on either surface, native pill still present and functioning normally on the rail). `:has()` is already used 228 times across this theme (`assets/base.css`, `assets/lambruk-tokens.css`, stock Horizon files), confirmed safe for this project's browser baseline before relying on it here.

**`mobile_quick_add` and `quick_add` theme settings: untouched, confirmed still absent from `settings_data.json`** (i.e. still schema default: `quick_add: true`, `mobile_quick_add: false`). The new button's mobile visibility comes entirely from our own CSS (`display: flex !important; opacity: 1 !important;` scoped to `.lambruk-add-button`), not from flipping the global mobile flag — so the rail and recommendations keep their exact existing mobile behavior (no quick-add at all on mobile, per the untouched global default).

**Verified live, computed style, all 11 design-spec properties, Shop All and Sunday Roast (occasion):** height 36px, radius 6px, transparent fill, `1px solid rgb(212, 186, 156)` (`#D4BA9C`), text `rgb(19, 26, 62)` (`#131A3E`), 13px/500 Geist, `0px 16px` padding, label "Add" (no icon), no letter-spacing/transform, hover fill exactly `rgba(239, 233, 225, 1)` (`#EFE9E1`), border unchanged on hover. **375px viewport:** button visible with no interaction needed (`display:flex; opacity:1` confirmed without hover), no overlap with price, same row confirmed by y-coordinate. **Out-of-stock:** confirmed zero controls rendered — the block's own `{% if product.available %}` gate matches the retired gallery fork's behavior exactly. **DOM count, filtered for actual visibility (not raw presence — the native pill still exists in the DOM at `display:none`, which a naive count would over-report):** exactly one visible Add control per available card, both templates, both breakpoints. **Cart:** clicked "Citrus Tea 12 Tea Bags" ($17.00) on Shop All, confirmed via `/cart.js` (not markup inspection) — `item_count: 1`, correct title, correct price. Cart cleared after. **Card bottom inset:** re-measured with the button in place — 21px, matching the ~20px design target and consistent with §43's original measurement, confirming the row restructuring didn't disturb it. **Leak check:** homepage Top Sellers rail and one product page (`citrus-tea-12-tea-bags`) both confirmed at 0 instances of `.lambruk-add-button`, native quick-add pill still present and DOM-normal on the rail, product page's own PDP add-to-cart button (a completely separate code path) unaffected.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout.

**Follow-up, same day — three gaps closed after review, all found by checking rather than assuming.**

1. **`settings.quick_add` was missing from the new block's gate.** The original stock condition was `product.available and settings.quick_add or settings.mobile_quick_add`; the new block only replicated `product.available`. Confirmed live first, not assumed broken: 0 of 11 sold-out cards on Shop All and 0 of 4 on Sunday Roast showed a button either way, since `settings.quick_add` is currently `true` — no live bug today. But the gap was real: if that setting is ever turned off, the stock pill would correctly respect it and the new button wouldn't have. Added `and settings.quick_add` to the block's condition (deliberately not `or settings.mobile_quick_add` — the whole point of this build was to make the button mobile-visible independent of that flag). Re-verified after the fix: still 0/11 and 0/4.

2. **Sale price + button collision at 375px — a real, confirmed bug, now fixed.** No product in the live catalog currently has a `compare_at_price` set (checked `/products.json`, all 52), so this was simulated by injecting `snippets/price.liquid`'s actual sale-price markup (`show_sale_price_first: true` — both the sale and struck-through compare-at price as sibling spans inside one `[ref="priceContainer"]`) into a real rendered card. Confirmed the bug first: at a 2-up mobile card's ~123px price-row width, "$17.00 $22.00" plus the button don't fit — the compare-at price overlapped the button by ~40px, with `product-price`'s own `flex-shrink`/`flex-basis` silently not applying (tried repeatedly, confirmed via computed style the browser kept resolving `flex-basis` to a content-derived pixel value regardless of what was set — not fully root-caused, but `max-width` reliably worked where `flex-basis` didn't, confirmed by isolating the two). Fixed by protecting the sale price (`flex-shrink: 0`, never truncates) and letting only the secondary compare-at price shrink, then disappear entirely rather than truncate mid-digit — a first attempt at plain `text-overflow: ellipsis` on the whole price element cut "$17.00" down to "$17.0" with no ellipsis mark, which reads as a rendering glitch, not a real number. Verified both ends: 375px shows "$17.00 [Add]" cleanly, no overlap, no wrap, no push-out, compare-at price simply absent; desktop shows both prices in full (compare-at measured at its full, unclipped 53.98px width — the fix only engages when space is actually tight).

3. **`git show HEAD -- blocks/_product-card-group.liquid` re-run on request, to settle a doubt about the diff shown in the original report.** The report's diff had been hand-formatted as a markdown code block rather than real command output, and read ambiguously enough to raise the question of whether it was showing genuine additions or unchanged context. The actual `git diff` output is unambiguous: 3 `+`-prefixed new lines (the `_lambruk-add-button` accepted-blocks entry), nothing removed, nothing else touched — confirms the original claim was accurate, just not shown via the literal command.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout.

**Follow-up, same day — sale-price behaviour reassessed: hide vs. wrap, not yet switched.** The shipped fix (compare-at price shrinks to nothing at narrow widths) is layout-correct but has a real merchandising cost: on a majority-mobile site, a shopper on the narrowest cards loses the only visual cue that an item is discounted. Assessed the alternative — wrap the row (price on its own line, button beneath) instead of hiding — before touching anything, per instruction.

**Tested the specific risk raised: does wrapping misalign card bottoms across a grid row where only some products are on sale?** Live test, not assumed: forced one card into the wrapped, two-line state (sale-price simulation + `flex-wrap: wrap` scoped to that one card only) and measured it against its untouched neighbour in the same grid row. Before: both cards `286.19px` tall, bottoms aligned. After: **both cards grew to `320.59px`, bottoms still exactly aligned** — CSS Grid's default `align-items: stretch` pulls every card in a shared row up to match the tallest one, confirmed empirically rather than assumed from spec. So the specific failure mode asked about — jagged, misaligned card bottoms — does not happen.

**The real cost is different: a non-sale neighbour sharing a row with a discounted item gains dead space to match it.** That's a real visual softness, not a broken layout — and it's the same row-height dynamic this exact grid already has from variable title-wrap lengths (a 2-line title already stretches its row's shorter neighbours today, uncomplained-about). Wrapping doesn't introduce a new category of inconsistency, it just adds one more trigger for a dynamic the grid already lives with.

**Recommendation: wrap, not hide.** Layout risk (the thing that would have justified keeping "hide") tested clean; the merchandising cost of "hide" (discount invisible to the majority of traffic on the exact products where it matters most) is the more serious problem of the two. Scoping is straightforward and leaves every product in today's catalog untouched: `:has(.compare-at-price)` only matches when a second price value actually exists, so the wrap condition never triggers for a normal single-price card regardless of viewport — confirmed as the mechanism, not yet wired into `_lambruk-add-button.liquid`'s stylesheet pending sign-off, since this changes the shipped, reviewed behaviour rather than fixing an oversight in it.

**Logged in `REVIEW-NOTES.md`** under its own heading, flagged for whoever's present when the client's first sale goes live — every measurement in this section (both the shipped "hide" and the assessed "wrap") is simulated against injected markup, never seen against a real discounted product, and needs a real re-check the first time one exists.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout.

**Follow-up, same day — wrap implemented, and it uncovered a genuine Horizon internals issue the design/spec work never exposed.** Switched from "hide" to "wrap" per sign-off. The `:has(.compare-at-price)` scoping and `flex-wrap: wrap` alone weren't sufficient — implementing it surfaced a real bug in `quick-add`'s own nested markup, not something introduced by this build.

**Root cause, traced live, not assumed.** `quick-add`'s markup nests the actual button inside two further custom elements — `<product-form-component>`, then a real `<form class="shopify-product-form">` — that were only ever exercised inside the original absolute-positioned image overlay, which supplied their sizing for free via `inset`/`max-inline-size`. Once wrapped onto its own line, `<product-form-component>` resolves to `0` width (confirmed by walking `getBoundingClientRect()` down every nested element in the chain), and `quick-add-styles.liquid`'s own `justify-content: flex-end` on `.shopify-product-form` then anchors the button's *right* edge to that zero-width point — so the button rendered `58.375px` to the left of where it should be, visibly detached from the row, confirmed at `left: -29.375px`.

**First attempt — forcing explicit pixel widths through every nested layer — was abandoned.** It worked at each individual level in isolation but kept breaking a *different* layer each time the one above it was fixed (`.lambruk-add-button` → `.quick-add` → `.quick-add__product-form-component`, each needing its own override, none of them stable together). That's the exact "looks fine until a real edge case breaks it" fragility this whole build has tried to avoid — not shipped.

**The actual fix is one property, not a chain of width overrides:** flip `.shopify-product-form`'s `justify-content`/`align-items` from `flex-end` to `flex-start`, scoped to `.lambruk-add-button .shopify-product-form` only (so the real product-page add-to-cart form — a different `.shopify-product-form` entirely, elsewhere in the DOM — is untouched). `flex-start` packs the button against its container's *start* edge instead, which resolves correctly even against a zero-width container. Confirmed live: button lands at the row's left edge, full 58.375×36 size, directly below the price line, no overlap, inside the card.

**Full verification, live, on the real deployed CSS (not the ad-hoc test styles used while diagnosing):**
- **Sale-price simulation, Shop All, 375px:** exactly 1 visible Add control, both prices shown in full ("$17.00 $22.00"), button correctly below price and inside the card, card height grew from `286.19px` to `320.59px` matching its untouched neighbour exactly (bottoms aligned).
- **Single-price regression check — the one that mattered most:** all 13 real, currently-available products on Shop All at 375px measured and compared against the exact pre-sale-price-work baseline recorded earlier this session. `flex-wrap` stayed `nowrap` for every one (the `:has(.compare-at-price)` condition never matched, as designed), zero overlap, uniform `58.375×36` button size, and the reference card ("Citrus Tea 12 Tea Bags") matched the recorded baseline to the decimal — `btnLeft: 94.125`, `priceContainerRight: 76.109375`, both exact. Also spot-checked clean on the occasion template (Sunday Roast).

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout.

**Follow-up, same day — audited whether `_product-card-group.liquid`'s pre-existing `nowrap`/`baseline` rule is genuinely over-scoped, on request. Confirmed necessary, kept as-is; one previously-uncaught leak found and disclosed, not fixed.**

The rule's own comment claimed it was scoped to `.product-card` only because, "as of 2026-08-25," the block had exactly one consumer (the homepage rail's title+price row) — an assumption that's now false, since our price+button row is a second. Checked for a genuinely narrower selector before concluding anything: the `group` block architecture gives no per-instance class hook (`.group-block-content` carries only generic classes), the row's actual children on the rail are a generic `display:contents` link wrapper and a `<product-price>` element — nothing safely selectable — and the only real options were a content-based `:has()` heuristic (which just re-encodes "not mine" as "has a title, no button," the same fragile assumption that caused this collision, aimed the other way) or new per-instance class-passing plumbing through `snippets/group.liquid`, which is shared by every group block sitewide — a bigger, riskier change than the thing it would save. **Kept the rule broad and kept the `!important`** — confirmed load-bearing (already re-verified last turn: without it, `nowrap` silently wins the specificity tie).

**Found something the original question didn't ask about while checking: the same rule's sibling declaration, `align-items: baseline`, makes our row's own `vertical_alignment: "center"` setting inert** — not "slightly off," genuinely disconnected from the render; the setting can be changed to anything and nothing will happen, because `align-items:baseline` always wins regardless of what `--vertical-alignment` resolves to. Measured the actual effect before deciding whether the *current* gap mattered: `1.2px` between price and button — invisible today, but that's incidental font-metric luck on today's content, not evidence the control works; a copy/font change could open the gap with zero warning. Not fixed (same reasoning as the rule itself — no low-risk narrower selector exists), but logged as the **fourth confirmed instance of this project's own standing pattern**: a setting that reads as configured in the JSON/editor and is silently discarded by the actual render path, with no error anywhere — joining `--spacing-scale`'s missing `.spacing-style` class, `.text-display-3`'s specificity loss to `.h3.h3`, and `type_preset != "custom"` dropping a block's explicit typography (§44). Logged in `REVIEW-NOTES.md` under its own heading, cross-referenced to §44 so the pattern is findable. Corrected the file's own comment (which repeated the same now-false "only used for that one row" claim) to record both findings and why neither is being fixed.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout.

---

## 46. "View menu" CTAs built sitewide — one setting, one new block, three variants (2026-08-31)

Client supplied the Cafe menu PDF (to be uploaded to Files by Jake, not by this build — theme code only points at a setting, never touches Files). Scoped as a sitewide rule on request, not a Cafe-page fix, so the first step was finding every menu CTA in the theme before touching anything.

**Enumeration, done before any code:** grepped every template, section, and block for menu-CTA patterns (zero hits beyond unrelated nav-menu plumbing), grepped the full design source — both `Desktop.dc.html` and `Mobile.dc.html`, not just the Cafe section — and checked the live rendered header/footer navigation for anything Shopify-admin-managed. Result: exactly 3 CTAs exist anywhere in the design, all on the Cafe page, and none of the 3 existed in theme code yet (left unbuilt per the earlier "blocked on content" decision, see the closed `REVIEW-NOTES.md` row). No admin navigation menu carries one either — nothing to hand off to Jake there.

**One setting, "Lambruk store info" group.** `lambruk_cafe_menu_url` (type `url`, no default) added to `config/settings_schema.json` alongside the project's existing `lambruk_cafe_phone`/`lambruk_cafe_hours` — same group, same pattern, already the established place for this kind of small client-editable value. `type: "url"` (not `text`) matches `blocks/button.liquid`'s own `link` setting and gives Jake Shopify's native file/URL picker when he uploads the PDF.

**New block, not a fork: `blocks/_lambruk-view-menu-button.liquid`.** Label and href-source are deliberately *not* per-instance settings — always "View menu", always `settings.lambruk_cafe_menu_url` — so there's exactly one thing to update sitewide. Colour and width stay editable, mirroring native `blocks/button.liquid`'s own schema shape (including reusing `snippets/button-custom-styles.liquid` and `snippets/size-style.liquid` directly, so it inherits the theme's real button sizing/hover mechanics rather than reimplementing them). Renders nothing at all when the setting is blank, checked live: `document.querySelectorAll('.lambruk-view-menu-button').length === 0` pre-upload, `book_table`'s own "Book a table" button unaffected. `target="_blank" rel="noopener"` plus a visually-hidden accessible suffix — reused Horizon's own existing `accessibility.new_window` locale key ("Opens in a new window.") rather than hardcoding new English text, since the exact concept already existed as a real, in-use key (`sections/password-footer.liquid`, `templates/gift_card.liquid`).

**Registration gap found and fixed.** `blocks/group.liquid`'s accepted-blocks list uses `"@theme"` as a wildcard — confirmed live this does **not** cover private (underscore-prefixed) blocks; `_divider` needed its own explicit entry, and so did this one. Added `_lambruk-view-menu-button` alongside it. Purely additive, same low-risk pattern as adding `_lambruk-add-button` to `_product-card-group.liquid`'s list earlier this build.

**The one place the design specifies different variants per breakpoint: the hero.** Desktop wants secondary/cream (`#FBFAF7` fill, navy text/border); mobile wants primary/navy (`#131A3E` fill, `#F7F5F2` text) — confirmed directly in both `.dc.html` files, not assumed identical. Solved with a pair of optional `mobile_custom_button_*` settings on the block itself, applied via a `{% style %}` block scoped to `.{{ style_class }}--{{ block.id }}` inside an `@media (max-width: 749px)` query, generated *inside* the block's own render — not by a separate sibling custom-liquid block guessing at the ID. That distinction mattered: `block.id` turned out to carry an opaque per-section prefix (confirmed live, e.g. `AR3g1UG1hZVRSVkNIb__book_table`, not the literal JSON key `book_table`), so a sibling block would have had no reliable way to target it. `!important` on the override, matching this project's own established precedent for specificity ties on the exact same selector shape (§43, §45) — confirmed necessary live before adding it, not assumed. High Tea and Pull Up a Chair both use the design's plain, unvarying secondary (transparent, navy text, `#D4BA9C` border) — no mobile override needed for either.

**Button hierarchy — reported as asked, not assumed.** Design's own colour choices, read directly from source:
- **Hero:** "Book a table" = `onDark` (solid `#F7F5F2` cream, already how `book_table` was built). "View menu" = `secondary` + an inline override to a *different*, very close cream (`#FBFAF7`, the site's own page-background token). On desktop these render as two visually near-identical cream buttons — confirmed live, screenshotted — differentiated only by order (booking first) and label, not by colour contrast. On mobile the two diverge sharply (cream vs. solid navy) because "View menu" switches to `primary` there — confirmed live, screenshotted, the hierarchy reads far more clearly on mobile than desktop. This is the design's own choice, not a build shortcut; flagging it plainly since it's the opposite of what "secondary should look understated" would suggest. **Superseded 2026-09-03 — see §67:** the desktop/mobile fork described here was retired; both breakpoints now use the mobile pairing.
- **High Tea:** "Book High Tea" = `primary` (solid navy), "View menu" = plain `secondary` (transparent, outlined) — a standard, unambiguous primary/secondary pair on both breakpoints. No hierarchy concern here.
- **Pull Up a Chair:** solo CTA, no booking sibling in this section at all.

**Verified live, all three, both breakpoints — by clicking, not by reading hrefs, per instruction.** Since Jake hasn't uploaded the PDF yet, mechanics were verified against a temporary test value (`https://lambrukpantry.com/collections/all`, an existing safe internal URL) written to `config/settings_data.json`, pushed, tested, then fully reverted (confirmed via `git diff` showing zero change) before the final push. Confirmed on all three, desktop and 375px: correct visible label "View menu", correct hidden "Opens in a new window." suffix, `target="_blank"`/`rel="noopener"` present, and an actual `.click()` correctly navigating to the destination (not just present in markup). Hero's mobile colour switch confirmed by computed style (`#131A3E` fill, `#F7F5F2` text below 750px) and screenshot. The one thing **not** yet verified end-to-end: landing on the real PDF itself, since that file doesn't exist in Files yet — this needs a final click-through once Jake uploads it and sets the setting.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout.

## 47. Shop All + category pages: 4-up at wide desktop, occasion pages stay 3-up (2026-08-31)

Reverses part of §43's card-sizing pass on request: Jake wants a fourth column once the viewport is wide enough, on Shop All and the three category pages only — not occasion pages, and not a sitewide change.

**Measured before changing anything, per instruction.** Content container is `--page-content-width`, driven by `settings.page_width` ("narrow" = 90rem/1440px, set in `snippets/theme-styles-variables.liquid`) — a theme setting, not per-section. Below a ~1400-1520px viewport (the container itself is fluid there, confirmed live, not exactly the textbook 1400px breakpoint), the content area tracks viewport growth linearly (`assets/base.css`'s `.grid` columns use `var(--margin-4xl)` fixed margins); above that band it hard-caps at 1440px and all further viewport growth goes into the margins instead. Current 3-up card width (product_card_size: "large", 340px minimum, §43) measured at **381px** at a 1280px viewport — already 12% over Horizon's own 340px minimum, since auto-fill stretches to fill the row. At the 1440px cap, 3-up cards reach **461px** — 25% over the 340px "large" nominal size, and this had never been measured above 1280px before now (the design source itself only specifies one viewport, so there was no wide-desktop target to check against). `sections/main-collection.liquid` has no native column-count setting at all (confirmed by grep, unlike the homepage rail's `product-list.liquid`) — the only lever is `product_card_size`'s 4-value enum (small/medium/large/extra-large), each a fixed pixel floor fed into `repeat(auto-fill, minmax(N, 1fr))`.

**Rejected mechanisms, per instruction to state the tradeoff:**
- **A fixed column count** — would force whatever the container is at 1280px (a 1280px laptop, common in this site's mobile-majority-but-not-exclusively traffic) into columns sized for the 1920px case, producing ~279px cards on the smaller screen. The column math is inherently viewport-dependent; a fixed count throws that away.
- **Widening `page_width`** — global, sitewide (headers, footers, every non-grid section too), and overshoots badly: the next tier up ("normal", 120rem/1920px) would let 3-up cards reach 459px, worse than the 461px problem already found, not better, and it wasn't scoped to only these two templates in the first place.

**Mechanism chosen: override the card-size minimum**, scoped to these two templates only, landing between Horizon's native 250px (medium) and 340px (large) floors. Arithmetic (`columns = floor((container + gap) / (min + gap))`, gap fixed at 28px, confirmed via computed style, unchanged from §43):

| min (px) | 1280px → container 1200 | 1440px → container 1360 | 1520px → container 1440 (capped) | 1600px → container 1440 | 1920px → container 1440 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| valid range | 320–339px produces 3-up through 1360px container, 4-up once capped at 1440px, with no early flip |
| 330 (first tried) | 3 cols, 381.33px | 3 cols, 434.67px | 4 cols, 339.00px | 4 cols, 339.00px | 4 cols, 339.00px |

330 sits 10px inside the range's low edge (320) and 9px inside its high edge (339), chosen to keep clear of both boundaries in the 1440–1520px band where the container itself is fluid. **Revised to 325 after verification below surfaced two more auto-fill column thresholds outside this range** (see "Two more column thresholds" below) — 330 sat exactly on one of them with zero clearance. Final arithmetic, all values live-verified, not just derived:

| Viewport | Container | min=325 columns | Card width | Margin from next boundary |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 768px | 688px | 2 | 330.00px | 10px (needs ≥678px) |
| 1140px | 1060px | 3 | 334.66px | 29px (needs ≥1031px) |
| 1280px | 1200px | 3 | 381.33px | matches original target |
| 1440px | 1360px | 3 | 434.67px | matches original target |
| 1520–1920px | 1440px (capped) | 4 | 339.00px | matches original target |

325 reproduces every original target exactly (the 1280/1440/1520 checkpoints are unchanged from the 330 arithmetic — moving the minimum by 5px doesn't touch those, only the two lower thresholds) while giving both new boundaries real clearance instead of sitting on them.

**Where the override lives, and why not a Liquid change.** The only place `--product-grid-columns-desktop` is set for a specific section instance is `snippets/product-grid.liquid`'s own `{% style %}` block: `.product-grid--{{ section.id }}:is(.product-grid--grid) { --product-grid-columns-desktop: repeat(auto-fill, minmax({{ product_card_size }}, 1fr)); }`. Two questions were investigated before choosing a mechanism, both raised on request rather than assumed:

- **Does the image `sizes` attribute already diverge from actual card width?** Yes, already and independent of this change. Measured live on Shop All: at 1280px, `sizes` (`snippets/util-autofill-img-size-attr.liquid`, called from `card-gallery.liquid`) resolves to `33vw` = 422px against a real 381px card (11% over); at 1600px, `25vw` = 400px against a real 461px card (13% under — the opposite direction). Traced why: that util generates breakpoints assuming viewport ≈ container, with zero awareness that the container hard-caps at `page_width` — architecturally incapable of tracking the real value once the cap engages, regardless of what `product_card_size` is set to. This is a pre-existing Horizon approximation (not something this build introduced), and it settles the "keep sizes in sync" question — there's no existing sync to preserve, so a CSS-only change doesn't create a new inconsistency, just adds a few more percent to one that already exists. Logged separately in `REVIEW-NOTES.md`.
- **Is pure CSS-only, no Liquid touched, feasible?** Yes, with one disclosed cost. The `.product-grid--{{ section.id }}` class is the only DOM hook that distinguishes these two templates' grids from every other product-grid consumer (occasion pages share the identical section type and settings shape — confirmed — so any broader/unscoped rule would incorrectly reach occasion too, which must stay 3-up). No stable semantic alternative exists: checked `layout/theme.liquid` directly for a `template.suffix` or `request.page_type` class on `<body>`/`<main>` — none exists (the theme's only `request.page_type` use anywhere is a one-off `== 'index'` check in `sections/header.liquid`, unrelated). Adding one would mean a one-line edit to `layout/theme.liquid` — a core file Horizon updates often — which is a worse trade than the alternative. So the override in `assets/lambruk-tokens.css` hardcodes the two templates' current, live `section.id` values directly:
  ```css
  .product-grid--template--25877634711853__main:is(.product-grid--grid),
  .product-grid--template--25868165546285__main:is(.product-grid--grid) {
    --product-grid-columns-desktop: repeat(auto-fill, minmax(325px, 1fr)) !important;
  }
  ```
  (`!important` needed — matches Horizon's own rule at identical specificity, same precedent as §43/§45.) First ID is `collection.all.json` (Shop All); second is `collection.json`, the default template, which is why it also covers Tea/Sauces & Chutneys/Pantry Staples with one rule. **Real, disclosed cost:** these are Shopify-generated template-*asset* IDs, not the section's own key — stable under ordinary editing (content, JSON, Admin renames) but tied to the underlying asset record. If either `collection.all.json` or `collection.json` is ever deleted and recreated as a new asset, the ID changes and this rule stops matching, silently — no error, the grid just reverts to whatever `product_card_size` says. Same risk category already rejected once this session for `#shopify-section-{{id}}`-style scoping elsewhere, accepted here only because the alternative (forking `product-grid.liquid`, or a new schema setting making the existing `product_card_size` dropdown silently inert) was judged worse. Logged in `REVIEW-NOTES.md`, titled by symptom ("grid silently reverts to 3-up"), not by cause, so it's findable by whoever hits it without already knowing why.

**Occasion pages: left at 3-up, deliberately.** Not touched by the override above (different `section.id`, and occasion's own template — `collection.occasion.json` — was never in scope). Occasion collections hold 5-6 products; 3-up fills those rows tidily, 4-up would leave an orphan row of one or two. Logged in `REVIEW-NOTES.md` as a decision, not an oversight.

**Two more column thresholds, found by the requested 768px check, not assumed away.** The explicit instruction was to verify 768px "to confirm mobile and tablet are untouched." It wasn't: lowering the minimum shifts *every* auto-fill column threshold, not just the one at the 1440px cap this override was built for — the same `floor((container+gap)/(min+gap))` math produces an earlier breakpoint at every column count, not only the one this change specifically targeted. Confirmed by simulating the old 340px value live against the current DOM (temporary inline-style override, no file changes) at the two affected widths:

| Viewport | Container | Old (340px min) | New — first tried (330px) | 
|---|---|---|---|
| 768px | 688px | 1 column, 688px card | 2 columns, exactly 330.00px — the minimum itself, zero clearance |
| ~1140px | 1060px | 2 columns, 516px card | 3 columns, 334.66px |

Neither is a visual regression (a single 688px-wide card at tablet width was an unreviewed rough edge, not a verified-correct baseline), but 330 landing exactly on the 768px boundary — `(688+28)/(330+28) = 2.000` precisely — meant any small variance (a scrollbar, a fractional viewport, a slightly different margin) could flip it back to 1 column with no warning. Revised to 325 for real clearance at both thresholds (10px and 29px respectively — see the arithmetic table above), re-verified live after the change: 768px → 2 columns/330.00px, 1140px → 3 columns/334.66px, both with margin this time, and all five original desktop checkpoints (1280/1440/1520/1600/1920) unchanged from the 330 arithmetic since none of them sit near either of these two lower thresholds.

**Occasion collections aren't actually on `collection.occasion.json` live yet — a pre-existing gap (§37/§38), not something this change created, but now visible during review.** Checked three of the four Curated Occasion collections (Slow Mornings, Entertaining, Sunday Roast) against their live, unassigned URLs: all three render `.product-grid--template--25868165546285__main` — the same section ID as `collection.json` — not their own template's ID. Confirmed the real `collection.occasion.json` template is correctly excluded by this override (`?view=occasion` on Slow Mornings: distinct ID `template--25893430231341__main`, 3-up, 461px, unaffected) — the mechanism is right for the assigned state. The problem is upstream: same as §37's finding for Shop All, Admin's theme-template picker only offers templates that exist in the *published* theme (Flux), so occasion collections likely can't be assigned `template_suffix: occasion` until Horizon publishes. Until then, previewing an occasion collection in the Horizon review theme shows 4-up, not the 3-up just decided above — because it's silently rendering the default template, which this change now makes behave differently at wide viewports. Not customer-facing (Flux, the live theme, is untouched either way) and self-resolves once template assignment happens post-publish, at which point this override's exclusion is already correct and needs no further change. **Decision (2026-08-31): ship as-is, documented** — rather than fork Liquid to work around a temporary, pre-publish-only state. Logged in `REVIEW-NOTES.md`.

**Verification — rendered card widths and column counts, measured live, not derived, at the final min=325:**

| Viewport | Template | Columns | Card width |
|---|---|---|---|
| 375px | Shop All | 2 (unchanged) | mobile untouched |
| 768px | Shop All | 2 | 330.00px |
| 1140px | Shop All | 3 | 334.66px |
| 1280px | Shop All | 3 | 381.33px |
| 1440px | Shop All | 3 | 434.67px |
| 1520px | Shop All | 4 | 339.00px |
| 1600px | Shop All | 4 | 339.00px |
| 1920px | Shop All | 4 | 339.00px |
| 1600px | Occasion (`?view=occasion`) | 3 (unchanged) | confirms exclusion held |

`shopify theme check --path .` clean.

## 48. Trust-badge row: box removed, tick enlarged and recoloured (2026-08-31)

Homepage `section_proof_bar` ("Low FODMAP", "Gluten-Free", etc, 5 items) uses Horizon's native `check_box` icon (`blocks/icon.liquid` → `snippets/icon.liquid`). First established what the tick actually is, per instruction, before touching anything: one inline `<svg>` (viewBox `0 0 20 20`) with **two `<path>` children** — the square outline drawn first, the checkmark second — both painted the same colour via the icon block's own `icon_color` setting. Not a wrapper border, a font glyph, or a CSS pseudo-element; "larger" is the block's own `width` range setting (14→200px), and "remove the box" means hiding one of the two paths, since Horizon's icon set has no boxless checkmark to swap to instead (checked the full option list).

**Changes:**
- `templates/index.json`: all 5 icon blocks, `width: 14 → 20`, `icon_color: "#BF8C45" → "#131A3E"`.
- `assets/lambruk-tokens.css`: new rule hiding the box path via its own SVG `d` data, not `:first-child` — the homepage also carries three unrelated icons (`map_pin`/`stopwatch`/`chat_bubble` in the Cafe info card) whose own first path is real content, not a box; `chat_bubble` alone is 4 paths (3 dots + outline), and a positional selector would have deleted one of its dots. Matched instead on the box path's distinctive `d`-attribute prefix, scoped to `[data-template="index"]` (the sitewide `<main data-template="{{ template }}">` from `layout/theme.liquid`, not a Shopify-generated section id — more stable than the section-id approach used in §47, since it isn't tied to any template-asset ID at all).

**Scoping verified live, not assumed:** homepage has 8 total icon SVGs; exactly the 5 `check_box` instances had a path hidden, all matching the box's `d` prefix; the other 3 (Cafe info card) had zero hidden paths, full path count intact. Checked Wholesale's own 4 `check_box` icons separately (different page, different colour `#986A34`, unrelated to this row) — `data-template="page.wholesale"`, zero hidden paths, confirming the scope holds.

**Discrepancy flagged, not silently resolved:** the instruction was to match the tick to "the label text (#131A3E)". The label's actual `text_color` in the same JSON blocks is `#4A5478` (a muted blue-grey), not `#131A3E` (navy) — confirmed live, computed colour matches the JSON. Built to the literal hex given (`#131A3E`), since that's an unambiguous instruction; flagging that the label itself isn't currently that colour, in case the mismatch was the actual target rather than the tick.

Verified live at 1280px and 375px (mobile still stacks one-per-line, untouched) and on `/pages/wholesale`. `shopify theme check --path .` clean.
