## 63. Token-driven image and card radius system (2026-09-03)

Followed a full corner-radius audit of the design source (every `.dc.html` in `design/design_handoff_website/`, both breakpoints, plus `design/handoffs/`) and an audit of the build's own existing radius mechanisms. **Horizon has no theme-level setting that reaches a content image or card container anywhere** — the only native radius settings are `product_corner_radius`/`card_corner_radius` (Search → Predictive search only) and `badge_corner_radius` (sale/sold-out pill, not the image). This is therefore a level-2 token system by necessity, not preference, per the customisation hierarchy in `CLAUDE.md`.

**Tokens.** Full ladder added to `assets/lambruk-tokens.css` from `design/design_handoff_website/design-system-tokens/spacing.css:13-14`: `--radius-xs: 4px`, `--radius-sm: 6px`, `--radius-md: 10px`, `--radius-lg: 16px`, `--radius-pill: 999px`. All five defined even though this build only consumes three — see `design/DESIGN-TOKENS.md` §4 for the full record, including the two design-source contradictions (category tiles 16 vs. 10 at the same rendered width; product card 16 desktop vs. 10 mobile) resolved in favour of 16px.

**The hard constraint — radius goes on the image element itself, never on `media-with-content`'s section-level `border_radius`.** That field renders via `border-override`, which does `{% if settings.border_radius > 0 %} overflow: hidden; {% endif %}` on the *section* element — an ancestor of both the media column and the content column. The homepage hero's award badge (`blocks/_lambruk-award-badge.liquid`) deliberately escapes its own content-column ancestor to overhang the image (`right: -16px; bottom: -24px` at desktop, confirmed against the design source's own identical technique — `Desktop.dc.html:56-60` positions the real badge exactly the same way, as a plain sibling `<img>` outside the image's own `overflow:hidden` div). Putting the radius on the section would clip it. Instead, radius was applied to `.media-block` itself (`snippets/media.liquid`) — the image's own crop wrapper, already `overflow: hidden` unconditionally, and a separate DOM branch from the content column the badge lives in. Verified live, both breakpoints: badge overhangs unclipped (1280px: hero right edge 1240px, badge right edge 1256px — the intended 16px overhang, badge bottom 24px past hero bottom, exact; 375px: badge straddles the hero's bottom-right corner per the block's own documented mobile CSS, `top: -66px; right: 12px`).

**Mechanism, two paths:**
- **(a) Components with a real `border_radius` field** (`_collection-card-image.liquid`, `blocks/image.liquid`) — set to the field. Carries the overlay/scrim wiring for free: `snippets/overlay.liquid` reads the same `settings.border_radius` and applies it to its own `--overlay-border-radius`, so the "Explore by category" gradient scrim rounds with its image automatically (verified live: both `.collection-card__image` and its child `.overlay` compute `border-radius: 16px`). **Reported before building, per instruction:** this field cannot be defaulted from the token — Shopify range-setting defaults are static numbers, not CSS custom-property references. Every instance is a literal `16` in the template JSON, and the schema default moved from `0` to `16` for forward safety (new instances stop being silently square). This is a documented cross-check, not a token-linked value — the full list of literal-`16` locations is recorded in `design/DESIGN-TOKENS.md` §4, to be walked manually if the token ever changes.
- **(b) Components with no field** (`.media-block`, `card-gallery.liquid`, `lambruk-occasion-card.liquid`, `lambruk-cafe-gallery.liquid`) — point directly at `var(--radius-lg)`. `card-gallery.liquid:269` previously read `var(--product-corner-radius)` with no fallback — a variable only ever set by `search-modal.liquid`'s own wrapper, so outside the search modal it silently resolved to the CSS initial value (0) on the placeholder-title box. Replaced outright.

**A real orphaned-setting bug found and left alone, distinct from today's fix.** `_product-card-gallery.liquid`'s schema *already* has a `border_radius` field, and `templates/index.json`/`collection.all.json` already carry `border_radius: 16` on every instance — but the block's own Liquid never passes `block_settings` into `card-gallery.liquid`, so that field is a pure orphan, doing nothing by name. It happens to work anyway: `card-gallery.liquid` reads Shopify's implicit `block` global directly (`assign block_settings = block.settings`, no explicit pass-through needed — confirmed live, not assumed from re-reading the render-scoping rules), so `border-override` still receives the real value and `.card-gallery`'s `border-style` class picks up `--border-radius: 16px` correctly. Verified live on Shop All: `.card-gallery` computes `border-radius: 16px; overflow: hidden`, image inside clipped to match. Product cards were already correctly rounded before this session touched anything — flagged, not touched further, since the actual render path works despite the schema field being unused by name.

**Two exclusions that needed an explicit lock, not just an absence of change.** `blocks/image.liquid`'s schema default moving from `0` to `16` would have silently rounded the 404 illustration (`templates/404.json`), which previously had no explicit `border_radius` key and was relying on the old `0` default. Added an explicit `"border_radius": 0` to that one instance before changing the default, so the exclusion is now a stated fact in the JSON, not an accident of what the default happened to be — same principle as the `image_ratio` explicit-value precedent (§55/§56).

**Cart line-item thumbnail — a real, working native setting, not a CSS override.** `settings_schema.json`'s `cart_thumbnail_border_radius` (Cart section) is genuinely consumed by `snippets/cart-products.liquid`, driving the actual cart drawer's line-item thumbnail. Was unset (schema default 0). Set to `6` in `config/settings_data.json`'s `current` block. Verified live: added an item to cart, `.cart-items__media-image` computes `border-radius: 6px`.

**Sitewide sweep beyond the components named in the build spec, found while verifying every JSON instance of the two schema-field block types:** `templates/list-collections.json` (native `/collections` list page's own collection-card, separately from the homepage's) and `templates/article.json` (blog post featured image) both carried `border_radius: 0` on the same two block types — set to `16` for consistency, since both are unambiguously "content image or card container" under the decision's own scope, even though neither was named in the original instruction.

**Two content-image surfaces found still at 0, out of today's scope, named rather than fixed per instruction** ("a surface with no radius is the failure mode here, name it"): the PDP's main product gallery (`.product-media-container`, Horizon's native `product-media.liquid`/`product-information.liquid` — an entirely different mechanism from anything touched today) and the PDP's sticky-add-to-cart thumbnail (`.sticky-add-to-cart__image-img`). Also noted: the design's PDP "From the same shelf" related-products rail isn't built on this theme's actual product page yet, so there was nothing to check there.

**Verified live, both breakpoints (1280px/375px unless noted), computed style plus screenshots, not source-reading:** homepage (hero + badge + Explore-by-category + Curated Occasions + Top Sellers rail + two-up cards, all 16px, badge unclipped both widths), Shop All/`collections/all?view=all` (24 product cards, all 16px), Cafe (`?view=cafe`, hero still 0px/full-bleed, 3 media-blocks + 8 gallery tiles + 3 "Bring home" category tiles all 16px), Our Story (`/pages/lambruk-pantry-about-us?view=our-story` — the real page handle, not `/pages/our-story`; both the native `story_image` block and the `media_with_content_meet_paige` block confirmed 16px), Wholesale (`?view=wholesale`, traceable-to-the-grower image 16px), a PDP (`/products/citrus-tea-12-tea-bags`), and 404 (illustration confirmed still 0px, both computed style and screenshot). `shopify theme check --path .` clean (383 files) both before and after. No `shopify theme dev` process running. Pull-check-push sequence followed for all 12 touched files — pull reverted the not-yet-pushed commit to stale remote as expected, restored via `git checkout HEAD --`, pushed.

**Cafe gallery mosaic — visual judgement call, not just a number swap.** Changed from a hardcoded 10px to `var(--radius-lg)` (16px) per instruction, screenshotted both the tight 3-column grid (16px gap) and the mixed span-2/stacked layout. Reads clean, not fussy — the 16px radius against the section's own 16px grid gap is a balanced ratio, no corner ever looks like it's fighting its neighbour. Caveat: the gallery still shows Horizon's placeholder illustrations (no real photos uploaded yet, a pre-existing open item, unrelated to this change) — worth a second look once real photography replaces the placeholders, since radius reads differently against a busy photo than a flat illustration.

**Buttons and inputs deliberately left untouched.** The decision statement assigns buttons/inputs to `--radius-sm` (6px), but `button_border_radius_secondary` is currently `8`, not `6`, and neither it nor `inputs_border_radius` was named in the build's `MECHANISM`/`REPLACE`/`VERIFY` sections — only images, cards, and the cart thumbnail were. Changing sitewide button/input radius is a separate, highly visible change with its own blast radius; flagged rather than actioned silently.

## 64. Closing the radius system — third tier, PDP main image, buttons/inputs (2026-09-03)

Follow-up to §63, covering everything that pass flagged rather than fixed: the PDP's own main image, the design's deliberate main/thumbnail radius split, the sticky add-to-cart thumbnail, and the buttons/inputs gap.

**PDP main image — a real gap in Horizon's own mechanism, not a hardcoded 0.** Traced every consumer of `_product-media-gallery`'s `media_radius` setting through `snippets/product-media-gallery-content.liquid`: it drives the zoom dialog's own thumbnail rail and the `::view-transition-group` zoom-morph shape — nothing applies it, or any radius, to `.product-media-container` itself, the element actually visible on the page. Confirmed live before touching anything: `border-radius: 0px` regardless of the setting's value. Fixed the way `.media-block` was fixed in §63 — `snippets/product-media-container-styles.liquid:9` already carries `overflow: hidden` unconditionally on `:where(:not(.dialog-zoomed-gallery)) > .product-media-container`, the same "just needs the radius line added" shape. Added `border-radius: var(--radius-lg)` to that exact existing selector, nothing new. Its own `:not(.dialog-zoomed-gallery)` guard means the full-screen zoom view is automatically excluded — confirmed live, unchanged at 0px inside the dialog, both before and after.

**Shared-selector consequence, checked not assumed.** `product-media-container-styles.liquid` is also rendered by `card-gallery.liquid` for product cards (Shop All, Top Sellers rail) — the same class, same file. Product cards' inner `.product-media-container` is now *also* 16px, doubly-clipped inside their own outer `.card-gallery` (already 16px via its own `border_radius` setting, §63). Confirmed live the two rects are pixel-identical (same x/y/width/height, zero gap between the two clipped boxes), so the double-rounding is exactly coincident — invisible, not a regression. Screenshotted Shop All to confirm no visible artefact.

**No small corner icon to clip against.** Checked what overlays the main image before adding the radius: `.product-media-container__zoom-button` (`product-media-gallery-content-styles.liquid:80`) is `position:absolute; width:100%; height:100%`, fully transparent — a full-bleed invisible click target, not a positioned corner icon. Nothing to look awkward against a newly-rounded corner.

**`media_presentation` correction — my own prior claim about the thumbnail rail was wrong, caught before building further on it.** §63 (and the follow-up report before this build) stated the PDP thumbnail rail was live and working, based on `thumbnail_position: "right"` being set in the JSON. Re-checked live before setting `thumbnail_radius`: this PDP's actual `media_presentation` is `"grid"` (2-column), `slideshow_controls_style: "counter"`, `slideshow_mobile_controls_style: "dots"` — none of which is `"thumbnails"`, the one value `thumbnail_radius`'s own `visible_if` requires to render anything at all. Confirmed live: no `.slideshow-control` element exists anywhere on the page in its current grid presentation, only `.slideshow-controls__dots` (mobile). `thumbnail_radius` genuinely has zero live consumers today — it's not broken, it's inactive, because thumbnails-mode isn't the mode in use. Set to `10` anyway per instruction, correctly, for whenever the presentation is switched to slideshow+thumbnails — but the value change has no visible effect on this PDP right now, and that's worth knowing before anyone goes looking for it on the live page.

**`media_radius` does have a live consumer today: the zoom dialog.** Unlike `thumbnail_radius`, `media_radius` drives `.dialog-thumbnails-list__thumbnail` and the view-transition morph shape regardless of on-page presentation mode — confirmed by actually opening the zoom dialog (`.product-media-container__zoom-button`, click) both before (0px thumbnails) and after (10px thumbnails) the change, screenshotted both states.

**Zoom-morph animation quality — reasoned, not watched, and said so.** The transition's own clip shape (`::view-transition-group`'s `border-radius: var(--gallery-media-border-radius)`) is now 10px throughout the ~380-500ms open/close animation, sitting between the 16px on-page source and the 0px full-screen destination. Attempted to capture a mid-transition frame via rapid sequential screenshots (real click, not programmatic `.close()`, since the latter likely bypasses whatever triggers the actual View Transition) — every screenshot call's own round-trip latency exceeded the animation duration, landing on the settled end-state every time, not a genuine mid-motion frame. Reported the mechanism and a reasoned judgement (a static clip value between the two endpoints is a plausible middle ground for a sub-500ms transition, not the kind of discontinuity that usually reads as a glitch) rather than asserting it was visually confirmed — flagged in `design/DESIGN-TOKENS.md` §4 for Jake to eyeball directly, since this is exactly the kind of motion call a real click settles faster than more agent inference would.

**Sticky add-to-cart thumbnail — smaller than assumed, and already wired to something, just not the right something.** §63's prior report claimed this was "still 0px" — wrong, caught before repeating the mistake: `.sticky-add-to-cart__image` (the wrapper the visible `<img>` sits inside) already had `overflow: hidden; border-radius: var(--style-border-radius-buttons-primary)` — riding on the *primary button's* radius, not left unstyled. The bare `<img>` itself has no radius of its own (doesn't need one, clipped by its parent), which is what the earlier check actually measured. Confirmed rendered size live before changing anything: 52px, smaller than the 64px cart-drawer thumbnail this was meant to match — cleared the size check. Re-wired to `var(--radius-sm)` directly: same rendered pixel value once the button-radius change below lands (both compute to 6px), but no longer incidentally piggybacking on an unrelated variable — the same single-source reasoning as the two forms below.

**Buttons and inputs — three theme settings, all genuinely consumed, none silently discarded.** `button_border_radius_primary`/`_secondary`: 8 → 6. `inputs_border_radius`: 4 → 6. Confirmed live before and after on the homepage hero's "Shop Now" (primary) and "Visit Our Cafe" (secondary) — both computed exactly `6px` after the change, screenshotted before and after at the same framing. Also checked, per instruction, every native input/textarea/select sitewide before assuming any of them needed the same fix: the sort dropdown (`<select>`, Shop All) and the cart discount-code input both ride the same `inputs_border_radius` setting and both moved from 4px to 6px correctly, confirmed live.

**The two hardcoded forms — same rendered pixel, different wiring.** `lambruk-contact-form.liquid` (both its own contact fields, line 328, and the Contact page's embedded "Wholesale Details" sub-form reusing the `.wholesale-enquiry-form__input` class, line 428) and `lambruk-wholesale-enquiry-form.liquid` (line 250) all hardcoded `border-radius: 6px` as a literal, disconnected from any setting — coincidentally already matching the design, not because they were linked to it. Pointed all three at `var(--style-border-radius-inputs)` — Horizon's own emitted `:root` custom property, not this project's `--radius-sm` token directly, per instruction: `inputs_border_radius` should be the single source for every input on the site, custom-built or native, and a parallel token would just be a second source pointed at the same number. Confirmed the variable is genuinely `:root`-scoped and reachable from these block-level stylesheets (already proven live earlier in the session), and confirmed rendered value is unchanged — 6px before, 6px after, on both the Contact page's own fields, its embedded wholesale sub-form, and the standalone Wholesale page's form.

**`_product-card-gallery`'s orphaned `border_radius` field — left alone, on purpose.** Reconsidered whether "the same treatment" (explicit wiring) applied here too. It doesn't, for a specific reason: the field already renders correctly today (confirmed live in §63) because `card-gallery.liquid` reads Shopify's implicit `block` global directly, the exact same unremarkable pattern every private block-content partial in this theme uses to get at its own settings (`snippets/media.liquid` included) — none of them explicitly pass `block: block` through their `render` tag, because Shopify's block-rendering context makes `block` ambiently available without it. Adding an explicit `block: block` parameter to `_product-card-gallery.liquid`'s render call would be pure redundancy — it wouldn't change what `card-gallery.liquid` receives, since it already receives it, and it would single out this one component as needing an explicit pass-through that nothing else in the theme uses. Nothing to wire that isn't already wired.

**Verified live, both breakpoints, computed style plus screenshots:** a PDP (`/products/citrus-tea-12-tea-bags` — main image 16px on 4 non-dialog `.product-media-container` instances, 0px confirmed unchanged on the 2 inside `.dialog-zoomed-gallery`; sticky thumbnail 6px; zoom dialog opened and closed, thumbnails 10px), Shop All (product cards unaffected by the shared-selector change, screenshotted), Contact page (`?view=contact`, both its own fields and the embedded wholesale sub-form, 6px, screenshot), Wholesale page (`?view=wholesale`, 6px), the cart drawer (line-item thumbnail unchanged at 6px via its own separate setting, discount input 6px, checkout button 6px, screenshot), and the homepage hero (primary + secondary buttons, before/after screenshots at identical framing, both 6px after). `shopify theme check --path .` clean (383 files) before and after. No `shopify theme dev` process running. Pull-check-push sequence followed for all 6 touched files.

## 65. PDP gallery, Stages 1 and 2 — carousel presentation and a fixed 4/3 cropped slot (2026-09-03)

Two-stage follow-up to the corner-radius work (§63/§64), prompted by a report that the PDP's crop looked wrong on the beetroot relish photos. Investigation (report-only pass, before either stage) found the actual problem was presentation, not crop: `_product-media-gallery`'s `media_presentation` was `"grid"` (all images shown side by side, all forced square via `aspect_ratio: "adapt"`'s own tallest-image lock), not the design's single-image-with-thumbnail-rail carousel (`Desktop.dc.html:392-397`, `productImageStyle` at line 1442 — a ~574px main image over a ~181px 3-up thumbnail grid; `Mobile.dc.html:316` has no thumbnail rail at all, bare image only).

**Stage 1 — presentation only, `templates/product.json`:** `media_presentation: "grid"` → `"carousel"`, `thumbnail_position: "right"` → `"bottom"`. Also changed, not originally named but required: `slideshow_controls_style: "counter"` → `"thumbnails"` — `thumbnail_position`/`thumbnail_width` are gated by `visible_if: slideshow_controls_style == 'thumbnails'` in the schema, so without this the rail stays invisible regardless of its own settings. `thumbnail_width` set to `72`, the schema's max (`min:44, max:72`) — the design's ~181px is structurally unreachable through this field; logged as a knowing divergence in `REVIEW-NOTES.md` rather than chased with a level-4 markup rebuild of Horizon's own scrolling thumbnail-row component. `slideshow_mobile_controls_style` left at `"dots"` — a fully independent setting, confirmed live to already produce the design's mobile behaviour (no rail, dots only) with zero extra work.

**`aspect_ratio: "adapt"` mechanism, traced before Stage 2 touched anything:** with `adapt`, Liquid computes `lowest_aspect_ratio_index` across the *whole* media set (`product-media-gallery-content.liquid:162-176`) and marks that one image `--tallest` — the container locks to that single ratio for the entire gallery, not per-image and not first-image. Confirmed live: `beetroot-relish-100g` (landscape genuinely first, square second) and `citrus-tea-12-tea-bags` (square-only) both render their main slot at an identical 776×776 — the square ratio wins in both, since square is "taller" than the 1.498 landscape. Clicked through slides on the mixed product and measured before/after: **zero height change**, confirmed live rather than assumed from source — the whole point of the `--tallest` mechanism is to prevent exactly the resize-under-cursor problem that would have blocked Stage 2.

**Stage 2 — fixed 4/3 slot with `object-fit: cover`, scoped to the PDP main slot only.** Two things checked live before building, per instruction:
1. **Is `object-fit` shared with the zoom dialog or product cards?** The base rule (`product-media-container-styles.liquid:55-58`, `.product-media-container img { object-fit: contain }`) is unscoped and genuinely shared. But the correct lever isn't that rule — it's the native `media_fit` block setting (already in `_product-media-gallery.liquid`'s schema, `contain`/`cover`), which Horizon has *already* pre-guarded: the zoom dialog's own slide-render path explicitly strips the `media-fit-cover` class (`product-media-gallery-content.liquid:571`, `{{ product_media_container_class | remove: 'media-fit-cover' }}`) regardless of the main gallery's setting. Set `media_fit: "contain"` → `"cover"` on the PDP's own block instance; confirmed live afterward that all three dialog images (including the portrait outlier) still compute `object-fit: contain`. Product cards use a different block type (`_product-card-gallery`, no `media_fit` field) and a different custom element (`<media-gallery>` wraps only the PDP's own gallery; product cards use a plain `.card-gallery` div) — unreachable by construction, confirmed live (`closest('media-gallery')` false on a Shop All card). Quick-add was also checked empirically (clicking `_lambruk-add-button` on Shop All never opens a gallery-bearing modal in this build, despite `quick-add-modal.liquid` existing in the theme) — a third potential leak point that turned out not to be reachable either.
2. **Does `--tallest` fight a fixed `aspect-ratio`?** No, and it's reversible. The container's `aspect-ratio` is set once, in the stylesheet, reading `var(--gallery-aspect-ratio, var(--media-preview-ratio))`. A new rule in `assets/lambruk-tokens.css` sets `aspect-ratio: 4 / 3` directly on the same element at higher specificity, which wins regardless of what `--media-preview-ratio` resolves to — the Liquid `--tallest` computation still runs (harmless, just unconsumed for sizing here). Deleting the CSS rule fully restores `adapt` behaviour; nothing else needs unwinding.

Selector: `[data-template="product"] media-gallery :where(:not(.dialog-zoomed-gallery)) > .product-media-container { aspect-ratio: 4 / 3; }` — the `:where(:not(.dialog-zoomed-gallery)) >` guard copied verbatim from Horizon's own base rule, `media-gallery` for the extra defence-in-depth against a future related-products rail on the same page (none exists today, confirmed), `[data-template="product"]` matching the existing project convention (`[data-template="index"]`, established earlier in this file) for a level-2 override with nothing route-agnostic to lean on.

**Verified live, five products (the square+1.498 majority shape, the milder 1.340 group, all-square, all-non-square, and the portrait outlier), both breakpoints, screenshots — a photographic judgement call, not a measurement pass:**
- `beetroot-relish-100g`, `green-tea-5-tea-bags`, `citrus-tea-12-tea-bags`, `pear-and-blackberry-sauce-240ml` — clean crops at both 1280px and 375px. Looked specifically for lid/base clipping on the all-square product (the group that newly gets cropped, 27 of 52 products) — none found, comfortable margins both edges, both of its two images, both breakpoints.
- `beetroot-relish-260g` — **confirmed bad, as predicted.** Its one portrait photo (3159×5616) crops to roughly its middle 42% height at 4:3 — jar lid cut off at top, bowl of beetroot cut off at bottom. Confirmed at both breakpoints, not fixed with `object-position` or a per-product exception per instruction — logged in `REVIEW-NOTES.md` as a photography problem for Jake to decide (re-crop the source, drop it from the gallery, or accept it), not a theme bug. The zoom dialog remains the customer's escape hatch to see the whole photo regardless of what's decided.

Also logged in `REVIEW-NOTES.md`, unrelated to this crop work but found while measuring it: the PDP media column renders at 776px against the design's ~574px (`1.1fr:1fr`, 64px gap) — an open layout divergence, not diagnosed, flagged for whoever picks up general PDP layout work next since it changes the crop math too.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean (383 files) both stages. No `shopify theme dev` process running. Pull-check-push sequence followed for both stages' touched files (`templates/product.json` alone for Stage 1; `templates/product.json` + `assets/lambruk-tokens.css` for Stage 2).

## 66. Cafe hours: two-tier schedule, closure notice, both wired to `lambruk_cafe_hours`/new `lambruk_cafe_closure` (2026-09-03)

`settings.lambruk_cafe_hours` already existed from an earlier build pass (see the phone/hours settings entry, this file, and `REVIEW-NOTES.md`'s existing `lambruk_cafe_phone` row for the sibling setting's own unset-default caveat) and was already the single source at all three locations that show cafe hours — a fresh sweep (`grep -rn "8:30"` across every `templates/*.json`/`sections/*.json`) found **zero hardcoded literal hours text anywhere**, only the schema's own default value. So this task's mechanism work was smaller than framed: nothing to retrofit, only content, a schema-capacity change, and a new field.

**Design format, checked before adopting anything:** `Desktop.dc.html:121,592,654` and the `Mobile.dc.html` equivalents all read `8:30 AM – 5:00 PM` — colon-minute always shown (`4:00 PM`, not `4 PM`), full-caps `AM`/`PM`, a spaced en dash (`&ndash;` on mobile, the literal character on desktop — both genuine en dashes). The design has no day-range or closure precedent at all — every instance is one flat range covering every day. Extended the same time-format convention to the new two-tier schedule and applied the same spaced-en-dash convention to the day ranges for one consistent punctuation rule across both range types in the string. Day names spelled out in full (`Monday – Tuesday`, not `Mon–Tue`) — the one adjacent precedent in the design source (the Shipping policy's "Friday, Saturday, Sunday and Public Holidays") spells days out, so matched that rather than inventing an abbreviation convention with no backing anywhere in the source.

**Schema change, reported before building per instruction:** `lambruk_cafe_hours` was a single-line `text` setting — Shopify's `text` type is a plain `<input>`, can't hold a real line break. Changed to `textarea`, each of the three consumers now pipes the value through `| newline_to_br` inside its existing single `<p>` tag rather than restructuring the block. New `lambruk_cafe_closure` (`text`, no `default` key at all — not an empty-string default, genuinely absent, which is what makes it render nothing when blank) added to the same `t:names.lambruk_store_info` settings group, with an `info` string on both fields (line-per-range on hours, blank-to-hide on closure) so the theme editor explains the format Admin can't infer from a bare textarea.

**Icon-row alignment, measured not eyeballed, both breakpoints.** The homepage cafe-intro's hours row (`templates/index.json`) is the only one of the three with an icon (`content_direction: row`, `vertical_alignment: center`) — the other two are plain stacked `<p>` blocks with no icon to misalign. Confirmed live at 1280px and 375px: row and icon vertical centers match to the pixel in both cases (1280px: row centre 461.84px = icon centre 461.84px; 375px: 417.83px = 417.83px) — flexbox `align-items: center` re-centres against the now-taller 3-line block automatically, no CSS change needed. Screenshot at 375px looks slightly off at a glance (the icon reads visually closer to the boundary between the two hours lines than the true centre) — the measurement is what settled it, not the impression.

**Verified live, both breakpoints, all three locations named:** homepage cafe-intro icon row, Cafe page "Reserve your table" booking panel, Cafe page "Pull Up a Chair" meta line — correct text, both hours lines present, closure line present (muted caption, colour matched to each location's own existing secondary-text colour: `#8A8FA3` where the Cafe booking panel's own `walkins_note` already used it, `#686D84` matching Pull Up a Chair's own `meta_walkins`, `#8A8FA3` reused on the homepage where no local muted-caption precedent existed). `shopify theme check` clean. No dev server running. Pull-check-push followed for all 5 touched files (`config/settings_schema.json`, `config/settings_data.json`, `locales/en.default.schema.json`, `templates/index.json`, `templates/page.cafe.json`).

**LocalBusiness structured data — checked, doesn't exist, reported not built (explicitly out of scope for this task).** Grepped the whole theme for `LocalBusiness`/`openingHours`/`structured_data` — the only matches are Shopify's native `| structured_data` filter applied to `product`/`article` objects (`sections/product-information.liquid`, `featured-product.liquid`, `featured-product-information.liquid`, `main-blog-post.liquid`), unrelated to the cafe. Confirmed live on both the homepage and the Cafe page: exactly one JSON-LD block on each, `@type: "Organization"` (Shopify's own automatic sitewide markup — `name`/`logo`/`url` only), no `LocalBusiness`, no `openingHours`, on either page. So there is no eighth silently-stale location today — but there's also nothing feeding Google's local panel from this site. Logged in `REVIEW-NOTES.md` as a build decision for Jake, not actioned.

## 67. Cafe hero CTAs unified on the design's own mobile pairing — desktop/mobile fork retired (2026-09-03)

**Supersedes the "two near-identical cream buttons" note in §46 (line 1298) and the "Exception, left untouched on purpose" note + table row in §61 (lines 1877, 1887).** Both described the same pre-existing state: "Book a table" solid cream (`#F7F5F2`/`#131A3E`/`#F7F5F2`), "View menu" a *different*, near-identical cream on desktop (`#FBFAF7`) that switched to solid navy (`#131A3E`/`#F7F5F2`/`#131A3E`) only below 750px via `mobile_custom_button_*` overrides. Jake's instruction, after seeing the standard secondary measured at 1.19–1.32:1 over this hero (report-only task, same day): adopt the design's own mobile pairing everywhere, retiring the fork rather than keep patching the desktop cream-on-cream state.

**Report before building, per instruction — which value is base and which is override.** Read `blocks/_lambruk-view-menu-button.liquid`'s schema directly: `custom_button_background`/`_text`/`_border` (the desktop values) each carry a `"default"` key; `mobile_custom_button_background`/`_text`/`_border` carry no `"default"` key at all, and only take effect inside a guarded `@media (max-width: 749px)` block when explicitly set on the instance. **Desktop is the base, mobile is the override** — so unifying on the mobile treatment meant changing the three base fields to the mobile values and deleting the three `mobile_custom_button_*` keys, not the reverse.

**Build:** `templates/page.cafe.json`, `cafe_hero.button_row.view_menu` block — `custom_button_background`/`_text`/`_border` changed to `#131A3E`/`#F7F5F2`/`#131A3E` (the exact former mobile values); the three `mobile_custom_button_*` keys deleted outright, no `@media` override left to carry. `book_table` untouched. Net diff: 1 file, 3 insertions, 6 deletions — simpler than before, as expected; no breakpoint fork left to reason about.

**Why the design's own desktop treatment was knowingly not followed:** the standard `button-secondary` style (transparent fill, `#A07037` border per §61) was measured against this hero's sampled backdrop at 1.19–1.32:1 — far under the 3:1 UI-component minimum, unusable over any photograph. The design's desktop pairing (two near-identical creams) doesn't fail contrast but fails *legibility of hierarchy* — the two buttons are visually interchangeable, which is why §46 flagged it as "the opposite of what 'secondary should look understated' would suggest." The design's own mobile pairing (cream + solid navy) solves both problems at once — real hierarchy, no contrast risk on the button *fills themselves* (both opaque) — and is already a real, shipped part of the design (`Mobile.dc.html:443-444`), not an invented third value. Applying it everywhere trades one attested design state for another, rather than inventing a new one.

**CHECK: hover states, both buttons, live, real mouse hover (not assumed from source).** `resolve-custom-hover.liquid`'s auto-shift needs no manual config for either button. "Book a table": background shifts `#F7F5F2` → `rgb(237, 233, 226)` on hover, text/edge (box-shadow) stay navy. "View menu": background stays navy, edge (box-shadow) shifts from navy-on-navy (seamless) to cream `#F7F5F2` on hover — the hover state is where "View menu" gets a visible outline for the first time, a mouse-only affordance distinct from the keyboard focus ring below.

**CHECK: keyboard focus ring, both buttons, both breakpoints — verified via genuine Tab-key navigation, not `.focus()`.** A first attempt called `element.focus()` via script and read `outline-style: none` / `:focus-visible` false — invalid, since programmatic focus doesn't reliably trigger `:focus-visible` in Chromium absent recent keyboard input. Redone by tabbing from the page's own skip link with the real keyboard: both buttons report `:focus-visible: true`, `outline-style: solid`, `1.5px` width, `2.5px` offset, at both 1280px and 375px. Per `snippets/button-custom-styles.liquid`, an opaque background makes `effective_focus_outline_color` equal the button's own background — "Book a table" therefore rings in its own cream (`#F7F5F2`), "View menu" rings in its own navy (`#131A3E`). Both are genuinely visible: the ring sits *outside* the button (2.5px offset) against the hero backdrop, not against the button's own fill, so same-colour-as-fill doesn't mean invisible — it means the ring's contrast depends entirely on what's directly behind the button's edge, checked next.

**CHECK: outline-vs-backdrop contrast — sampled from the actual placeholder illustration, both buttons, four points around each button's edge.** Same SVG-fill-plus-gradient-overlay compositing technique as the earlier report task, refined this time to exclude shapes living inside `<defs>`/`<clipPath>`/`<mask>` (an initial pass wrongly hit an invisible white clip-path shape and returned a falsely light backdrop). With that fixed, both buttons sit over the illustration's teal water band, not the pale sky/moon above them: "View menu" navy ring vs. composited backdrop (`~rgb(77–82, 139–149, 141–149)`) = **4.33–4.89:1** across the four sampled edges. "Book a table" cream ring vs. the same backdrop = **3.16–3.82:1**. Both clear the WCAG 1.4.11 3:1 non-text/UI-component minimum, with the cream ring's margin noticeably thinner than the navy ring's, since the backdrop's mid-tone luminance sits closer to cream than to navy.

**This measurement is provisional and will not survive real cafe photography.** The composited teal-water backdrop is Horizon's placeholder illustration, not a photo — the numbers above describe today's placeholder, not the final page. **"View menu" (the navy button) is the one to re-check once real photography lands**, since a dark ring on a dark/busy photo backdrop is the pairing most likely to lose its edge; "Book a table"'s cream ring has more headroom by comparison but should be re-checked at the same time, not assumed safe by omission.

**CHECK: visually-hidden "Opens in a new window." span.** Confirmed present and unchanged in the rendered accessible text (`"View menu\n     Opens in a new window."`) — untouched by this task, since only colour settings were edited, not the block's own markup.

**CHECK: text-on-fill contrast, both buttons, both opaque.** 15.50:1 both directions (navy-on-cream, cream-on-navy) — comfortable pass, unaffected by any of the placeholder-backdrop uncertainty above since both fills are fully opaque.

**Verified live, both breakpoints, screenshots taken.** 1280px and 375px both show the intended pairing — cream "Book a table" first, solid navy "View menu" second, full-width stacked at 375px matching the design's own mobile layout exactly. `shopify theme check --path .` clean. Pull-check-push followed (`templates/page.cafe.json` only); the pull reverted this session's own not-yet-pushed commit as expected, restored via `git checkout HEAD --`, pushed. No dev server running.

## 69. Cafe "Bring the Lambruk experience home": square tiles, radius 16, CTA spacing, divider (2026-09-03)

Four fixes from a prior report-only audit of this module, all traced to the same root cause pattern (unset defaults silently winning) plus one CSS-shape mismatch.

**1. Image ratio.** `templates/page.cafe.json`'s `cafe_shop_pantry > static-collection-card` had no `collection-card-image` block instance at all — `blocks/_collection-card.liquid`'s unconditional `content_for 'block', type: '_collection-card-image', id: 'collection-card-image', …` call rendered purely on schema defaults, landing on `image_ratio: "portrait"` (4/5) instead of the design's `aspect-ratio:1/1` (`Desktop.dc.html:701` etc.). Added the instance with `"static": true` (matching the homepage's own `section_explore_by_category` instance of the same block, and required — Theme Check flags the block as not in `_collection-card.liquid`'s accepted-blocks list without it, since static content_for targets are exempt from that check, ordinary child blocks aren't) and `image_ratio: "square"` set explicitly. Verified live: 394.66×394.66px (1280px), 343×343px (375px), both `1 / 1`.

**2. Radius.** Card wrapper's `border_radius` 10 → 16, matching the site's three-tier radius system (content images are 16 — `--radius-lg`, `assets/lambruk-tokens.css` §63/§64) rather than the design's stale 10, the same resolution already applied to the other two design-source contrast/contradiction cases on this build. The 10 had been inert on this instance regardless (placement is `below_image`, no visible border drawn on the card wrapper itself) — removed as a stale value rather than left to confuse the next person reading the JSON. The image's own `border_radius` set to 16 explicitly on the new block instance (not left to its own schema default, even though the default happens to also be 16 — naming it removes any doubt this is deliberate). Verified live: card `--border-radius: 16px`, image wrapper `border-radius: 16px`, both breakpoints.

**3. CTA spacing — two edits, the second correcting the first.** `browse_shop` wrapped in a new `cta_group` (type `group`, `padding-block-start: 24`), the same mechanism as High Tea/Pull Up a Chair/Catering's own `cta_group` blocks elsewhere on this page — not a one-off margin. First push measured **32px** above the button, not 24: the `header` group wrapping eyebrow/heading/body/cta_group has its own `gap: 8`, which stacks with a child's own top padding (flex `gap` adds space between generated boxes in addition to, not instead of, a child's own padding) — 24 + 8 = 32. **This means the padding-block-start values reported for the three reference instances in the original audit (20/24/24) were never a 1:1 stand-in for their own rendered gap either** — High Tea's own `cta_group` (padding-block-start: 20) sits inside a parent with `gap: 24`, rendering 44px, not 20; the earlier report captured the JSON setting, not the measured total. Corrected `cta_group`'s `padding-block-start` to 16 (16 + 8 = 24) so the *rendered* result matches the design's own 24px and the stated verification target, rather than matching the other three modules' raw padding number, which was never the actual point — the shared mechanism (a dedicated `cta_group` wrapper) is what's consistent across the page, not a single portable pixel value, since each instance's parent contributes a different amount.

Section-level `gap` (header block → grid) set explicitly to 32 (was unset, falling back to the `collection-list` schema default of 12 — confirmed via `sections/collection-list.liquid`). Verified live: 24px above / 32px below at 1280px, exact. At 375px: 24px above (unaffected — this stacking relationship isn't width-dependent) / **24px below**, not 32 — this is Horizon's own native fluid gap-scaling (`assets/base.css`'s `.gap-style`, `--gap-scale` switches from `--spacing-scale-md` to `--spacing-scale-default` at the 990px breakpoint), the same mechanism that fluid-scales this section's own `padding-block-start/end` (88px → 61.6px at mobile, confirmed elsewhere this session) — expected, sitewide, not specific to this edit, not a defect.

**4. Divider.** The `row` block's `"border": "none"` is correct and untouched — a group block's native border setting (`blocks/group.liquid`, consumed via `assets/base.css`'s `.border-style` class: `border-width`/`border-style`/`border-color`/`border-radius`, no per-side property) applies to all four sides at once, the wrong shape for the design's `border-bottom: 1px solid #E3D6C5`-only hairline (`Desktop.dc.html:701/704/708`, `Mobile.dc.html` equivalent). A scoped rule added to `assets/lambruk-tokens.css` instead: `[id$="__cafe_shop_pantry"] .collection-card__content .group-block { border-bottom: 1px solid #E3D6C5; }` — the section-key-suffix scoping convention already used elsewhere in that file, since `.group-block`/`.collection-card__content` are both generic classes reused sitewide. `#E3D6C5` verbatim, no contrast substitution — decorative hairline, not a UI component, WCAG 1.4.11 doesn't apply. Verified live: `1px solid rgb(227, 214, 197)` under all three labels, both breakpoints.

**Crop check, per instruction — especially the tea pyramid.** All three source photos are landscape, confirmed identical natural ratio `422×281` (1.502:1) via `img.naturalWidth`/`naturalHeight` live (Shopify's CDN width param resizes without cropping, so this is each master's true ratio). Landscape source into a square (narrower) target means `object-fit: cover` matches the box to the source's *height* and crops width symmetrically left/right — the full vertical extent of every source photo is preserved, nothing trimmed top or bottom. Confirmed visually at both breakpoints: the tea tin pyramid renders complete, clear headroom above the topmost tin (French Earl Grey), no clipping. The "may lose its top tin" risk would only apply to a portrait-source-into-square-or-wider-box crop, which this isn't.

**Horizontal scrollbar, reported in Jake's screenshot — investigated, not reproduced or fixed here.** `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` vs `window.innerWidth`: no overflow found at 375, 768, or 1280px, before or after this fix. Ruled out a hidden carousel: `sections/collection-list.liquid`'s `carousel_on_mobile` schema default is `false` and Cafe's JSON never sets it, and `snippets/resource-list.liquid`'s mobile-carousel branch (`{% if settings.layout_type != 'carousel' and settings.carousel_on_mobile %}`) doesn't render when that's false — confirmed no duplicate carousel markup exists for this instance, live. Most likely explanation, not confirmed live in this tool: `assets/base.css:325`'s `--full-page-margin-inline-offset: calc(((100vw - var(--full-page-grid-central-column-width)) / 2) * -1)` — Horizon's own full-bleed-section mechanism (the Cafe hero is `section_width: "full-width"`), which is a classic `100vw`-includes-scrollbar-gutter overflow source on any browser/OS that reserves layout space for a vertical scrollbar. This environment's browser pane appears not to reserve that space (no overflow reproduced at any width tested), so this couldn't be confirmed directly — but it would explain a scrollbar appearing regardless of scroll position (dockable at the window's own bottom edge, not tied to any specific module) and would be sitewide, not something introduced by or fixable within this module. Not actioned here; worth confirming on Jake's own browser if he wants it chased further.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout (two pushes — the second correcting item 3's spacing). Pull-check-push followed for both; each pull reverted the not-yet-pushed commit as expected, restored via `git checkout HEAD --`, pushed. No dev server running.

**Correction, 2026-09-03 — the scrollbar paragraph above named the wrong mechanism.** A follow-up investigation (same day, §70) found `assets/base.css:325`'s `100vw` calc is consumed *only* by `blocks/_marquee.liquid` — confirmed the sole reference sitewide — and no marquee exists anywhere on the rendered Cafe page. Horizon's actual general-purpose full-width-section mechanism (`.section--full-width`, what the Cafe hero's `section_width: "full-width"` actually uses) is CSS Grid with `width: 100%` and `minmax(var(--page-margin), 1fr)` margin columns — no `vw` unit anywhere, immune to the scrollbar-gutter bug by construction. The "most likely explanation" above doesn't hold up; see §70 for the corrected investigation, which also could not identify a real source with the tools available.

## 70. Cafe CTA spacing normalised to 28px rendered — three modules, plus the mechanism that cost two rounds to find (2026-09-03)

**The fix, by rendered result, not setting.** High Tea, Pull Up a Chair and Catering's `cta_group` block each had a different `padding-block-start` (20/24/24) sitting inside a parent group with its own `gap: 24` — none of which are the same number as what actually renders, because the two stack. Changed all three to `padding-block-start: 4`, so each renders **28px** — matching High Tea and Pull Up a Chair's own design source (`Desktop.dc.html:635`/`652`, both specify 28px there) and giving Catering, which has no design source of its own (grepped both design files for "Catering"/"Lambruk on your table" — zero matches, this module was built without one), the same number as its siblings rather than inventing a fourth value. Cafe hero (32, matches design), Reserve panel (22, matches design — a text-block mechanism, not a `cta_group`, deliberately untouched) and Bring the Lambruk experience home (24, matches design, §69) were already correct and left alone.

**Verified live by `getBoundingClientRect`, both breakpoints, all six CTAs on the page — not by reading settings:**

| CTA | 1280px | 375px |
|---|---|---|
| Cafe hero | 32 | 22.4 |
| Reserve panel | 22 | 22 |
| High Tea | 28 | 28 |
| Pull Up a Chair | 28 | 28 |
| Catering | 28 | 28 |
| Bring the Lambruk experience home | 24 | 24 |

**The anticipated mobile drift didn't happen — better than planned, not a surprise to chase.** Going in, the expectation (padding 4 + a parent `gap: 24` assumed to floor toward 20 on mobile like padding does) was 24px at 375px, a 4px undershoot against the design's own 20 there, accepted as not worth restructuring three parent gaps that also govern eyebrow/heading/body spacing in the same column. Live measurement shows 28 at *both* breakpoints, no drift at all: a `group` block's own flex `gap` setting does not fluid-scale the way `padding-block-start` does (below) — it's a flat literal at every viewport. The acceptance called for in the brief turned out to be unnecessary; recorded here so the next person doesn't go looking for a drift that isn't there.

**Why a JSON padding value is never the rendered gap — the mechanism, so this doesn't cost a third round.** `snippets/spacing-style.liquid`:
```liquid
if value > scale_min   " scale_min = 20
  echo 'max(' scale_min 'px, calc(var(--spacing-scale) * ' value 'px))'
else
  echo value 'px'
endif
```
Any `padding-block-start`/`-end`/`-inline-start`/`-inline-end` value **greater than 20** renders as `max(20px, spacing-scale × value)` — `--spacing-scale` is `1.0` above 990px, `0.7` below, so anything above 20 gets fluid-scaled toward a hard floor of exactly 20px on mobile. Any value **at or under 20** renders as a flat literal, unscaled, at every breakpoint. This is why High Tea's old 20 never moved, why Pull Up a Chair/Catering's old 24 used to float down to 20 on mobile, and why all three now sitting at the literal-mode value of 4 don't move either. **This threshold is specific to `padding-block-*`/`padding-inline-*` via this one snippet** — confirmed separately that a `group` block's own flex `gap` setting does not route through it and never fluid-scales (see above). Two different spacing mechanisms share the word "gap"/"padding" in the settings UI; only one of them is fluid.

**And the padding always stacks with the parent's own gap — it is never the whole story.** A `cta_group`'s `padding-block-start` adds space *inside* its own box; the parent flex container's `gap` adds space *between* it and its previous sibling. Both apply simultaneously. The rendered distance from the last line of body copy to a button is always `parent gap + cta_group padding`, never one or the other. This is the exact thing that cost two rounds here: the first attempt at this normalisation (§69) set Bring-the-Lambruk's `cta_group` padding to the design's literal 24 and it rendered 32, because the header group's own `gap: 8` was added on top and nobody had checked the rect first. See the house rule below — this is now a standing check, not a one-off lesson.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean. Pull-check-push followed; the pull reverted the not-yet-pushed commit as expected, restored via `git checkout HEAD --`, pushed. No dev server running.

## 71. Mobile page margins: gutter to 20px, collection grid, Cafe hero text (2026-09-04)

One token change and two independent bugs it exposed, following a report-only sweep of all eight page types at 375px.

**1. Gutter: `--page-margin` 16px → 20px below 750px.** `assets/lambruk-tokens.css`, scoped `@media (max-width: 749px)`, same selector list as the rule it overrides (`assets/base.css:269/277`) — base.css itself untouched, level 2. Design specifies 20px in 73 places across every page type in `Mobile.dc.html`; Horizon's native 16px had no design basis, and the earlier "accept Horizon's 16px" concession (`DESIGN-TOKENS.md`, approved 2026-08-19) is reversed here — see that file's corrected entry.

**Checked before building, per instruction: does anything hardcode 16 expecting it to match the gutter?** One real hit — `snippets/spacing-padding-surface.liquid` (this project's own fluid-padding snippet for the "surfaces" pattern, quote-panel's only consumer), whose own doc comment said its 16px inline floor "matches Horizon's own --page-margin concession." Left alone, quote-panel's inline padding would have floored at the old value while everything else moved to the new one — a new, self-inflicted 4px mismatch. Changed to 20 in the same commit; doc comment corrected to point at the new override instead of the reversed concession. Nothing else found on a broader grep for 16px tied to margin/gutter/inset — the rest were unrelated (button padding, icon sizes, popover offsets).

**2. Collection grid, filter row, item count — two separate sibling bugs, not one shared container, confirmed only by measuring after the first fix, not by trusting the plan.**

`full_width_on_mobile` set `true` → `false` on all three collection templates that use `sections/main-collection.liquid` (`collection.all.json`, `collection.occasion.json`, `collection.json` — the bare default; the brief named one, all three carried the identical setting from the same origin, so all three were fixed together, flagged here rather than assumed). Horizon's own schema default is `true`, never chosen.

**Checked before building: does `--margin-lg` match `--page-margin`?** No — `--margin-lg` (`theme-styles-variables.liquid:404`) is a flat `1rem`/16px with no responsive component at all, confirmed live (`getComputedStyle`, both properties read at once: `--margin-lg: 1rem`, root font-size 16px, `--page-margin: 20px`). `full_width_on_mobile: false` alone would have made `snippets/product-grid.liquid` emit `--grid--margin--mobile: 0 var(--margin-lg)` — insetting the grid 4px less than the heading above it and everything else on the newly-20px page, a smaller version of the bug it was meant to fix. Repointed at `--page-margin` directly via a scoped `!important` override on `#ResultsList.main-collection-grid` (`!important` needed because the value it replaces is itself an inline style, not a stylesheet rule).

**Verified live rather than assumed fixed: this alone did not "bring the filter row, item count and grid in together."** Product cards measured correctly inset (left 20, right 355) after the `#ResultsList` fix — but the "Filter" button and the category chips still measured flush (left 0, and 13 for the Filter icon specifically) on the same page load. Traced why: `#ResultsList` (the grid), `.facets-toggle` (Filter button + view-density toggle) and `.lambruk-tag-filter` (this project's own chip/count block, `blocks/lambruk-tag-filter.liquid`) are three **siblings** inside `.collection-wrapper`, not nested inside each other — fixing one sibling's own margin mechanism does nothing for the other two. Neither `.facets-toggle` (stock Horizon) nor `.lambruk-tag-filter` (this project's block) had ever carried any horizontal padding of its own; both simply assumed a margin-respecting grid column that `.product-grid-container`'s own `display: block` below 750px (`snippets/product-grid.liquid` — deliberate, the same reason `#ResultsList` needs its own inline padding rather than relying on the section grid) never gives them. Fixed with a second, plain `padding-inline: var(--page-margin)` rule on both selectors, no `!important` needed (nothing else sets it).

**3. Cafe hero text.** `sections/hero.liquid:506` ties the content wrapper's class directly to the section's own `section_width` setting — only two values exist, `page-width` or `full-width` (`:1093-1104`), no third "full-bleed background, margined text" option. `.hero__content-wrapper.page-width` gets `grid-column: 2/3` (`:586`, the margin-respecting column); plain `.hero__content-wrapper` — what `full-width` emits, correctly, since the photo has to bleed — gets none. Choosing full-width for the photo takes the text down with it by construction. Confirmed sitewide (grep across every `templates/*.json`) that Cafe is currently the only `"type": "hero"` instance — scoped the fix to the section's own emitted class combination (`.hero__content-wrapper.full-width`) rather than this instance's id, so it reaches any future full-width hero the same way, per instruction. `padding: 0 var(--page-margin) 32px`, mobile only — matches `Mobile.dc.html:439`'s `padding:0 20px 32px` exactly once the gutter token itself is 20. Desktop untouched: not reported broken there, and the design's own 20/32 values are mobile-specific numbers, not ones that also happen to be correct above 750px.

**Verified live, `getBoundingClientRect`, all eight pages, 375px, after both fixes:**

| Page | Flush elements found | All on the established deliberate list? |
|---|---|---|
| Homepage | header underlay, cart/menu hitbox, trust marquee, 2× quote-panel band, Top Sellers carousel | Yes |
| `/collections/all` | header underlay, cart/menu hitbox | Yes — filter row, chips, item count, grid all confirmed inset (20/355) |
| PDP | header underlay, cart/menu hitbox, gallery, recommendations band | Yes |
| Cafe | header underlay, cart/menu hitbox, hero (photo only — text confirmed inset 20/355) | Yes |
| Our Story | header underlay, cart/menu hitbox | Yes |
| Wholesale | header underlay, cart/menu hitbox | Yes |
| Contact | header underlay, cart/menu hitbox | Yes |
| 404 | header underlay, cart/menu hitbox | Yes |

No new element appeared outside the established list on any page. Two things checked and ruled out as false alarms rather than reported as regressions: a per-card image slideshow's second (off-screen) slide, `overflow-x: scroll` by design, momentarily flagged as "content bleeding" inside the fixed grid — it isn't page content, it's an unopened carousel slide; and a pre-existing `margin-left: -3px` on `.facets-toggle__wrapper` (stock Horizon, optical icon alignment, present before and unrelated to this change) that puts the Filter icon 3px inside the new 20px line — not a page-margin defect, the padding itself measured exactly 20px.

Extended beyond the two named collection templates to all three sharing the identical setting (`full_width_on_mobile`), and to any future full-width hero (class-scoped, not id-scoped) — both flagged here rather than done silently.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean throughout (two pushes — the second adding the filter-row/chip-row fix once verification showed the first push incomplete). Pull-check-push followed for both; each pull reverted the not-yet-pushed commit as expected, restored via `git checkout HEAD --`, pushed. No dev server running.

## 72. Product card title/price: step down to design values at mobile (2026-09-04)

Title 24px→16px (line-height 1.35) and price 16px→14px below 750px, across all three collection templates (`collection.all`, `collection.occasion`, and the bare default `collection`). Desktop untouched. Occasion's title was also raised 1.375rem→1.5rem so all three templates share the same *desktop* value before the mobile override applies uniformly on top — it had drifted to its own third number, matching neither sibling nor the design.

**Revised same day, same decision, not a new one.** The design's own literal mobile values (14px/13px) were built first and shipped — then read too small once actually rendered: Horizon's card is ~161px wide at 375px, not the design's own ~168px, and text merely adequate at the design's width was cramped at this one. Raised to 16px/14px; line-height stays 1.35.

**Report-before-build confirmed the design steps down, not flat.** Mobile (`Mobile.dc.html:283-284`, raw markup): title `font-size:14px;line-height:1.35`, price `font-size:13px`. Desktop (`Desktop.dc.html:334`, the `ProductCard` design-system component, `ProductCard.jsx:23-26` + `tokens/typography.css:14,19`): title `var(--display-5-size)` = 24px/1.25, price `var(--body-size)` = 16px/1.6. A genuine mobile-specific pair, not the desktop numbers reused.

**Mechanism decision — the crux, resolved before writing any CSS.** Option (a), a `clamp()`/`var()` inside the JSON `font_size` setting, was checked against `snippets/typography-style.liquid` and rejected. That file parses `font_size` as a bare rem number (`settings.font_size | split: 'rem' | first | times: 1.0`, lines 26/40) with no passthrough for an expression — unlike `line_height`, which has an explicit, documented `contains 'var('` branch a few lines below, added to this same file earlier in the project for exactly this problem. Traced what a `clamp(...)` string actually does through that parser: the non-numeric `split` result coerces to `0` via Liquid's `times`, which happens to still land on the flat/echo branch (`0 < 3.0`) and print the font-size string verbatim — so the *font-size* might come out looking right by accident — but the same `0` also drives the `type` classification two lines up (display/heading/body), silently misrouting `--letter-spacing`/`--line-height` token lookups to the wrong family regardless of the real intent. Relying on an accidental pass-through with a real, silent side effect elsewhere is bug class #5 (`var()` coerced to 0) wearing a disguise, not a clean second option. The schema settles it independently: `font_size` on both `product-title.liquid` and `price.liquid` is a closed `select` of literal rem strings, never designed to hold an expression at all.

**Built as (b): a scoped, commented CSS override**, `assets/lambruk-tokens.css`, `@media (max-width: 749px)`. Selector distinguishes title from price structurally rather than by block id — the three templates don't even share one (`collection.all`/`collection` both key the title block `product_title_4nY4eT`, occasion keys it plain `product_title`) — using the fact that Horizon renders `product-title` as a plain `div.text-block` and `price` as the `<product-price class="text-block">` custom element; confirmed live these are the *only* two `.text-block` elements inside a `.product-card`, so the tag distinction is clean with no risk of catching something else. Overrides `--font-size`/`--line-height` (the custom properties `typography-style.liquid` sets inline, consumed by `assets/base.css`'s `.custom-font-size`/`.custom-typography p` rules) rather than the final `font-size` property directly — same reasoning as the collection-grid margin override in §71, and `!important` is required for the same reason: beating an inline style needs it. Recorded at the override site itself, in full, per instruction — this is a knowing JSON-value-≠-rendered-value split, not a missed one.

**Verified live, `getComputedStyle`, all three collection templates, 375px, both passes (14px/13px built first, 16px/14px after the revision):**

| Template | `data-template` | Title | Price | Worst-case line count |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Shop All | `collection.all` | 16px / 21.6px lh | 14px | 3 lines ("Blueberry & Apple Sauce 100ml") |
| Slow Mornings (occasion) | `collection.occasion` | 16px / 21.6px lh | 14px | 2 lines ("Pear & Blackberry Sauce 240ml" — page's own longest; the citywide worst case isn't in this collection) |
| Tea Collection (bare default) | `collection` | 16px / 21.6px lh | 14px | 3 lines ("Lemon Ginger Tea 35g Loose Leaf") |

**Breakpoint boundary checked both sides, no jump:** 749px → 16px/14px (mobile); 750px → 24px/16px (desktop) — a clean snap at exactly the rule's own `max-width`, nothing in between.

**Separate finding, named per instruction, not fixed this pass.** `templates/search.json` and `templates/product.json`'s recommendation-row title/price carry `type_preset: "h6"`/`"rte"`/`"paragraph"` (not `"custom"`) — their `font_size` settings (`1rem` on all of them) are already inert, the real bug-class-#3 shape, just on different pages than this one. Confirmed what they actually render as: search results — title **14px** (Horizon's own `rte` default), price **12px** (`h6` default). PDP recommendations — fetched and measured the section's own markup directly (`data-url`, since the block never loaded live in this tool, presumably an intersection-observer lazy-fetch this environment doesn't reliably trigger — see Browser tooling notes) — identical: title **14px**, price **12px**, same mechanism, same fallback classes. The design source doesn't cover either page at all (grepped both `.dc.html` files for "isSearch"/"search results"/"recommendation" — zero matches) — no explicit target exists, but the obvious inference is the shop-grid's own values, since it's the same `_product-card` block reused, just mis-configured. Left alone, logged in `REVIEW-NOTES.md` so the dead settings don't sit unnoticed.

`shopify theme check --path .` clean. Pull-check-push followed; the pull reverted the not-yet-pushed commit as expected, restored via `git checkout HEAD --`, pushed. No dev server running.

**Addendum, same day — title-to-price gap raised 6px → 12px, same component, same override family, same three templates.** Reported first, then built: measured the rendered gap between title bottom and the price/Add row (by rect, both breakpoints) and found it identical everywhere — **6px** on sold-out cards and Add-button cards alike, at both 375px and 1280px. The gap itself had never changed; what changed was what occupied the row below it. Traced to `price_row`'s own `padding-block-start: 6` (the `_product-card-group` block wrapping price + Add button) — the parent flex column has `gap: 0`, and the title's own `padding-block-end` is `0`, so this one setting is the entire mechanism, the same "one block's own padding, not a group gap" shape the card uses everywhere (image→title is built the same way, via the title's own `padding-block-start: 20`). 6px was sized for a line of price text; `_lambruk-add-button` (this project's own block, no settings of its own) renders a real 36px-tall control into that same slot, so a gap that read fine next to text read cramped next to a button — same 6px number both times, confirmed live, not a regression that crept in.

Raised to **12px**, uniformly — not scoped to `:has(.lambruk-add-button)` cards only, deliberately: a split value would leave adjacent cards in the same grid row with their price rows sitting at different heights depending on stock status, which is worse than a sold-out card gaining 6px of harmless extra space. Not raised to 20 (the card's own image→title value) — that would make the title bind to its own price as loosely as it binds to the photo above it, collapsing a hierarchy the card already expresses on purpose. 12 sits on the card's existing 6/12/20 rhythm and keeps title→price visibly tighter than image→title.

Verified live, `getComputedStyle`, both card types, both breakpoints: **12px** on a sold-out card and an Add-button card, at 375px and at 1280px — four confirmations, one number. `shopify theme check --path .` clean. Pull-check-push followed; pull reverted the not-yet-pushed commit as expected, restored via `git checkout HEAD --`, pushed. No dev server running.

**Second addendum, same day — bottom-align the price/Add row across a grid row, same component, same override family, same three templates.** Bottom-alignment alone doesn't line prices up; two parts were needed. (1) `margin-top: auto` on the price row (`.product-card__content` is already `display:flex; flex-direction:column; gap:0`, no existing auto-margin to conflict with). (2) `min-height: 68px` on the price row, matching what an Add-button row already renders at (`box-sizing: border-box`: 36px button + 12px + 20px padding, not the task's literal "36px" — that number is the button's own content height, not the row's border-box height). Both report-first checks came back non-issues on the *first* one (card genuinely stretches to its grid cell; `.product-card__content`'s rendered height already equals the full stretched card height) but the *second* — "does `vertical_alignment: center` mean min-height alone can't align the text" — was answered wrong at the reasoning stage: reported as a non-issue on the theory that equal row height + equal row bottom implies equal row top, so centring inside two now-identical boxes lands text identically either way. **Wrong, caught only at verification.** The JSON setting `vertical_alignment: "center"` doesn't win live — a different, pre-existing, already-documented override in `blocks/_product-card-group.liquid` (`.product-card .group-block-content.layout-panel-flex--row { align-items: baseline }`, built 2026-08-31 for the homepage Top Sellers rail, scoped too broadly and silently reaching this grid too) sets the real computed `align-items` to `baseline`. Previously this cost an inert ~1.2px, explicitly flagged in that file's own comment as a latent risk ("a copy or font change could open the gap with no warning"); this change was that event, widening it to a visible ~7.6px mismatch on a sold-out/Add-button pair, confirmed live before touching anything further. A second, compounding issue: `.group-block` (the price row's outer box) is plain `display: block`, so `min-height` alone doesn't make a shorter child (price-only content, ~19.6px) stretch to fill it — the child just sits at the content-box's own top. Fixed with two rules, scoped to the same three templates only (confirmed live `templates/index.json`'s Top Sellers rail is the only other `_product-card-group` consumer, and is untouched): `display: flex` on the outer `.group-block` (so its one child stretches via flexbox's own default `align-items: stretch`), and `align-items: flex-end` overriding `baseline` on the inner row, so price/Add button sit at the row's bottom instead of wherever their baselines put them. Tested by temporary live style injection before writing the permanent CSS.

Consequence, not a regression: the 12px title→price gap (previous addendum) becomes a floor, not a fixed value — a short single-line title on a sold-out card can now sit further than 12px above its price, since the row's own min-height sets the row's top position. Intended; the alternative is prices at different heights across a grid row, the actual complaint.

Verified by rect, both required row pairs, both breakpoints: a 1-line/2-line title pair and a sold-out/Add-button pair, each confirmed to have matching price-top coordinates. At 375px both pairs occur naturally on Shop All ("Citrus Tea 12 Tea Bags"/"Citrus Tea 35g Loose Leaf", both Add, price tops matched; "Citrus Tea 5 Tea Bags" sold-out/"Citrus Tea 60g Loose Leaf" Add, price tops matched) — screenshotted together in one frame. At 1280px (3-column grid) every naturally-loaded title fit on one line, so the 1-line/2-line case was verified by temporarily lengthening one product's title client-side to force a wrap, confirming its price row still matched its two 1-line row-siblings (screenshotted, then reverted — no persisted change); the sold-out/Add-button pair occurred naturally in the same "Citrus Tea" row and was screenshotted directly. `shopify theme check --path .` clean. Pull-check-push followed; pull reverted the not-yet-pushed commit as expected, restored via `git checkout HEAD --`, pushed. No dev server running.

## 73. Homepage hero: mobile top padding reduced 50.4px → 32px (2026-09-04)

Reported before building: at 375px, the gap between the sticky header's bottom edge (`y=60`) and the hero image's top edge was **50px**, all of it coming from one source — `section_hero`'s own `padding-block-start: 72` ([templates/index.json:219](templates/index.json:219)), scaled by Horizon's native responsive-spacing utility ([snippets/spacing-style.liquid](snippets/spacing-style.liquid:22-27): `max(20px, calc(var(--spacing-scale) * value))`) at `--spacing-scale-md: 0.7` below 990px ([snippets/theme-styles-variables.liquid:434](snippets/theme-styles-variables.liquid:434)). `72 × 0.7 = 50.4`. No wrapper margin, no other contributor — confirmed live via `getComputedStyle` on every element between the header and the `<img>`.

Desktop's 72px matches the design exactly (`Desktop.dc.html:45`, `padding:72px 0 80px`) — not touched. The design's own mobile hero doesn't give a like-for-like number: `Mobile.dc.html:60-74` puts the image *last*, after the text and button stack, with the section's own 32px top padding landing on the eyebrow text, not the image (image separated from the button row by its own `margin-top:28px` instead). The built page orders image-first on mobile regardless — Horizon's `media-with-content` section stacks in block order (`media` before `content` in the JSON) at narrow widths independent of the `media_position` setting, which only reorders columns at the desktop breakpoint. 32px was carried over from the design's own top-padding number since there's no better source, with this order difference named rather than silently assumed away.

The 72px JSON value is `section_hero`'s own, independent setting — not shared with any other homepage section (every section on the page carries its own distinct `padding-block-start`: 16, 32, 56, 72, 80, 88, 96 all appear once). Reducing it doesn't move anything else on the page; what *is* shared is the scaling mechanism itself (`--spacing-scale`, the `max(20px, …)` clamp), used by effectively every section and many blocks sitewide — not touched here.

**Built as a scoped CSS override**, `assets/lambruk-tokens.css`, `@media (max-width: 749px)`, per instruction: JSON stays at 72 throughout. Overrides the `--padding-block-start` custom property (the one `spacing-style.liquid` sets inline via `style=""`), not the final `padding-block-start` property — same reasoning as §72's font-size override, and `!important` is required for the same reason: beating an inline value needs it. Scoped to `[id$="__section_hero"] > .section` — a direct-child combinator, deliberately *not* `.spacing-style` generally, since that class is reused by every block inside this section (eyebrow, heading, body, button row) for their own padding via the same utility; confirmed live the shopify-section wrapper's direct children are a `<style>` tag, the section-background div, and this one `.section` div, so the selector can't reach anything else. Recorded at the override site in full — same JSON-value-≠-rendered-value split as §72, same reason: `templates/index.json` reads 72 at every width; the number actually on screen below 750px comes from this file.

**750–989px deliberately left untouched, on Horizon's own scaled 50.4px** — not extended to match, because that band exists independent of this fix: Horizon's own `--spacing-scale` switches at 990px, while this project's other mobile overrides use 750px, so a genuine middle band sits between the two thresholds regardless of what this rule does. Result is three deliberate steps, not a cliff-then-jump: 32px (<750px) → 50.4px (750–989px, Horizon's own scale) → 72px (≥990px, the JSON value, unscaled).

Verified live by rect at all four boundaries: **32px** at 375px, **32px** at 749px, **50.4px** at exactly 750px, **72px** at 1280px — the middle-band snap confirmed to the decimal. `shopify theme check --path .` clean. Pull-check-push followed; the post-push pull matched exactly, no revert needed this time. No dev server running.

## Summary

| Area | Path taken |
|---|---|
| Global fg/bg | Native `page_text_color`/`page_background_color` |
| Heading colour | One CSS override rule |
| Muted text | Explicit custom property, not Horizon's opacity fade |
| Hairlines | `--color-border` override + activating the dormant product-card border |
| Surfaces (paper-2, navy, etc.) | Native `background_color` (+ `text_color`/`accent_color`) per block, `contrast-override`-driven — demonstrated on one block above |
| Breakpoint, mobile gutter, Display 5 | Accepted Horizon's native values (§3) |
| Motion | Separate tokens, scoped to our components only |
| Container, shadows, section rhythm, scrims, icon accent | New layered stylesheet, additive only |

Next step, on approval: build the one worked block from §2 for real and confirm it renders as expected in the theme editor before extending the pattern to the rest of the surface list.
